<!-- lecture 1 -->

*A'udhu billahi minash shaytanir rajim*. (I seek refuge in Allah from the accursed Satan.)
Please recite *Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim*. (In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful.)
*Wa bihi nasta'in, sallallahu 'ala rasulihi Sayyidina Muhammadin wa 'ala aali Sayyidina Muhammadin wa barik wa sallim*. (And in Him we seek help. May God's blessings, peace, and benedictions be upon His messenger, our master Muhammad, and upon the family of our master Muhammad.)

This is a book whose name is *Kunooz al-Hikmah al-madhkurah li-arbab al-'ismah* — The Stored Treasures of Wisdom for the Masters of Infallibility. These are the treasures of wisdom that have been preserved for *arbab al-'ismah*, the masters of infallibility. Who are the *arbab al-'ismah*? They are those who possess *'ismah*, infallibility. In one sense, this points to the Ahl al-Bayt (*'alayhimus salam*) and their progeny, but that is not the intended meaning here. The Ahl al-Bayt and the *A'immah Tahirin*, the Pure Imams, are the masters of *kulli 'ismah*, total infallibility — it is impossible for them to err, and they are free from all sin. The *du'at* (*'alayhimus salam*), by contrast, are the masters of *juz'i 'ismah*, partial infallibility. This distinction has been clarified in a *risalah*, a treatise, by Sayyidna Ali ibn Hussain al-Jamari, who explained that the *A'immah Tahirin* are the masters of *kulli 'ismah*, while the *du'at* (*'alayhimus salam*) — especially the *qa'imi du'at*, the standing *du'at* — are masters of *juz'i 'ismah*. It is for these *arbab al-'ismah*, masters of *juz'i 'ismah*, who are the *du'at*, that these matters have been stored, so that they may derive new understanding from them.

This book was written by Sayyidna Ali ibn Hibatullah, who is known as al-Shamsi. He holds a great station. Sayyidna Hibatullah Sahib — that is, Sayyidna Hibatullah ibn Ibrahim — had several children, and he performed *nass*, designation, upon his eldest son. That eldest child was Sayyidna Ali ibn 'Ibad, and his full lineage is Sayyidna Ali ibn Hibatullah ibn Ibrahim ibn Muhammad — the *shajarah*, or family tree, continues without interruption all the way back to Adam (*'alayhis salam*). Sayyidna Ali ibn 'Ibad, however, passed away during his father's lifetime, and so he became a *ghayr-munfarid Da'i*, a non-independent *Da'i*. Sayyidna Hibatullah Sahib then performed *nass* upon another son, who became eighth in the order of succession. When Sayyidna Ali ibn 'Ibad passed away, he was eighth in that order. Sayyidna Ali ibn 'Ibad had himself performed *nass* on yet another son, Sayyidna Isma'il, who was tenth in the order of succession. It is said that Sayyidna Isma'il ibn Hibatullah possessed the most excellent outward form, *surat*, in the community, *jama'at*, of the *wulat* (*'alayhimus salam*). Another child of Sayyidna Hibatullah Sahib was Sayyidna Hasan ibn 'Ibad, who likewise became a *Da'i* of great station. And yet another was Sayyidna Ja'far, who was the seventy-fourth *Da'i*. He did not himself reach the rank of *Da'i* in succession, yet he holds a great station, and he composed *qasa'id*, odes, among other works.

The book *al-Kawakib al-Durriyah*, which is itself a work of great station, opens with a *qasidah* by Sayyidna Ja'far ibn Hibatullah.

As for Sayyidna Ali ibn Hibatullah, he wrote only one *risalah* — only one book — and no others. He wrote it during his father's lifetime. His station among the *du'at* is nonetheless exceedingly high.

*Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim*. (In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful.) Above it is written the *Ism al-A'zam*, the Greatest Name. In the *risalah* by Sayyidna al-Wajih — that is, *'Abd al-Ali ibn Hasan al-Wajih* — he wrote of great wonders and secrets, and the entire *risalah* addresses the question of whether the *Ism al-A'zam* is greater or whether the *nur*, the light, of the *Bismillah* is greater. He engaged in a lengthy discussion and concluded that the *nur* of the *Ism al-A'zam* is more excellent than that of *Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim*. This is why, when we write the *Bismillah*, we write the *Ism al-A'zam* above it.

The script in which it appears is *khatt al-Teseri*, the Teseri script. In this script, the first dot is a *khatam*, a seal — that is, a ring — and this ring alludes to the *Ahl al-Sharif*, the noble family. Then the three marks above it carry their own significance, and there are also verses, *ash'ar*, by Amir al-Mu'minin on the *Ism al-A'zam*. The *madd*, the elongation mark above it, refers to the *mu'awanah*, the assistance, received from the *Hudud 'Aliyah*, the higher ranks of the hierarchy; the *madd* points toward that. The precise details are not at hand at this moment, but God willing, if the occasion arises, they will be brought forward. This is a separate matter from the present text.

*Wal-hamdulillahi rabbil-'alamin*. (And all praise is for Allah, Lord of the worlds.)

*Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi rabbil-'alamin. Wal-'aqibatu lil-muttaqin. Was-salatu was-salamu 'ala Sayyidina Muhammadin wa alihi ajma'in*. (In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful. All praise is for Allah, Lord of the worlds. And the best outcome is for the righteous. And blessings and peace be upon our master Muhammad and all his family.)

*Al-hamdu lillahil-'azim jalla nasutun khas min riyadh ahlil-ikhtisas*. (All praise is for Allah, the Great, who manifested a special *nasut* from the gardens of the people of specialty.)

All praise and laudation are for Allah, that Exalted Being who created the special *nasut* from the *riyadh*, the gardens, of the people of specialty — the *ahl al-ikhtisas*. The varieties of *nasut* are several: there is *nasut adna*, the lowest *nasut*; *nasut awsat*, the middle *nasut*; and *nasut khas*, the special *nasut*. The *nasut tabi'i*, or natural *nasut*, which is the lowest, is also called *nasut tanasuli*, the procreative *nasut*. After it comes the *nasut dhilli*, the shadow *nasut*. And after that comes the *nasut khas*. The *ahl al-ikhtisas* are the *hudud al-da'wah*, the ranks of the *da'wah*, in the *jaza'ir*, the regions, together with the *mu'minin*, the believers, and the *hadarat*, the dignitaries, among them. The *nasut khas* is fashioned from them.

*Wa ja'ala al-nasut al-dani min riyadh al-madunun fil-maqam*. (And He made the lower *nasut* from the gardens of those who are lesser in rank.)

The *nasut dani*, the lower *nasut*, is made from the *riyadh* of those who are more distant, lesser in rank. This refers to the *nasut dhilli* or the *nasut tabi'i*.

*Ahmaduhu hamda man abda al-ma'na al-zahir*. (I praise Him with the praise of one who has manifested the apparent meaning.)

I praise Him with such praise — the worship of one whose meaning is luminous. *Wa 'arafa haqqa al-ism al-zahir*. (And he knew the reality of the apparent name.) He knew the reality of this apparent name. That is, the author has grasped both the outward, *zahiri*, reality of the name and its inner, *batini*, meaning.

*Wa ashhadu an la ilaha illa alladhi tasawa fil-'ajzi 'an idraki huwiyyatihi al-muta'aliyah awwalu mawjudin wa akhiruhu min bariyyatihi*. (And I bear witness that there is no god but He, in the inability to comprehend whose exalted essence the first of existing things and the last of His creation are equal.)

I bear witness that there is no deity worthy of worship but He — that the first existent thing and the last existent thing in His creation are equal in their inability to comprehend His exalted essence. The first existent thing is the First Intellect, *'aql-e-awwal*. And the last existent thing, from among His creation, is the believer — the believer who has entered the *da'irah-e-iman*, the circle of faith. Both of them are equal in their inability to have gnosis of God. The First Intellect has not attained greater gnosis of God, and this last existent thing has not attained lesser gnosis of the First Intellect — rather, such gnosis is simply not possible for either.

The word "thing," *shay'*, is significant here. A "thing" is that which possesses real existence, as in *inna Allaha 'ala kulli shay'in qadir* — Indeed, Allah has power over all things. Here "thing" refers to the believer, whose value and existence are real. *La shay'*, "no-thing," by contrast, is that which is a mirage — it appears to be water, but when you reach it, it is not there. It has no real value. This usage appears in three places in the Holy Quran: *inna Allaha 'ala kulli shay'in qadir* — Allah has power over all things, meaning especially over every believer; *inna Allaha bikulli shay'in 'alim* — Indeed, Allah is knowledgeable of all things; and *inna Allaha bikulli shay'in muhit* — Indeed, Allah encompasses all things. These three expressions refer, in their inner meaning, to the believer — and ultimately to the Imam of the time.

This state of equality in inability — shared by the First Intellect and the last existent thing — calls to mind what is said about Sayyidna Ali ibn Sulayman. When believers would come to meet him, he would rise to receive them. And similar conduct has been observed among the *du'at*, who would remain standing while exchanging the handshake of *juma'*, Friday, and other occasions. When asked why he would not be seated, he replied: "I remain standing because I stand to shake a believer's hand, for he is my companion in the *Ahl al-Sharif*." In the *tawzi'*, the distribution, of the *Ahl al-Sharif*, the believer takes his share just as the Imam takes his share. In the *Ahl al-Sharif*, the believer is his companion, and so he honors him by rising.

Then consider what Sayyidna Hussain ibn Isma'il — may his holy secret be sanctified — wrote about Sayyidna Abdullah ibn Muhammad and Sayyidna Ahmad al-Jamari. He used the expression *afdal al-mawjudat*, the most excellent of existing things, in reference to Sayyidna Abdullah ibn Muhammad. People present began to wonder at the *Da'i*'s words. But *afdal al-mawjudat* here means he who is present in the *da'wah* — the believers — and among the believers, he is the most excellent. That was the point being made.

*Wa ashhadu anna Muhammadan 'abduhu wa rasuluh*. (And I bear witness that Muhammad is His slave and His messenger.)

And I bear witness that Muhammad is His slave and His messenger. *Al-murtaqi min nasut al-khas ila jawar dhi al-'izzah wal-jabarut*. (The one who ascended from the special *nasut* to the proximity of the Possessor of Might and Power.) He ascended from the special *nasut* to the proximity of the Possessor of Might and Power — meaning that in the *Mi'raj*, the Ascension, the Prophet traveled through the special *nasut*, which was fashioned from the *riyadh* of the *ahl al-ikhtisas*, the people of specialty: the *hudud* of the *da'wah*, the believers, the *hadarat* and the *hudud* of the regions. From those *riyadh* the *nasut khas* was made, and through it the Prophet ascended and was elevated to the proximity of the Possessor of Might and Power. *Jabarut* derives from *jabr* and denotes the all-powerful, the compellingly mighty.

*Sallallahu 'alayhi wa 'ala akhihi*. (May God's blessings be upon him and upon his brother.) *Al-mustakhlaf 'ala al-ummah al-Muhammadiyyah talbisan li-sur al-radiyyah*. (The appointed successor over the Muhammadan community, clothing himself in lowly forms.) He is the one who was appointed successor over the Muhammadan community. Before the Prophet's passing — at Ghadir Khumm — he declared: *man kuntu mawlahu fa-hadha 'Aliyyun mawlahu* — Of whomsoever I am master, this Ali is his master. From that moment, the Prophet lived the remainder of his life in the *hijab*, the veil, of Ali, and Ali lived within the *hijab* of the Prophet. This is what the text alludes to. It is said that Sayyidna Isma'il ibn Ibrahim has stated — though without full elaboration — that the *awsiya*, the successors, pass away before the *natiq*, the speaker-prophet.

The remaining life of Mawla Ali was, in essence, the life of the Prophet. He was within the Prophet's *hijab*. As declared: *lahmi lahmuka wa dami damuka* — My flesh is your flesh and my blood is your blood. And this proceeds from *ruh wahidah*, a single soul. When the Prophet's body was present, Ali was within his *hijab*; and when the realities were made manifest, Ali was the one in whom they appeared.

In the Battle of Jamal, Mawla Ali said to Lady A'isha words to the effect of divorce. The Prophet had granted Ali the right to do so after his passing — "I give Ali the right that after my passing, he may divorce you." The proof of Ali's status as the Prophet's very self comes from the Holy Quran: *ta'alaw abna'ana wa abna'akum wa nisa'ana wa nisa'akum wa anfusana wa anfusakum* — Come, let us call our sons and your sons, our women and your women, our selves and your selves. When "our sons" was called, Hasan and Hussain were brought. And when *anfusana* — "our selves" — was called, Ali was brought. He thus became the *nafs*, the self, of the Prophet.

*Al-muhtajib bihi al-'ashir bil-amr al-nahir*. (The Tenth who is veiled by him in the manifest affair.)

He was veiled — *al-'ashir*, the Tenth. What is meant by *al-'ashir* here? The one who occupied the rank of *al-'ashir* became veiled, and that rank passed onward. The first *al-'ashir* did not remain; he changed into another rank. So who became *al-'ashir*? The one who, in the subsequent series, within its sphere, occupied that station — the lofty intellect within its sphere. And from the *sahib al-qawr*, the master of the cycle, the *juz'-e-idafiyyah*, the relative part, of that cycle is what is intended here. It has been consistently noted that *al-'ashir* in this passage does not refer to that *al-'ashir* in the absolute sense, but rather to the *juz'-e-idafiyyah* of this *qawr* — the *Da'i* who is the relative portion of this cycle, not the *sahib al-qawr* himself, but the *juz'-e-idafiyyah* — and he became veiled by the Prophet.

*Wa 'ala fatin mudawij al-a'immah*. (And upon the youth who is the spouse of the Imams.)

And upon the *fatin*, the youth — Lady Fatimah — who became the spouse of the Imams. She was the wife of Ali, and from Ali she became the mother and, in the deeper sense, the companion of the Imams. *Bi-sirrin daqqa ma'nahu*. (With a secret whose meaning is subtle.) With a secret whose meaning is most profound. As the Prophet declared in the presence of the Commander of the Faithful: *khalifatu abiha* — she is her father's successor; and *hujjatu ba'liha* — she is the *hujjah* of her husband. And the most excellent *hujjah*, *afdal al-hujjah*, is she herself. It is for this reason that Shahr Rajab al-Murajjab, which is attributed to Ali (*'alayhis salam*), has its first night as a night of great station — and that first night alludes to Lady Fatimah, who is the *hujjat al-uzma*, the greatest *hujjah*.

The *du'at* of that era did indeed possess knowledge of these matters. As for the caliphs, some things were known to them. Abu Bakr and Umar had some knowledge of these realities, having lived in proximity to the time of *'Abd al-Muttalib*, but their knowledge was not complete. There is a well-known incident in which Umar said to Hasan or Hussain — in substance — that the honor upon your head was not placed there by me, but by you and your father. This is also preserved in broader tradition.

On another occasion, Imam Hasan or Hussain seated himself upon the *minbar*, the pulpit, and declared: "This *minbar* belongs to my father." And Umar himself testified: "Yes, this *minbar* belongs to your father" — and then took his seat below.


So, the successor who was appointed for the community of the Prophet was Lady Fatimah al-Zahra. And because of this, your passing occurred... first of all, you passed away within six months. And when you would arrive, the Prophet would stand up. *Wa ajlasaha fi maqamiha* — and he would seat her in his place — or *fi maqamihi*, in his place. You would seat her in your own place. This was a great thing. That is, this was to indicate that the one who will sit in my place is Lady Fatimah al-Zahra.

*Wa ghaba 'an hadha al-sirr illa ahluhu wa ahwah* — and this secret was hidden from all but its people and its folk. Those who were unaware of this secret, of its hidden matters, were unaware of it, except for those who were its people.

*Wa 'ala al-Hasan al-mustawda' sirr al-imamah* — and upon al-Hasan, the trustee of the secret of the Imamate. Hasan is such that he is the trustee of the secret of the Imamate.

*Wal-qa'im min al-nasut ila al-lahut ka-arbab al-za'amah* — and the one who rises from `nasut` to `lahut` like the masters of leadership. He is the one who rises from `nasut` to `lahut`, just as the `arbab al-za'amah` — the masters of leadership, the noble ones — rise likewise. Even while designated as the trustee (`mustawda'`), he possesses both a `nasut` and a `lahut`. That is the meaning.

All the `du'at` are connected to Hasan, and the `A'immah` are connected to Sayyidna Hussain. But all the `du'at` are connected to Imam Hasan. In the Holy Quran, Surah al-Nas alludes to Sayyidna Hasan. *Qul a'udhu birabbin-nas* — say, "I seek refuge in the Lord of mankind." The word `nas` here refers to those who are *manusina bid-da'wah*, intimate with the `da'wah`, who have affection for it. So who became the Lord of mankind? That is Sayyidna Hasan. And *rabb al-falaq* — the Lord of the dawn. *Qul a'udhu birabbil-falaq* — say, "I seek refuge in the Lord of the dawn." The word `falaq` means to split — and from that splitting, the Imamate began.

*Wa 'ala al-Hussain al-muzhar al-ghaybah* — and upon al-Hussain, the one who made manifest the occultation (`ghaybah`). He made his own self absent. *Bil-qatli lada ahl al-raybah* — through death at the hands of the people of doubt. Those who harbored doubt watched as your murder took place. Can an Imam be killed? He is infallible. Yet he is the one who made death manifest — he manifested his own death.

*Wa 'alal a'immati min dhurriyyatihi majami' al-anwar* — and upon the Imams from his progeny, the assemblages of lights. `Majami' al-anwar` — assemblages of lights — means what? `Anwar` (lights) and `asrar` (secrets) are two different things. `Asrar` is used for `niyyat` (intention). As for `anwar`, Sayyidna Sulayman has written in this very book that he is the `jami' al-asrar` — the assembler of secrets — of the `du'at` of Yemen who became `du'at` after Sayyidna Dhu'ayb bin Musa. He has written this. There, the word `asrar` is used, not `anwar`. And `anwar` refers to the `suwarat` — the luminous forms. He is their assembler.

*Wa asma' al-'ayn al-'azimah fil-'ali min al-asrar* — and the names of the great essence in the high secrets. These `A'immah Tahirin` are the names of the great essence, dwelling in the great and high secrets. Because the great essence — all the `A'immah` who came until the Day of Resurrection — they are veiled by him, and he is their `hijab` (veil). These Imams are a `hijab`, and their veiled one is Amir al-Mu'minin.

*Wa 'ala Imam al-zaman al-mutawari bil-hijabi* — and upon the Imam of the Time, who is concealed by the veil. *Al-mustatir bil-sirr 'an jami' al-bara'a* — the one who is hidden by the secret from all of creation. Your original self is hidden, and what has appeared is a `hijab`.

*Wa sallama 'alayhim wa 'ala abwabihim al-mutalali' anwaruhum fis-suwar* — and may peace be upon them and upon their `abwab` (gates), whose lights shine forth in the forms.

*Wa 'ala hujajihim wa 'ala du'at hadha al-dawr al-maymun al-qa'in khamsina alfa sanah mimma ta'uddun* — and upon their `hujjahs`, and upon the `du'at` of this auspicious `dawr` (era), which will last for fifty thousand years of what you count.

Now look at what he said: "upon the `du'at` of this `dawr`" — that is, the `dawr` of the `qa'im`, the era of the Qa'im — "upon its `du'at` too, may God's peace be upon them." This `dawr` will last for fifty thousand years. Where did fifty thousand come from? When the Qa'im comes, from after him fifty thousand years will begin. So this present era is included within it — the `dawr al-satr` (era of concealment) ended with Sayyidna Sulayman Sahib; the seven eras finished, as confirmed by calculation as well. And this `dawr` is included in the `dawr` of the Qa'im. That is why he says: *al-qa'in khamsina alfa sanah mimma ta'uddun*.

*Wa Sulayman bin Hasan al-mujtama' ladayhi asraru du'at al-Yaman* — and Sulayman bin Hasan, with whom are gathered the secrets of the `du'at` of Yemen. The secrets were gathered in whose possession? With you. What a great station is yours!

*Wa Ja'far al-sabi' fis-sirr al-ma'mun* — and Ja'far, the seventh in the protected secret. Ja'far, who is the seventh — that is, being the 28th, you became the seventh — in the manifest, protected secret.

*Wa mimun min la'la* — and a Mim from above. Mim refers to Muhammad bin Fu'ad, that intermediate figure who came — although he was non-independent, he came in between.

*Al-qa'im bi-fard al-jihad fi mala'in* — the one who established the duty of jihad amongst the assembly. He is the one who establishes the duty of jihad in the midst of the assembly, that is, among the people. You established jihad. What a tremendous jihad you carried out! Consider: if only Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fu'ad's position had been ruined, as happened with `Abd al-Muttalib` and others, what would have become of the `da'wah`? The history of the `da'wah` would have changed entirely. But what a tremendous jihad you carried out!

*Wa al-'ayn al-mutafajjir 'ala lisanihi yanabi' al-asrar* — and 'Ayn, upon whose tongue gushed forth the springs of secrets. `Ayn` means Sayyidna Ali bin Sulayman — such that gushing forth from your tongue are the springs of secrets. The various secrets of the doctrines, their springs and fountains, are manifest from your tongue. In your `haqa'iq` and other books, what sublime matters of doctrine you presented — things that had not been presented before. You manifested them. And *Shu'al al-Anwar*, which is the first book, you yourself wrote that "this *Shu'al al-Anwar* is in the `haqa'iq` (realities) and the `daqa'iq` (subtleties)" — *hadha al-kitab fil haqa'iq wad-daqa'iq*. From there, the books of subtleties began.

Sayyidna Ja'far did not write a single book on `haqa'iq`. All his books are on `hujjat` (proof) or `ta'wil` (interpretation), although his own station is very lofty. And Mim and `Ayn` are often mentioned to refer to the `muhtajib`, but here they are being used to refer to the `du'at`.

*Al-'ati bi-ma la yakhtur 'alal afkar* — bringing forth that which had never occurred to the thoughts. Such things you brought forth that had never crossed people's thoughts, had never been made manifest in them. The books you have — *Anwar al-Shashaniyyah* and others — and the lofty realities within them, are very special things that you have presented.

*Wa saribuhu alladhi ghada ghidha' al-nufus bi-asrar al-malakut al-quddus* — and his drinking companion, who became the nourishment of souls with the secrets of the holy `malakut`. Your `sarib` — your drinking companion — means Sayyidna Ibrahim. He became the nourishment of souls with the secrets of the holy `malakut`, from the secrets of Allah, the Holy.

*Wa Muhammaduhu al-muhajir min baldatihi li-'uluwwi shanih wa rutbatihi* — and his Muhammad, the emigrant from his town for the loftiness of his station and his rank. Muhammad here means Muhammad bin Isma'il. He is the first `Da'i` who emigrated from his town — to Najran. Until then, the `da'wah` had been in Yemen: in Sana'a, in Haraz, and in Sa'fan. But he is the first `Da'i` who left Yemen and came to Najran, for the loftiness of his station and rank. He is also the first and only `Da'i` who was given permission to lead prayers in `ghayr jazirah` — outside the Arabian peninsula — and so he led prayers in Habasha (Abyssinia).

After him, did the `du'at` go back to Yemen? A few remained where they were, but then they moved. It was in the time of Sayyidna Hasan Shihab and Sayyidna Ali bin 'Ibad that they were here. And at the time of Sayyidna Ghulam Hussain Sahib, they came fully to Najran — though in fact it was somewhat before that.

And what did you do? You emigrated towards Najran, and you also went to Abuna — Sayyidna Muhammad bin Isma'il. So Sayyidna Sulayman Sahib, in his time, toured Abuna and stayed there for quite some time. Whereas when you came from Yemen to India, it was a journey of great hardship that took one year, traveling through various cities of Syria and so on. You stayed in Abuna as well, and you built a mosque there, which still exists today. Sayyidna Hussain bin Hasan later enlarged that mosque, and in the time of Sayyidna Hussain bin Isma'il it was further expanded and rooms were built for those sitting in `i'tikaf` (seclusion). It is a very magnificent mosque.

Abuna is not a port — it is a mountainous area, very far from the coast, about two hundred kilometers from Najran, quite remote and mountainous. Sayyidna Sahib went there, and I also had the chance to go there once. The grave of Sayyidna Rashid — Rashid bin 'Isa — is also there, and we visited his grave and performed the *ziyarat*. Sayyidna Rashid is the one who, after becoming a `Da'i`, stood for six years in his prayer niche making up his missed prayers. It is possible that in the early period of the `da'wah`, some prayers were missed; he made up for six years of them standing in the prayer niche.

After the passing of Sayyidna Hussain bin Hasan, Sayyidna Hussain bin Isma'il gave one of their companions — Ahmad was his name, a boy from Yemen, very intelligent and an exceptionally good young man — he gave him the prayers of Sayyidna Hussain bin Hasan, the prayers of four years that had been missed, because in your last days you became like an angel and could not keep track of your prayers. So he made up for four years of prayers. And when Sayyidna Hussain bin Hasan passed away, you came from your house barefoot to his side — a considerable distance, from roughly here to Grand Road station — you came there barefoot.

*Sallam 'alayhim ajma'in abad al-abadin* — peace be upon them all, forever and ever. May peace be upon all of them, for all time.

*Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim*.

*Fatiha*.

*Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim...*

*Fatiha*.

*Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim...*

<!-- lecture 2 -->

Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. By His name, the Exalted, the High. *Wa bihi nasta'in, wa sallallahu 'ala rasulih, Sayyidna Muhammadin wa 'ala aali Muhammadin wa barik wa sallim.* As it is said, it is the statement of Allah Ta'ala: *Wa idha qara'ta al-Qur'an fasta'idh billahi min al-shaytan al-rajim* — and when you recite the Quran, seek refuge in Allah from Satan the accursed. Since this is the Book of Allah, why was the command given to seek refuge (`ta'awwudh`)? When it is said *Wa idha qara'ta al-Qur'an* — when you recite the Quran, you should seek refuge — what is the reason for this? Although this is the word of Allah, the source of guidance, people have been led astray by this very thing. A `da'i` once held up this `Kitabullah` (Book of Allah) and said that it is this very book that has led communities astray — meaning that those who misinterpreted it and derived wrong meanings from it caused communities to go astray. For this reason, upon the command of Allah, while reciting the Quran and reading other `du'as` that contain Quranic verses, we also adopt the practice of `ta'awwudh` at the beginning of the `da'wah`.

But not while giving the adhan. One should not do it while giving the adhan. There, one should only say `Bismillah`. And in the adhan, the two final utterances of *La ilaha illallah* — the very last one should not be recited in the same emphatic manner as the first, but rather in a soft tone. There should be a difference. Because that is related to the `ihtizaz` of a particular being (`dhat`), for which there are special prayers, in that he is the last `mushir` — the last person who will depart from his entire circle in the blink of an eye. That matter will happen in the blink of an eye, and therefore it should not be elongated.

As for the verse *wa qulil hamdu lillah...* — and say, "All praise is for Allah" — after that, we should recite `salawat`. Because the verse only says to say the `takbira`, one might think one should start directly with "Allahu Akbar," even without `Bismillah` — that between the words of the adhan and the verse there should be no other words.

No, it is not like that. After *wa kabbirhu takbira* — and magnify Him with great magnification — one may recite the `salawat` first, and then *wa qulil hamdu lillah... wa kabbirhu takbira*, "Allahu Akbar, Allahu Akbar, Allahu Akbar." There is no hard and fast rule. `Bismillah` should be said before the adhan. There is no strict rule that the adhan must start immediately after *wa kabbirhu takbira* — before it we read from our own side, and there is no harm in doing so.

As for the order (`tartib`): Imam-ul-Ma'mool explained in a `tashrih` that since `hamd` (praise) should come first — that is why it says *qulil hamd* — the `salawat` is recited later, so it comes later. Recite `hamd` first, do not put anything before it. `Hamd` is a higher thing, so recite `hamd` first, then the `salawat`. He explained the order in this way.

And another point: this is the last verse of Surah Bani Isra'il. Among the names that verses carry, it is called `Ayat al-'Izz` — the Verse of Honor.

*Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Allahumma wahfaz dhata al-da'i al-jami'a li-asrarihim al-mujtama'a ladayhi anwaruhum* — O Allah, protect the being of the `da'i`, who is the collector of their secrets (`asrar`) and with whom their lights (`anwar`) are gathered. This is speaking about the future. Because in life, `asrar` means `riyah`. And `anwar` means `surah` — forms. In the `da'i`'s lifetime, the `riyah` of the `hudud al-da'wah` or the `mu'minin`, and the `anwar` meaning the `surah`, are not yet fully formed. The `riyah` will emerge after death — after being buried or after passing away, the `riyah` begin to emerge, for three days. That is a matter for later. But later, they will be gathered with him: their `asrar`, meaning their `riyah`, and gathered with him will be their `anwar`, meaning their `surah`.


`Wahmi hawzatahu`. And protect his `hawzah`. A `hawzah` is a place around which a boundary has been drawn — a walled enclosure. What is meant here? The center of the `da'wah`. Protect the center of the `da'wah` `min fawaran fir'awni al-zaman` — from the aggression of the Pharaoh of the age. Because `li-kulli da'in fir'awn` — for every `da'i` there is a Pharaoh, an opponent of the `da'wah`. We had asked in Hujjat that during the time of Sayyidna Husayn ibn Ahmad, there did not seem to be any such Pharaoh openly opposing the `da'i`. It was a magnificent era — a time of complete peace. The disturbances came later. So it was said, "Well, the `da'i` of the Dawoodis that you have is also a kind of Pharaoh" — in a passive sense. He did not openly confront, but had he found the opportunity, he would have done as Dawood ibn Qutub did and made a claim, becoming a `mudda'i`.

But here we see that the opposition is greater. Yes — there it was not so, but here there is a Pharaoh.

`wa ballighhu jami'a ma'moolihi ya Rahim ya Rahman` — and may Allah convey to him all his aspirations, his desires; may He fulfill the `da'i`'s desires. `Ya Rahim ya Rahman`. Now `ya Rahim ya Rahman` is also a `tasbih` — the `tasbih` of the Pir.

Shouldn't it be `ya Rahman ya Rahim`? In the Quran, `Rahman` always precedes `Rahim`. But in books this order is not always observed, because of rhyme schemes (`qafiyah-payma'i`) — `Fir'awn al-zaman` came before it, so `ya Rahim ya Rahman` follows for the sake of the rhyme. In other divine attributes as well, `Rahim` always comes second: `Ra'uf-ur-Rahim`, `Aziz-ur-Rahim` — it has never come first in the Quran. In the `du'as` and `sahifas`, however, `Rahim` precedes `Rahman` in several places. And `Rahman` has also appeared alone in the Quran, as in the verse `Qulid'ullaha awid'ur-Rahman` — "Say, 'Call upon Allah or call upon al-Rahman'" — whereas no other name of Allah has appeared alone in this way.

In Surah al-Hajj, at one place it reads `Ya Dhal-Jalali wal-Ikram` — O Possessor of Majesty and Honor. The verses of Surah al-Hajj from 58 to 65 are very blessed verses. Among them are eight verses called `Mafatih al-Jinan` — the Keys to the Gardens — corresponding to the eight heavens, whose keys these verses are. There is great virtue in reciting them. One verse alone contains fifteen of the `Asma' al-Husna` — the Beautiful Names of Allah — and `Ya Dhal-Jalali wal-Ikram` is counted as one among them. `Rahman` appears there as well.

`wa anir masabih da'watihi` — "And illuminate the lamps of his `da'wah`" — meaning the `hudud al-da'wah`; illuminate the entire `da'wah`. `wa kaththir 'adada shi'atihi` — "And increase the number of his followers." `wa asri ilayna mawaddahu` — "And cause to flow towards us his substance (`mawad`)" — and his confirmations (`ta'yidat`). `wa 'allimna bi-lata'ifi barakatihi` — "And teach us through the subtleties of his blessings" — direct the subtle dimensions of his blessings towards us. `warzuqna tahannunahu wa shafaqatahu` — "And grant us the sustenance of this `da'i`'s kindness (`tahannun`) and compassion (`shafaqah`)." `wa 'afwahu wa rahmatuhu` — "And his forgiveness (`'afw`) and his mercy (`rahmah`)" — that he may forgive our sins. `Ya 'Aliyyu ya 'Azim, ya 'Aliyyu ya 'Azim`. This too is a `tasbih`, one performed on Wednesdays.

`Wa ba'du, fa-inna-ha waradat masa'il 'aliyatun` — "And after this, there came lofty issues." Some lofty questions were presented (`warid`) — they came forward — `ila al-hadrat al-samiyah`, to the elevated presence, that is, to the venerable `da'i`. `min ba'd al-awlad` — from some of the children (`awlad`). That is, to Sayyidna Hibtullah al-Mu'ayyad, from one of the children — the questions that the `abna' al-da'wah`, the children of the `da'wah`, bring. `Fa-sata al-amru al-'alami` — the term `al-'alami` is used for Sayyidna Hibtullah; one of his titles is `Alamuddin`, the banner of religion, the high banner of the faith. "So the command of `al-'alami`" — of the `da'i` — "flowed `ila 'abdihi akhar`," to his other servant, upon me. Hamza b. al-Walid speaks; Sayyidna Ali says it flowed upon him — `al-haqir` — `bi-an yujibu 'ala su'alatihi`, that he should answer those questions. `wa ma lahu bi-aqlamihi tatr` — "And with his pens, to set it down in writing as well." The answer was to be not merely verbal but committed to writing.

`Wa kuntu aradtu an a'tadhira ila mawlayya li-qusuri 'ilmi wa bu'di fitrati wa fahmi` — "And I had wanted to make an excuse to my master on account of the deficiency of my knowledge and the distance of my nature and understanding." I wished to present the excuse to my master that my knowledge is deficient, that I am greatly lacking, and that my intellect and understanding are weak. The `da'i` — Sayyidna Ali — is saying this. `fa-'aradtu mawani'a ba'd al-bilad` — "And I presented the obstacles posed by distant lands" — the people from various lands who had brought questions; how could I answer them all? This was my excuse. `fa-asra'tu al-ta'ata` — "But in the end, I hastened to obey." I obeyed; I accepted your command. `wa asdaqtu al-murad` — "And I found the desired object" — I attained the goal. `wa tadharra'tu ila suratihi al-nuraniyah` — "And I supplicated through his luminous form (`surah nuraniyah`)." `wa awwaltu ila mawaddihi al-qudsaniyah` — "And I relied upon his holy substance (`mawad qudsaniyah`)."

Now observe what a remarkable thing has been said here. He says, "I supplicated through your luminous form." The `da'i`'s form (`surah`) has not yet been constituted — the `da'i` is still `natiqah`, `hissiyah`, and `namiyah`. The `surah` is only formed after passing away. But the `da'i`'s station is such that his personality is so magnificent that we must understand his very `natiqah` as the blessed `surah` — the one that will be fully realized as a `surah` on the Day of Resurrection.

`wa wassamtu hadhihi al-risalah bi-kunuz al-hikmah` — "And I named this treatise `Kunooz al-Hikmah`" — the Treasures of Wisdom. Treasures stored for the People of `Ismah` (`arbab al-'ismah`). By `arbab al-'ismah` here, it does not mean the Imams, but those who are the masters of partial `ismah` (`juzwi 'ismah`) — the `du'at`. For them these things have been stored, so that they may draw substance (`mawad`) from them. As was stated in the introduction to this `risalah`, this matter had not been articulated by any `da'i` before: that the `aql al-awwal` — the first intellect — and the `akhir al-mawjud` — the last existent being, meaning the `mustajib` who has just entered the `hadrat sharifah` and stepped into the circle of faith — are equal. Equal in what? In the `ma'rifah` — the gnosis — of the `Mubda' Ta'ala`, the Exalted Originator. The `aql al-awwal`, despite its proximity, has no advantage in the `ma'rifah` of the `Mubda' Ta'ala`; it is perplexed and bewildered in the very same way that we are perplexed and bewildered. No `da'i` had said this before.

`fa-ma kana fiha min sawab` — "And whatever is correct (`sawab`) in it" — whatever correctness is present in this book — `fa-mansubun ilayya ila man ajra lisani` — "then it is attributed to the one who caused my tongue to flow," the one who gave such strength to my tongue that I could express and set it down — attributed to him, to the `da'i`. This `sawab`, from `sad-waw-alif-ba`, means correctness, not reward. As one sees in common usage, "Jazakallah bil-khairi was-sawab" — people write it with the sense of reward (`thawab`). But here `sawab` means correctness: "What you have said is correct."

It is a subtle point — everyone tends to read `sawab` as `thawab`.

`ila man ajra lisani` — to the one who caused my tongue to flow. `wa ma kana fiha min khata', wa billahi al-'iyadh` — "And whatever mistake is in it — and in Allah I seek refuge — `fa-mansubun ilayya` — then it is attributed to me," the error is mine. `idh ana alaqtu al-jani` — "As I am a sinner (`jani`)." People sometimes call their beloved or their children `jani`. But in Arabic, `jani` means sinner.

`wa ana akhidhu hadh al-'ahd al-mu'akkad` — "And I am taking this confirmed covenant (`'ahd`)." `wa mithaquhu al-mughalladh al-mushaddad` — "And its pact, which is severe and strong." `'ala man waqa'a hadha fi yadayhi` — "Upon whosoever this falls into his hands" — whoever's hands this book reaches and who becomes aware of its secret — `an la yaqra'a fihi` — "that he should not read in it," `wa la yata'ammala ma'anihi` — "and should not ponder its meanings," `illa bi-fushin min huwa al-naqid` — "except with the permission of one who is the `naqid`." A `naqid` is one who can discern the good from the bad, who understands what is right and what is wrong — one who criticizes with insight. `wal-basir` — "and the insightful one" — who possesses inner sight. `man lahu al-taqdim wal-ta'khir` — "the one who has the authority of `taqdim` and `ta'khir`" — who can bring someone forward within the `hudud` or put them back, who holds full authority in appointments. Only with his permission may one read it. `wa illa, fa-huwa bari'un min al-'Aliyy al-ma'bud` — "Otherwise, he is absolved from the Exalted, the Worshipped." Meaning, `al-'Aliyy al-ma'bud` — Allah, the Most High, Whom we worship — will know his matter. `wa min kaffat al-mahdudin wal-hudud` — "And from all the `mahdudin` and `hudud`" — from their obedience, he is absolved.

`wa hadha qawl al-sa'il hujjatuhu` — "And this is the statement of the questioner, his argument" — the one who is writing this introduction has set down the entire question within it. `awradtuhu bi-fassih ru'yatihi` — "I have presented it with the clarity of his vision" — in the very manner in which the question was brought to the `da'i`. `hafidhallahu al-maqam al-'azim` — "May Allah protect the great station (`maqam al-'azim`)." `wal-'alam al-rabbani al-'alim` — "And the lordly, knowing banner" — the one who is a lordly scholar, deeply learned: `Alamuddin Hibtullah b. Ibrahim al-Muqrami`. `hafidhahullahu wa dafa'a 'anhu al-aswa'` — "May Allah protect him and ward off evils from him."

Now, `aswa'` — does it mean the `da'i` has evils, that we are praying for them to be warded off? Consider how Sayyidna al-Sijilmasi says, "My sins are like mountains — `idhama intalat al-jibali`" — they have risen so high. Were his sins truly so great? We do not regard even great sins of our own in such terms. So what is the meaning of such expressions? The `da'i`'s sins, in reality, are this: when they intend to perform a good deed — to write a book, to record a noble thought or concept, to benefit someone — but due to some obstacle they are unable to carry it through. The good deed they intended but could not complete — this they regard as their sin. That they had the intention but could not translate it into action. They count those as grave sins.

Allah also says to the Prophet that his former and latter sins are forgiven. God forbid — the Prophet's sins? For that, one recalls what Sayyidna Husayn b. Ismail, Qaddas Allahu Ruhahu, said. He recounted that he and his brother were studying when Muhsin came — Sayyidna Ali b. Husayn's brother, a thin and frail man who was not himself a great scholar but was always in the presence of the `da'i`, his brother, and used to ask many questions. He was both Muhsin's son-in-law and his father-in-law — Muhsin b. Ali. He came and asked, "May I put a question?" The question was about the first verse of Surah al-Fath: `Inna fatahna laka fathan mubina` — "Indeed, We have granted you a clear victory" — `li-yaghfira laka Allahu ma taqaddama min dhanbika wa ma ta'akhkhara` — "so that Allah may forgive you your former and latter sins." What were the Prophet's former and latter sins?

Sayyidna looked and asked, "Do you know?" The answer, drawing from the `da'i`'s substance (`mawad`), is this: the verse is not referring to the Prophet's own being, which is pure (`bari`). Rather, the `hujab` — those who rise from the `hudud al-da'wah` and later become deputies — do not initially possess the substance of `ismah`; `ismah` comes to them later. In the meantime they are unable to fully govern themselves and sometimes err. The allusion is to them. The former and latter sins refer to the `hujab` of the Prophet — those inclined towards Abu Bakr. That inclination was the sin, and the second was the yielding of a portion of Ali's `wilayah` to him, so that the system of the `da'wah` might be preserved. Allah was displeased with this, and a verse came: `Wa in ashrakta layahbatanna 'amaluka` — "And if you associate partners, your work will surely become worthless." To whom is this addressed? To the very Prophet from whom we learned the lesson of faith. This is not referring to outward shirk — it means: if you include even an iota of anyone else in the `da'wah` of Ali, in the `wilayah`, We will nullify your work. That is the meaning. And here Allah says: all your matters, We have completely forgiven them.

`wa hadha qawl al-sa'il hujjatuhu awradtuhu bi-fassihi wa hay'atihi`. `wa dafa'a 'anhu al-aswa'`. The `aswa'` — the evils — are in reality the `mahdudin` and the `hudud al-da'wah`. They are what is truly meant by the evils.


`wa ballighhu kulla ma yarumu wa yahwa`. "And convey to him everything that he intends and desires." May Allah fulfill whatever you intend and whatever you desire. `wa 'alayhi min 'abdihi afdhal al-salam`. "And upon him from his servant, the best of peace." `wal-matlub min tafaddulihi jawab ma sa'altu 'anhu`. "And what is requested from his grace is the answer to what I have asked about." It is requested that, from your grace, you answer what I have asked. These are the words of the questioner. `bi-hadhb al-manni 'alayya wal-tafadduli wal-tatawwuli`. "By the favor of grace upon me, and grace and bounty." The grace that you wish to bestow upon me. `wa uqaddimu bayna yadayhi ma as'alu 'anhu ilayhi`. "And I present before him what I ask about to him." Of the `wilayah` of the `hudud` of your religion. `wa al-dallina 'alayhi`. "And those who guide towards him." `wa abra'u mimman ankara addanihum`. "And I absolve myself from those who deny their lowest ones." Meaning the `hudud al-din`. `wa atadharra'u ilayhi bi-wusuli 'alam al-ibda' al-kiram`. "And I supplicate to him through the connection of the noble world of creation." Who are the honorable ones. `wa bi-haqqi kulli wasiyyin 'azimin wa imamin`. "And by the right of every great successor (`wasi`) and Imam." `wa bi-kulli da'iratin tadawwarat`. "And by every circle that revolved." The various circles — of Imamate, of `qa'imiyat` — the circles. `wa nuktatin sharifatin takawwanat`. "And a noble point that came into being."

`tahjibuni`. That you may not set me aside, given the weakness of my master. `fa-mathaluka man yasturu 'ala misli`. "For your example is one who covers the faults of someone like me." `wa idha tafaddalta an la tukhayyibni mimma ammaltu`. "And when you show grace, that you may not disappoint me in what I have hoped for." `bi-haqqika al-'azim la tahmalu haqqi`. "By your great right, do not neglect my right." Meaning, do not abandon me by withholding an answer. `wa an tarhamani birrika wa 'ammika`. And your piety (`bir`) and your beneficence (`'am`) — your `sirrika`, your forgiveness (`'afw`), your help (`awn`), and your kindness (`ghawth`) — in the two worlds (`darayn`), meaning this world and the hereafter. `wal-nazar ila ma afuh bihi nafsan aw tafawwuhan`. And what I will say and what my soul is filled with, or has uttered. `la yujidu lahu ma'na`. "Which has no meaning." Meaning, I am asking questions, and among them there will also come matters in which my soul is implicated, or which it has uttered. So for me — `fa-albisni sawb al-sitr wal-ghina'`. "Then clothe me with the garment of concealment (`sitr`) and contentment (`ghina'`)." Place the veils upon me. `wa umnun 'alayya bi-tatabu'i ihsanika`. "And favor me with the continuity of your benevolence." `razaqani Allahu ridaka`. "May Allah grant me the sustenance of your pleasure (`rida`)." `wa qad lakam a'udhu billahi wa bil-'ali`. "And indeed, I seek refuge in Allah and in the Exalted." `an la yadhunna al-dhannu anni ma as'alu illa mutaddi'an fasaahat lisan wa ghadharat jinan`. "That the one who thinks may not think that I am asking only as a claimant of eloquence of tongue or sharpness of mind." No, by Allah, never. `wa innama qasartu 'an al-shurbi min ra's al-ma'in`. "But rather, I fell short of drinking from the head of the spring." Meaning, from the `da'i` himself — from benefiting directly from him — I was deprived. `wa qad 'alima al-hatm al-'azim al-barr al-rahim bi-aqdari 'abdihi`. And the great, pious, merciful decree knew what the `qa'id` has stated — `kaifa wa sadqa min sadaqatihi`. That whatever you bestow upon me, it will be a charity (`sadaqah`) from your charities. `wa nafkhatan min nafahatihi`. "And a breath from his breaths." It will be a forgiveness from your forgiveness. `wa la akhira qurbanan illa ma qaddamtu min masturihi`. "And I do not seek any other nearness except what I have presented from his written work." I have presented it to you. That is all.

This is the introduction. The questions have not started yet. The questioner did not frame his questions with great precision — pointing things out directly — it is not framed in that way.

<!-- lecture 3 -->

In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful. And in Him we seek help. May Allah's blessings and peace be upon His Messenger, Sayyidna Muhammad, and upon the family of Muhammad.

This book of ours — as you all know — is `Kunooz al-Hikmah al-madhkura li-arbab al-'ismah` (The Treasures of Wisdom Mentioned for the People of 'Ismah). This is the name of the book. It is a work by Sayyidna 'Ali ibn Hibatullah al-Mukarrami, which we have begun, and two lessons have already been completed. This is our third lesson.

`Al-su'al al-awwal` (The first question).

`Al-su'al al-awwal`. In our copy, at the beginning — after `mustadall ilayh` — it reads `awwalan al-su'al al-awwal` (Firstly, the first question). First — the first question. `mastur al-awwalan`. `Al-su'al al-awwal. Inni basartu wa ta'ammaltu fima tafaddaltum bihi min kutub awliya' Allah` (The first question. Indeed I have seen and contemplated that which you have graciously provided from the books of the friends of God) — from the books of the friends of God — that is, the questioner is asking his question. He says: I have reflected and pondered upon the matter with which you have graciously favored me — from the books, the books of the `awliya' Allah` (friends of God). `wa ba'd al-mufidin` (and from some of the benefactors) — and the benefactors with whom I have studied. `lam ajid ma dallani 'ala ma kana qabla huduth 'alam al-ibda'` (I did not find that which would guide me to what existed before the origination of the 'Alam al-Ibda' [World of Origination]). Even after studying with them, I have not found the answer to this question — that which would indicate what existed before the 'Alam al-Ibda'.

What was there before the 'Alam al-Ibda'? This is the questioner asking. `wa kayfa kana al-hal wa ma al-majal wa al-minwal?` (And what was the state of affairs, and what was the scope and the manner?) What were the matters occurring before the 'Alam al-Ibda'? What things were taking place, and what were the conditions there? He has asked about this. `Wa mimma hadathat 'uqul 'alam al-ibda'?` (And from what did the Intellects of the 'Alam al-Ibda' come into being?) And as for the Intellects of the 'Alam al-Ibda' — what things pertained to them? `fa-qad ra'ayna fi kutub awliya' Allah wa afadna anna al-Imam hijabuhu al-khass min riyad al-hadrah.` (For we have seen in the books of the friends of God and we have benefited from the fact that the Imam, his special veil (`hijab al-khass`), is from the gardens of the Presence (`hadrah`)). We have seen in the `kutub awliya' Allah` (books of the friends of God), and we have also benefited from the understanding that the Imam's `hijab al-khass` (special veil) is from the `riyad` (gardens) of the `hadrah` (Presence). Meaning, the Imam's `hijab al-khass` is fashioned from the `hadarat` (presences) — it is made from the `riyad` of the `hadarat`.

The questioner has asked this question. Al-Walid 'Abdullah Sahib used to always say that one should not rely on the question, but rather on the answer — on what the Da'i is answering. This questioner has asked about the Imam's `hijab al-khass` (special veil), for there are three types of `hijabs` in a broad reckoning: `tabi'i` (natural), `dhilli` (shadowy), and `khass` (special). Beyond these is the `dhat-i Imam` (essence of the Imam). The questioner asks how this has been explained in the `tasbih` (prayer beads).


So, he is saying here that what existed before the `Ibda'` — this is what he is asking — and along with that, he is also speaking about what came after. The questioner has posed two questions: first, what was there before the `'Alam al-Ibda'`; and second, what were the circumstances of the `uqul` of the `'Alam al-Ibda'` that came — what happened to them? He is saying that the `hijab al-khass` (special veil) is made from the `riyad al-hadrah` (gardens of the presence). He has presented his own concept. Now, there are three types of `hijabs`. So how have they been placed within the `tasbih`? In the `tasbih`, we first say `Allahu Akbar` (God is the greatest). When we say `Allahu Akbar`, that points toward the `hijab al-adna` (lowest veil), which is also called the `hijab al-tabi'i` (natural veil) or the `hijab al-tanasuli` (procreative veil). The second part of the thirty-three is the `hijab al-dhilli` (shadowy veil). And the third part, of `Subhanallah` (Glory be to God), is the `hijab al-khass` (special veil). In the end, we come to the Imam, who is the `dhat-i Imam` (essence of the Imam). So we pass through these three states. The one who is speaking says that the `hijab al-khass` of the Imam is made from the `riyad` (gardens) of the `hadarat` (presences). He has presented his own concept. `wa hijab al-la'iq al-tabi'i` — the veil that comes after it, connected to it, is the `tabi'i` (natural) one — `min riyad hujaj al-jaza'ir` (from the gardens of the Hujaj of the Islands), meaning it is made from the `riyad` of the `hujaj al-jaza'ir` (Proofs of the Islands). `ta'wi ila ba'd nisa' al-hijab al-zawahir` — this `hijab al-tabi'i` comes from some women — `al-hijab al-zawahir 'ala hadhihi al-kayfiyyah` (the outward veil upon this modality) — with their outward veils. Meaning, those who perform the act of marriage with their wives — who does that? The `hijab al-tabi'i` (natural veil) of the Imam does that. That is his point, that the `tabi'i` veil performs that act. `ta'wi ila ba'd nisa' al-hijab al-zawahir 'ala hadhihi al-kayfiyyah wujuduhu` (resorting to some of the women of the outward veil upon this modality of its existence). Its existence is in this modality. `wa yadharu bi-khalqihi wa min khalqihi fi khalqihi` (And he appears through his creation, from his creation, and in his creation). That veil appears from creation, from the side of creation, and within creation — meaning that it is made from `riyad` (gardens), and the `riyad` are from the creatures (`makhluqat`). After the veils are formed, they come into human form; those veils, those `riyad`, take human form. That is what is being pointed to.

`wa kayfa kana qabla wujud al-samawat wa al-ard` (And how was it before the existence of the heavens and the earth) — before the existence of the skies and the earth, what things were there? `wa qad 'alimtu mimma wujudha` (and I have known from what its existence is) — I know how the existence of the heavens and the earth came to be. `wa min ayyi fudulatin` (and from what remnants) — from what remnants did it happen? `wujud al-awwal al-jussa' fi safil al-ard` (the existence of the first particle in the low part of the earth). And from what remnants did the `awwal jussa'` (first particle) — meaning the first particle of the `karr` — come into existence? It was created from the clod in the earth. The one that comes at the beginning of the `karr` is created from the clod in the earth, and so it holds a great station. From what remnants did it come, given that there was nothing at that time? `fa-tafaddal bi al-jawab` (So graciously provide the answer). Benefit me with the answer and the explanation.

`Al-jawab 'ala dhalika al-su'al` (The answer to that question). Now the Da'i — Sayyidna 'Ali ibn Hibatullah — says that in response to this question, he is giving his answer. `naqulu bi-'awnillah al-kabir al-muta'al` (We say with the help of Allah, the Great, the Exalted). We say, with the help of Allah, who is `al-kabir al-muta'al` (the Great, the Exalted). `Al-kabir al-muta'al` is also a `tasbih` that we have, which is recited on Thursdays. `wa minnati waliyy al-'asr wa al-zaman` (and from the favor of the master of the age and time) — and from the favor of the `wali al-'asr wa al-zaman` (master of the age and time) — `alayhi salawat al-rahim al-rahman` (upon him be the blessings of the Merciful, the Compassionate). Here `rahim` has come first. In the Quran, `rahim` has never come first; `rahman` has always preceded it. `wa ta'yidi mawlay al-'alam` (and with the support of my master of the world). In `Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim`, among the various interpretations, `al-rahman` in one interpretation points to Mawlana 'Abd al-Muttalib, and `rahim` refers to Mawlana Abu Talib. Mawlana 'Abd al-Muttalib was certainly greater than Mawlana Abu Talib — he is the `jadd al-qa'im` (grandfather of the Qa'im), the `jadd al-'ayn` (grandfather of the 'ayn), and the `jadd al-'ayn al-akhirah` (grandfather of the final 'ayn) as well. `wa ta'yidi mawlay al-'alam` — and with the support of `mawlay al-'alam` (my master of the world) — `wa sirrihi al-bahir al-mu'azzam` (and his manifest and magnified secret) — from this support I am giving the answer to this matter — `anna al-jawab 'ala dhalika qad sabaqa min mawla al-malik 'alam al-iman` (the answer to that has already been given by the master, the king, the flag of faith). This answer has already been given in the book of Sayyidna Hibatullah, in `Hibat al-Rahman`, which he has written — `fi kitabihi al-sharif al-mawsum bi-kanz al-'ulum` (in his noble book, named Kanz al-'Ulum [The Treasure of Knowledge]). In your book `Kanz al-'Ulum`, the answer to your question is already there. This is a very special book; God willing, if we have the opportunity, we will bring it. I recall that in `Kanz al-'Ulum`, there is a passage about Bibi Fatima al-Zahra to the effect that her station and greatness are such that even the A'immah Tahirin (the pure Imams) could not attain her gnosis. For this very reason, the later Imams are called *A'immah Fatimiyyin* (the Fatimid Imams). The Imams up to the twenty-first are called *A'immah Fatimiyyin* because they are perplexed and bewildered by the station of Bibi Fatima al-Zahra.

`'alayka ya ayyuha al-arshad bi al-jawab al-awwala 'ala al-awwal` (Upon you, O rightly guided one, is the first answer to the first question) — the answer to your first question is `bi al-qawl al-'ali al-mumajjad` (with the high and glorified saying) — a saying that is high and great — `ma fihi kifayah kafiyah` (in which there is sufficient sufficiency) — and in it there is sufficiency — `wa ni'ma al-shafiyah` (and what an excellent cure) — and it is a cure. That is: `anna wujud 'alam al-ibda' la min shay'in` (the existence of the World of Origination is not from a thing) — `wujud 'alam al-ibda' la min shay'in wa-la fi shay'in wa-la yajibu al-bahth 'an kayfiyyatihi` (the existence of the World of Origination is not from a thing, and not in a thing, and it is not required to inquire about its modality). This `'Alam al-Ibda'` — meaning the `Mubdi' Ta'ala` (Originator, the Exalted) created the `'Alam al-Ibda'` — so our natural assumption is that there would be a world in which the intellects were created, but it is not like that. Mawlana 'Iddan Sahib also used to say that the world and the intellects are not separate things. Rather, `'alam al-ibda' hum al-'uqul` (the 'Alam al-Ibda' is the Intellects themselves). The `'Alam al-Ibda'` itself is the intellects whom Allah Ta'ala created. The `'Alam al-Ibda'` is without any thing and without being in any thing. `wa-la yajibu...` — therefore it is not obligatory, nor even permissible, to inquire about the modality of the `sudur al-ibda'` (emanation of the origination) — how the `ibda'` came into existence. `wa-la su'al idh laysa li al-'aql fihi majal` (and no question, since the intellect has no scope in it). Our intellects cannot reach that question of how the `ibda'` emanated, how it was created, how it appeared. This is a matter far beyond our intellects. `wa fi al-ajwibah 'an dhalika dalal wa ishrak` (and in the answers to that is misguidance and polytheism). If someone attempts to answer — saying it happens like this or like that — that is misguidance and `shirk` (polytheism). `wa tahawwutun fi bahr al-halak` (and a plunge into the sea of destruction). Whoever speaks of it will fall into the sea of destruction, for we do not even know how the `'Alam al-Ibda'` came into existence. `na'udhu billahi min dhalika` (We seek refuge in Allah from that). `wa nas'aluhu al-nijah min al-mahalik` (and we ask Him for salvation from the places of destruction). We ask for salvation from being destroyed in that matter.

`li-anna dhalika mu'allal fi-man la tajasar naḥwahu al-khawatir` (because that is substantiated for one towards whom thoughts do not dare to venture). That matter is proven by evidence of a kind that the heart — the `khawatir` — cannot dare to approach: how the `Mubdi' Ta'ala` (Originator, the Exalted) created the `'Alam al-Ibda'`, and what that modality was. We cannot have knowledge of its modality. `wa-la tuhitu bihi al-awham wa al-dama'ir` (and estimations and inner thoughts do not encompass it). `Awham` (estimations) and `dama'ir` (inner thoughts) cannot encompass that modality. `wa yahtaj al-'arif li-dhalika 'ala an yakuna ma'ahu surah aw 'aql aw wahm qabla huduth al-ibda'` (And the gnostic needs for that knowledge that he should have with him a form, an intellect, or an estimation from before the origination of the 'ibda'). For the gnostic to know what the matter was before the `ibda'`, he would need some form, intellect, or estimation from before it. `aw fikrah bal suduruhu 'an al-huwiyyah al-muta'aliyah` (or a thought; rather its emanation is from the Exalted Identity). One should simply know this much: that the emanation of the `'Alam al-Ibda'` happened through the `huwiyyah al-muta'aliyah` (Exalted Identity) — meaning the `Mubdi' Ta'ala` (Originator, the Exalted) — by His own means. `la min shay'in` (not from a thing). It did not happen from any pre-existing thing.

`wa qad awdaha al-du'at sababu qillati al-wusul ila ma'rifati dhalika` (And the du'at have clarified the reason for the scarcity of access to the gnosis of that). The `du'at` (preachers) have explained and clarified that our access to its gnosis is almost non-existent. `dhalika li-'ajz al-'uqul al-ibda'iyyah` (that is due to the inability of the originated intellects). These `uqul ibda'iyyah` (originated intellects) — whom the `Mubdi' Ta'ala` (Originator, the Exalted) has created, namely the First Intellect, the Second Intellect, and so on — they themselves are at a loss to know how the `Mubdi' Ta'ala` brought this about. `fa-kayfa bi-man dunahum min al-suwar al-daniyah` (So how about those below them from the lowly forms?). Our forms are very lowly compared to the intellects of the `'Alam al-Ibda'`. How then could we know that modality?

`wa amma al-hijab al-'ali alladhi huwa al-nasut al-khass al-hawi li al-sharaf wa al-ikhtisas` (As for the high veil, which is the special human form, containing honor and specialty) — the `hijab al-'ali` (high veil) is the `nasut al-khass` (special human form), which contains honor and specialty, meaning it is a `khass` (special) one. `wa al-nasut al-tabi'i al-dani` (and the natural, lowly human form) — the `nasut al-tabi'i` (natural human form), which is the lowest `nasut` — `fa-khudh haqiqata ma'rifatiha min awwal al-tamhid` (So take the reality of its gnosis from the beginning of the preface). Take the reality of it from the first preface. Which preface? At the very beginning of this book, he wrote: "All praise is for Allah, who made the `nasut al-khass` from the `riyadat ahl al-ikhtisas` (gardens of the people of specialty)." The `nasut al-khass` is made from the `ahl al-ikhtisas` (people of specialty) — that is, from the `riyadat` (spiritual gardens) of the `hadarat` (presences) and the `hudud` (ranks) of the da'wah; this is the term used for them. `wa ja'ala al-nasut al-dani min riyadat man dunahum min al-mawani` (And He made the lowly human form from the gardens of those below them in station). The `nasut` that is lowly — the `tabi'i` (natural) — is made from those of lesser station. So, the questioner's view that the `hujaj al-jaza'ir` are the source of the `nasut al-tabi'i` is being corrected: that is a wrong concept, and the questioner will understand its reality from the first preface. `tataddahu laka ma'ani` (its meanings will become clear to you). Its meanings and secrets will become clear to you. `wa sun dhalika yasunka Allah fa-huwa min al-sirr al-rabbani` (And guard that, may Allah guard you, for it is from the Lordly secret). Guard this, and Allah will guard you, for it is from the `sirr al-rabbani` (Lordly secret). These matters — concerning the veils and the `nasut`, how they originated and from what they are made — constitute a great `sirr al-rabbani`.

`wa amma qawluka inna al-nasut al-tabi'i al-zahir min hujaj al-jaza'ir fa-huwa minka ghalat` (As for your saying that the manifest natural human form is from the Hujaj of the Islands, that is a mistake on your part). Your concept that the `nasut al-tabi'i` (natural human form) which is most manifest is made from the `hujaj al-jaza'ir` (Proofs of the Islands) is wrong. `wa man i'taqada dhalika fa-qad taradda wa saqat` (And whoever believes that has been rejected and has fallen). Whoever holds this belief will be rejected and will fall. `bal al-awwal fi dhalika ayyuha al-sa'il innahu yajtami'u fi kulli nasutin riyadatu jami' al-sa'irin ila al-lahut` (Rather, the first principle in that, O questioner, is that in every human form gather the spiritual gardens of all the travelers to the Divine Realm). In every `nasut`, the `riyadat` (spiritual gardens) of all who are traveling `ila al-lahut` (to the Divine Realm) — meaning all believers — are gathered, `min 'alin wa danin` (from the high and the low), whether high or low.

`hasbama amma 'anhu sayyiduna al-da'i sarimuddin` (according to what our master, the Da'i Sarimuddin, implied) — as Sayyidna Sarimuddin, meaning Sayyidna Ibrahim ibn Muhammad ibn Fahd, explained — `ba'd kutubihi al-'aliyah` (in some of his high books) — and besides him, other `du'at` (masters of the da'wah) have also pointed to this matter — `wa ghayruhu min mawalinna al-du'at 'ala al-lawh al-sharif al-altaf` (and others from our masters, the du'at, upon the noble and subtle tablet) — `fi al-majam' al-nuraniyyah` (in the luminous collections). The `riyadat` ascend within the `nasut`, and the `surat` (forms) ascend within the `lahut`. The point of saying this is that the `nasut` of the Imam — within which the `riyadat` are ascending — and the `lahut` of the Imam — within which the `surat` are ascending — are in perfect correspondence. It is not that the `riyadat` that go to the place of the ear in the Imam's `nasut` will arrive at a different place, such as the heart or a lower station, in the `lahut`. At whatever place the `riyadat` go in the `nasut`, the `surat` will go to that same place in the `lahut`. That is what he means to say.

`murad al-hijab al-khass huwa al-mubtana fi al-bayt al-haram` (The intended meaning of the special veil is that which is built in the Sacred House). The `nasut-i khass` (special human form) has its foundation laid in the `Bayt al-Haram` (Sacred House of God) — `huwa alladhi la ta'zibuhu qara'ib al-aslab wa al-arham` (it is that which the proximities of loins and wombs do not confine). This `nasut-i khass` is not bound by loins and wombs; it is not born from a mother and father. Its construction, its making, happens in the Sacred House of God. The `tabi'i` (natural) one, by contrast, is born from a mother and father. `wa-la tahwih afniyah wa-la dur` (and neither courtyards nor houses contain it). No courtyard, no ground, no house can contain the `nasut-i khass` — it is so exalted that it lies beyond all these things. `wa-la yaliju fi 'iḍam al-qubur` (And it does not enter into the bones of the graves). Nor does it descend into the graves; at the time of death, the `nasut-i khass` does not even descend into the grave. That is the Imam's affair. `wa-lladhi yaqa'u 'alayhi al-mawt wa al-qatl wa al-fawt` (And the one upon whom death, killing, and passing away occur) — the one upon whom death occurs, killing occurs, and passing away occurs — `huwa adna min al-hujub` (it is the lowest of the veils). That is the lowest among the veils. `li-yaqdiya ma 'alayhi wa yajib` (so that it may fulfill what is upon it and is obligatory). The states that befall it fall upon this lowest veil, not upon the `nasut-i khass`. Just as it was with Imam Husayn — the killing and all that happened befell the veils; nothing happened to the real self. The real self is protected.

As for which specific `hijab` was in use at that time, there is no mention of it anywhere. Hadrat Gyani Sahib muqaddas also used to ask whether anyone had read anywhere which veil came into use and when, but it has not been found in any source. However, regarding Rasulullah, salawatullah 'alayh, it is known that the veil used for his `ghusl` (ritual bath) was different from the one that was buried. The `ghusl` veil was 'Abdullah ibn Mas'ud, and the one that was buried was 'Abdullah ibn Rawahah. The one present for `ziyarat` (visitation) is Ubayy ibn Ka'b. These are their own `hijabs` that are buried. Ubayy ibn Ka'b holds a great station — he is also among the teachers of Rasulullah. As it is said, Rasulullah himself said that Ubayy is his spiritual mother (`ummi al-ruhaniyyah`), his first teacher, who taught him all the laws and principles of Adam, alayhis salam. After that teaching, Rasulullah became `mamnun` (indebted). Similarly, five teachers taught him, and after their teaching, they became `mamnun`. After the `ba'thah`, the final teacher was Janab-i Khadijah. Before the `ba'thah` — yes, before it. Janab-i Khadijah's position is that she became `mamnun` to Rasulullah, and she not only became the teacher of Sayyidna Rasulullah but also the teacher of Janab-i 'Ali. For this reason it is said: `Ibn Maryam` (son of Mary) — who is `Ibn Maryam`? `Isa Ibn Maryam` (Jesus son of Mary) is Janab-i 'Ali, and `Maryam` is Janab-i Khadijah. For this very reason, Janab-i Maryam was exempted from the five prayers. The five prayers were exempted because, after the five prayers, Janab-i 'Ali joined — because of her being his teacher.


But I wonder — did Ubayy ibn Ka'b and all those who were `hijabs` have any awareness that they were `hijabs` in that form? Yes, absolutely. Without `ma'rifah` (gnosis) it cannot be. `Ma'rifah` is a very essential thing. Yes, they knew. And they are people of great station. Were they to manifest, they themselves would manifest as prophets and messengers. They are people of such exalted rank. But we have never seen in any incident that the Prophet taught them in any outward manner. It was given from heart to heart. Yes. To the people... Yes. They taught. I mean, in the entire thirteen years of life in Mecca, the dominance of the disbelievers and polytheists was so great. And in the ten years in Medina, most of the time was spent in battles. Yet the ethical teachings that the Prophet gave — in the way that you used to give a lesson — the Prophet, peace be upon him, in Medina, after the prayers, would sit after the Fajr prayer and would ask, "Did any of you see a dream at night?" He would ask this. And someone would say, "Yes, I saw a dream." So he would say that I am a better interpreter than Yusuf, peace be upon him. I am an interpreter. And then when he would give a lesson, in that gathering the hypocrites would also be sitting, and Muslims, and great personalities like Salman al-Farsi as well. So whatever the Prophet would say, every person would gain knowledge from it according to his own level. The Prophet would say something, and its subtleties and deeper realities would be received by whomever they were meant for. This was the beauty of the Prophet's discourse — that whoever sought, would receive.

And this Sayyidna Ubayy ibn Ka'b is the one about whom there are narrated incidents: once Rasulullah asked Ubayy ibn Ka'b, "O Ubayy, do you know which is the best verse of the Quran? You are the finest reciter (`qari`). You recite very well. You are a great `qari` and you recite the Quran abundantly. So which is the most excellent verse?" He said, "Allah and His Messenger know best." So the Prophet said, "It is Ayat al-Kursi." Rasulullah said that at that time — Ayat al-Kursi. And then, on another occasion, Rasulullah was present and Ubayy ibn Ka'b was praying — Rasulullah was likely sitting in the mihrab while Ubayy was engaged in a supererogatory prayer. So he called out, "Ya Ubayy!" Janab-i Ubayy heard the voice but continued his prayer. Then a second or third time he called again. Ubayy then quickly completed his salutations and came. So the Prophet said, "Ya Ubayy, I called you, and you did not come." He submitted, "Ya Rasulullah, I was praying." So the Prophet replied, "Who taught you this prayer? Is prayer greater, or my command and call? Is my calling not greater?" For this reason, a verse came down in the Quran — what is the verse? — that if Allah and His Messenger call you, you should answer the Messenger. A most remarkable verse: not "you should answer Allah and His Messenger," but rather "Allah and the Messenger call you, so you should answer the Messenger." So this is what was said.

`wa ta'azzaz al-maqam al-sani bi-ghayrihi fi lubnani wa-tashahad fi alf surah li-al-anam` (And the second station was magnified by other than it in its very essence, and he was witnessed in a thousand forms by mankind). This Imam is such that he can manifest in a thousand forms. `wa fi dhalika wujuh wa asbab wa li-hi al-hujjat al-ujab` (And in that are aspects and reasons, and for him is the wondrous proof). For his manifestation there are various aspects and reasons, and his affairs are very strange and wondrous. `wa amma qawluka kayfa kana qabla wujud al-samawat wa al-ardina` (As for your saying: how was it before the existence of the heavens and the earths) — so your question was: before the heavens and the earth, what was there? `kanat min al-dhawat al-nadimah` (it was from the penitent essences). So they are the `dhawat al-nadimah` (penitent essences) — `nadin dhawat` (penitent essences) — from which this came into existence. `allati usila minha ma hasula wa tarattaba khalq al-aflak allatihiya aqsam al-nadimin` (from which was derived what was obtained, and the creation of the celestial spheres was arranged, which are the categories of the penitent ones). So the categories of the `nadimin` were divided into fifteen types, and from the first type the celestial spheres (`aflak`) were made, and from these the heavens and the earth were formed. `wa amma qawluka min ayyi fudulatin kana wujud al-awwal al-jussa' fi safil al-ard` (As for your saying: from what remnants was the existence of the first particle in the lower part of the earth) — your question was from which remnants the existence of the `awwal jussa' ibda'iyyah` in the earth came about, given that the earth is wide and vast — `fa-ta'lam hadaka Allah li al-sawab wa bawwa'aka fi jami' al-asbab` (So know, may Allah guide you to what is right and settle you in all the means) — may Allah guide you toward correctness and may your station be established in all the `asbab` (means), meaning the `hudud` (ranks). `innama kana wujud al-fudulat al-sharifah illa ba'd dhuhur al-jussa' al-ibda'iyyah` (The existence of the noble remnants was only after the appearance of the originated particle). The `fudulat sharifah` (noble remnants) came into existence only after the appearance of the `jussa' ibda'iyyah`. `illa ba'd dhuhur al-jussa' al-ibda'iyyah al-dhalifah` (only after the appearance of the weak originated particle). `wa innama yuqal fudlah illa al-baqiyyah min al-jism ba'da sudur al-ruh wa al-nafs` (And it is only called a remnant — that which remains of the body after the emanation of the spirit and the soul). So `fudlah` (remnant) is what remains of the body after the departure of the `ruh` (spirit) and `nafs` (soul). `bi al-wadih min al-khitab` (with the clarity of the discourse). `wa amma wujud al-awwal al-jussa' al-ibda'iyyah allati khalafat al-nafs al-kulliyyah` (As for the existence of the first originated particle which succeeded the Universal Soul) — the `jussa' ibda'iyyah` is that which has taken the vicegerency of the `Nafs al-Kulliyyah`. The `Nafs al-Kulliyyah` means the `'Ashir` (Tenth Intellect). So the `jussa' ibda'iyyah` takes a partial vicegerency of the `'Ashir`. The full vicegerency of the `kulli` belongs to the `akhir mawr`, the last person of the `mawr`. `fa-kana min asfa aqsam al-nadimin` (So it was from the purest of the categories of the penitent ones). Among the `nadimin` (penitent ones), it is from their purest categories that the existence of the `jussa' ibda'iyyah` comes about. `allati kana tartibuha 'ala khamsata 'ashara darajatan fi al-ma'alim` (whose arrangement was upon fifteen degrees in the realms). These `nadimin` have fifteen categories. `wa asfa man habata wa ashrafa man hafaw wa ta'bata` (And the purest of those who descended and the noblest of those who stumbled and were trampled). Those who fell are the most pure, and those who slipped and were trampled are the most noble among them. The `nadimin` also made a mistake — that is, the `'Ashir` first gave the command: I have acknowledged my mistake, so you also accept your mistake and repent. Everyone refused at first, saying `la nasma'u wa la nuti'` (we do not hear and we do not obey). Then a second command came to guide them once more. He guided them and said that if you do not obey, I will remove you from here, from this realm. At that point, the `musirrin` (the insistent ones) remained on their stubbornness, and so he removed them. The `mutahayyirin` (the bewildered ones), those in a state of confusion about what they should do, were also removed. And while falling, the `nadimin` (penitent ones) repented — they repented even as they fell. Yet even in that falling, fifteen categories were formed. We believers are in the fifteenth category. Of the fifteenth category, it is also said that we are in the tenth division — each category having ten divisions. So the believers of the `sitr` are in the tenth division: the last of the last. From this alone, one can begin to estimate our station.

Sir, what is the reason for the believers' falling? I mean, to fall somewhere means one goes down — so to what extent can one descend? In the prayer we recite on the third night, we mention the `darkat` (levels of hell) and seek refuge from them — up to `atiran`, in `du'a yazul qadr` — `damad`, the garment of `damad` — up to there, the garment of `damad` is the last, the very last. The seven `darkat` within it, the last `darkat`, from which lava comes out. Yes.

Thank you. Sir, the believers who come later and enter — No, no — are they not in this category? The one who comes later — No — the voice is coming — he is not in that category. But here, guidance is received from the Imam or from his `hudud`, and so the lower forms ascend. The lower forms — the forms of the disbelievers and polytheists — ascend and move forward. Just as there are `'uyafa'`, as Sayyidna Muhammad bin Tahir has mentioned in `Anwar al-Latifah`: `'uyafa'` are noble souls who love the Ahl al-Bayt — whether they are non-Muslims, or Muslims who love the Ahl al-Bayt and abide by the shari'ah, who are mindful of their halal and haram — they love the Ahl al-Bayt and are thus `'uyafa'`, and they gradually ascend and enter the fold of Iman. What I want to say is that whether one is a disbeliever or a non-Muslim, when they come and accept Islam, they will not be in those fifteen categories. No, they are not. Sir, Janab Salman al-Farsi was first of another religion — so was he not in that category? A question arises. No, his reality is different. He was already... I am simply saying his reality is that he was already from the `hudud`. Correct, but outwardly he was of another religion, then he came here. So a history, a biography (`sirah`) is formed. Yes. And before that, he already was. And... what was I going to say... the disbelievers do not ascend until they accept Islam. They will not enter directly. Because `shirkun zulmun 'azim` (`shirk` is a great wrong). `Shirk` is a great wrong. And Allah Ta'ala forgives all sins except `shirk` — He does not forgive `shirk`. This is in the Quran as well.
