Yet the Quran also says `Innallaha yaghfiru al-dhunuba jami'an` (Indeed, Allah forgives all sins). Allah Ta'ala forgives all sins — but He does not forgive `shirk`, because `shirk` is a great wrong. Allah knows when they will turn back. Absolutely. For this very reason it is said, is it not, in Surah Yasin: `dhalika taqdir al-'aziz al-'alim` (That is the determination of the Exalted in Might, the Knowing). By `al-'aziz al-'alim` is meant the `'Ashir` (Tenth Intellect). Because the `'Ashir` knows how many `mawrs` (cycles) it will take for this troop to be purified, and in every `mawr` how many `dawrs` (periods) there will be, and in every `dawr` how many Imams will come. He holds the entire map within his awareness. His station is great and vast. And who was he? He was our companion — at one time, he was our companion. From what an exalted station we have fallen, one can begin to estimate from this: the `'Ashir` was a soul, an intellect, among us. Yet we, by not obeying, by reason of the `wahm al-musawat` (illusion of equality), brought this upon ourselves. `Wahm al-musawat` is such a great affliction. Because its first principle is this: no person can occupy the same station as another. Two people cannot sit at one station. Even if there are two Imams — like Hasan and Husayn: `Imama haqqin qama aw qa'ada` (Two true Imams, whether they stand or sit) — both are Imams, but their ranks are different. Similarly, if there are two `da'is`, like Sayyidna Gulam Husayn Sahib and Sayyidna Husayn bin Ahmad — at one time there were two `da'is` — or even two `ma'dhuns`, their ranks will differ. In one rank, no two persons can sit; every person's rank is distinct. He did not accept this reality. The `'Ashir`, who was the third intellect and who later became the `'Ashir`, did not accept it — which is why the `wahm al-musawat` took hold, the notion that I and the `Mumba'ith al-Awwal` (First Emanation) are of the same rank. Whereas the gaze of the `Mumba'ith al-Awwal` was upon the `surah 'ilmiyyah` (intellectual form) of the First Intellect, and his gaze was upon the `surah 'amaliyyah` (practical form) of the First Intellect. From this a difference was created: `nazar-i ashraf` (the nobler gaze) and `nazar-i adwan` (the lower gaze).

O Allah, send blessings and peace upon the Fatiha to the soul of Sayyidna 'Ali ibn Hibatullah, may his soul be sanctified and his grave be illuminated, and may its blessings and support be multiplied upon us. Accept from us, O Allah, the benefit and the benefiting, for the sake of Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, peace and blessings be upon them. Al-Fatiha. Bismillah…

Sir, what is the reason for the believers' falling? I mean, to fall somewhere means one goes down — so to what extent can one descend? In the prayer we recite on the third night, we mention the `darkat` (levels of hell) and seek refuge from them — up to `atiran`, in `du'a yazul qadr` — `damad`, the garment of `damad` — up to there, the garment of `damad` is the last, the very last. The seven `darkat` within it, the last `darkat`, from which lava comes out. Yes. Thank you. Alhamdulillah.

<!-- lecture 4 -->


<!-- page N --> markers are not present in this input window's prose body (they were omitted from the provided content). I will refine the prose faithfully, preserving all transliterations, citations, Arabic phrases, and inline structural markers exactly as they appear, and omit the context-overlap block.

And from Imam Ahmad bin Yusuf on the *falak al-mustaqeem* (the straight sphere), the *muheet* (the encompassing one), and `wa anna noor al-shamsi min noor al-'azeem` (and that the light of the sun is from the magnificent light). This is the second question: the questioner states that he benefited (*istifadah*) from his *sayyid* (master), from his *madadgaar* (helper), and from his Imam — meaning the one upon whom he relies — Ahmad bin Yusuf. In that era, he must have been one of the *hudood* (ranks of the religious hierarchy). From him, he learned of the *falak-e-mustaqeem* (the straight sphere), meaning the *falak-e-muheet* (the encompassing sphere), which is the highest sphere and is *ghayr-mar'i* (invisible). It is after this *falak-e-mustaqeem* that the *'alam-e-ruhani* (spiritual world) begins. And `wa anna noor al-shamsi min noor al-'azeem` (and that the light of the sun is from the magnificent light) — the light of the sun derives from its light, meaning from the light of the *falak-e-mustaqeem* or *falak-e-muheet*.

And from the Imam: `wa ma ya'oodu amr al-falak al-mustaqeem` (and what returns of the affair of the straight sphere) — what will be the state of the *falak-e-mustaqeem* `'inda irtiqa' al-rubaataat` (at the time of the ascension of the *rubaataat*)? That is, at the end of the *kawr* (cycle), when all things come to their conclusion and the skies fall — what will be the state of the *falak-e-mustaqeem* at that time? `Wa ayna yaseeru` (And where will it go?), and where will it proceed? `Wa ayna su'ooduhu` (And where will its ascent be?), and what will its ascent be like? `Wa ma alladhi yuwasilu ilaihi al-ayimmah minhu` (And what is it that the Imams attain from it?) — what will the *ayimmah* (Imams) attain from the *falak-e-mustaqeem*? `Faqad qeela innahu yas'adu` (It has been said that it will ascend) — and it is said that `innahu yas'adu min mawdi'ihi` (it will ascend from its own place), that the *falak-e-mustaqeem* or *falak-e-muheet* will ascend from right where it is. For what is the principle? Whoever must ascend, whoever must progress, must first come to the earth and then advance. The state of the *falak-e-mustaqeem* is different in this respect: it will ascend from exactly where it stands. But this remains a question — reliance is not placed on the question itself; reliance will rest upon the answer to be given. `Wa munni 'alayya` (And bestow upon me) — do this favor upon me — `wa jud 'ala mithli man yurhamu` (and be generous to one like me who is shown mercy) — be generous to one such as me, upon whom mercy is shown. `Warham man laisa lahu rahimun siwak` (And have mercy on one who has no merciful one besides You) — have mercy, for there is no one to show him mercy besides You. `Wa la malan illa iyyaka` (And no refuge but You) — and none will treasure or benefit him besides You.

`Al-jawab 'ala dhalik al-su'al` (The answer to that question). Now it is the *da'i* (summoner) Sayyidna Hibatullah who provides the answer to this question.

[Side conversation: What was the question, actually? I didn't understand.]

It concerned the *falak-e-mustaqeem*: the concept was that it would depart from its place. And there was also the question of what the *ayimmah* would attain from it, and whether the light of the sun truly derives from the light of the *falak-e-mustaqeem*. The questioner raised all of these points.

`Aqoolu bi 'awnillah al-kabeer al-muta'al` (I say with the help of Allah, the Great, the Most High) — I say this with the help of Allah, the Great, the Most High — `wa minnati wali al-'asr wa al-zaman` (and by the grace of the Master of the Age and Time) — and by the bestowal of the Master of the Age and Time — `alayhi salawat al-raheem al-rahman` (upon him be the blessings of the Merciful, the Compassionate) — upon him be the blessings of the Merciful and the Compassionate — `wa ta'eed-e mawlaya al-a'lam` (and by the support of my most learned master) — and by the support of Mawlaya al-A'lam. By *a'lam* is meant Mawlana Hibatullah; that is his title. `Wa sirrihi al-baahir al-mu'azzam` (and by his manifest and magnificent secret) — and by his luminous and lofty secret — `anna al-falak al-mustaqeem` (that the straight sphere) — `wa anna noor al-shams min noorihi al-'azeem` (and that the light of the sun is from its magnificent light): the light of the sun derives from the magnificent light of the *falak-e-mustaqeem*. `Fa dhalika ka dhalika` (So that is as it is) — the matter you have conceived is precisely so.

`Wa la budda an yuwasiluha shay'un min anwar al-qalb fi jamee' al-masalik` (And it is necessary that something of the lights of the heart reach it in all the paths) — it is necessary that the light of the sun receive lights from the lights of the heart. By "heart" is meant the Baytullah (House of Allah) that stands opposite its heavenly counterpart, the *taamur* — from that Baytullah, lights are received by the sun. This is why the pronoun used here is feminine, *yuwasiluha* — it will receive them. `Fi jamee' al-masalik` (In all the paths) — at every moment and in every path.

`Wa qawluka ma yakoonu haal falak al-mustaqeem 'inda irtiqa' al-rubaataat` (And your saying: what will be the state of the straight sphere at the time of the ascension of the *rubaataat*?) — at the end of the *kawr*, when the final matter comes to pass, what will be the condition of the *falak-e-mustaqeem*? `Wa yas'adu min mawdi'ihi` (And it will ascend from its place) — your understanding is correct: it will ascend from its own place.

[Side conversation: It will go into the tenth *barzakh* (intermediate realm), right?]

Yes.

[Side conversation: The tenth *'arsh* (throne) that is made — it will enter that.]

Yes, he has not elaborated on this point. And it will ascend from there. `Wa yakoonu 'aqlan qa'iman bil-fi'l` (And it will become an intellect existing in actuality) — it will become an intellect that is `qa'iman bil-fi'l` (existing in actuality). Until that point, it was `qa'iman bil-quwwah` (existing in potentiality): it was helping others, sustaining the lights of the sun, and the *ayimmah* were receiving assistance from it — all of this was occurring `bil-quwwah` (in potentiality). Its state `bil-fi'l` (in actuality) will be that it ascends as an intellect.

`Fi jamee' al-haalat wa la budda... wa yakoonu lahu ruhun min noorin... wa maqamuhu ruhun... mashkoorah lahu...` (In all states and it is necessary... and it will have a spirit of light... and its station will be a spirit... acknowledged for it...) — and its station will be that of a spirit, for which the full explanation is lengthy. `Ma yuheetu biha illa al-maqaamaat al-nooraniyyah` (None can encompass it except the luminous stations) — its state cannot be encompassed except through the luminous stations, meaning the words of the *ayimmah*. `Aw man kaana min da'ina... al-mutasawwir bi suwar al-imaniyyah... al-muhawwin lil-mushkilat` (Or one who was from our *da'i*s... who are envisioned in the forms of faith... who make the difficult things easy) — or only our *da'i*, one envisioned in the forms of faith, who renders religious difficulties accessible. `Wal-'ati bil-khawariq al-'adat` (And the one who comes with extraordinary feats) — and who possesses great and extraordinary states.

`Wa laqad kashafa lana ma la yakshifuhu` (And indeed he has unveiled for us what he does not unveil) — he has unveiled for us what others do not unveil. `Wa tafaddala 'alayna ma la yuhaat` (And he has bestowed upon us what cannot be encompassed) — and he has bestowed upon us that which cannot be fully encompassed. `Wa la yukayyaf` (and cannot be described) — and whose nature cannot be described. `Jazahumullahu 'anna ma jaza ahl al-du'at` (May Allah reward them on our behalf as He has rewarded the people of the *du'at*) — may he receive the same reward from Allah that the *du'at* (summoners) receive, meaning Sayyidna Hibatullah. `Wa 'ammalana min sirrihi wa barakatihi` (And benefit us from his secret and his blessings) — may we receive benefit from his secret and his blessings. `Innahu waliyu dhalika` (Indeed, He is the master of that) — He is capable of this matter and is its master. `Ala ma hunalika` (Over what is there).

`Wa amma rooh al-imam al-noorani` (And as for the spirit of the luminous Imam) — and as for the soul of the luminous Imam — `wa shabhuhu al-qudsani... fa'lam anna dhalika min asrarillah allati la tusattaru bil-aqlaam` (and his sacred likeness... so know that that is from the secrets of Allah which cannot be written with pens) — know, then, that this matter is among the secrets of the Lord, and these are things that cannot be committed to writing with pens. `Wa dhalika anna al-maqam 'alayhi al-salat wa al-salam mata haanat nuqlatuhu ila dar al-na'eem al-muqeem, tahallala fil-qalb` (And that is because the station, upon him be prayers and peace, whenever the time of its transfer comes to the abode of bliss, the everlasting, it dissolves into the heart) — that is, when the time of the Imam's passing, *alayhis salam*, arrives and he departs to the everlasting abode of bliss, his soul dissolves into the heart — meaning into the Baytullah. From there, his ascent takes place.

[Side question: When they are buried, does something similar happen to the *mu'min* (believer)?]

Yes. The *mu'min*'s ascent also occurs through the Baytullah. His *fuzala'* (excellent parts) will ascend through it. The *surat* (form) of the *mu'min* — his form — ascends to the *hudood-e-'uliyyah* (higher ranks), while his *riyat* (subtle essences) enter the *aflak* (spheres), passing into the sun and Jupiter and so on. As for his *fuzala'*, they ascend gradually, in a state of prayer, until they reach the *mihrab* (prayer niche) where he used to pray in the mosque — to that very station where he had established his place of worship. They ascend to reach that *mihrab*. From there they proceed to the center, which is the center of his *jazirah* (island). For us, the center of the two *jazirahs* is said to be the tomb of Sayyidna Sulaiman. From there, his ascent continues through twenty-eight stations until he enters the Baytullah. And within the Baytullah there are four pillars; ascending through them, he passes to each pillar in turn and finally arrives at the *rukn-e-aswad* (Black Stone corner), from which his ultimate ascent takes place. These *fuzala'*, in their ascending state, are called `zu'ubat`. Once they have ascended, they become `zakha'ir` (treasures).

[Side question: The four pillars you mentioned…]

Yes, the *asatir*.

[Side question: The four pillars you mentioned…]

Yes, they are the *atqiyah* (pious ones).

[Another side question.]

Yes, they are present in their physical form.


And when the time of his passing comes, he finds dissolution in the *qalb* (heart). `Thumma sara mawaddan wa qiwaman li quloob al-ulama min al-hudood` (Then he becomes substance and strength for the hearts of the learned ones from the *hudood*). And after dissolving, they become substance and strength for the hearts of the learned ones who are the *hudood* — the great *hudood* of the *da'wah* (mission). Substance here means that the *hudood* of the *da'wah*, the great ones, receive help in the strength they possess: the strength of speech, the strength of benefiting others. Yes — through them. Just as for Janab Yusuf there is `lawla an ra'a burhana rabbihi` (had he not seen the proof of his Lord) — had he not seen the proof of the Lord, he would have erred in the matter that occurred with Zuleikha. So what is the proof of the Lord? It is this `mawadi` (substance) that is obtained from the Imam. Yes. `Alladheena bi hidayatihim 'urifat ta'at al-ma'bood` (Those through whose guidance the worship of the Worshipped One is known) — such that through their guidance, the worship of the Worshipped One can be performed.

`Fa idha haanat nuqlatuhum` (So when their time of transfer comes). And when the passing of these *hudood* occurs, then — `ila 'alam al-anwar... ittasala dhalika al-maddah bil-qalb al-saafi min al-akdar` (to the world of lights... that substance connects with the pure heart, free of impurities). When their passing occurs to the world of lights, `yarji'u ila aslihi` (it returns to its origin). So that substance by which their hearts were given strength — received from what dissolved in the *qalb* of the Imam — is returned. It returns to its origin at the time of passing. These were trusts they had held, so that in their decisions, in their benefiting of others, and in the matters of their lives, the *hudood* should not make mistakes and should remain safe from evils and sins. They become masters of all those states. But they receive this temporarily, not permanently. At the time of their passing, it connects and is returned to the origin — to the pure one, free of impurities.

`Wa yakoonu min ma'ooneen nafsu imam al-sharqi wal-gharbi ma madda lahu min al-qalb` (And from those helpers, the soul of the Imam of the East and the West is that which is supplied to him from the heart). From that, the soul of the Imam is formed — through the dissolution that happened in the Baytullah, the soul of the next Imam takes shape, the Imam of the East and the West. `Ma madda lahu min al-qalb` — that which is obtained for him from the heart. `Wa min al-ma' alladhi hawlahu` (And from the water that is around it). And from the water around it, it is also obtained. `Wa shay'in min al-bata'ih allati talyihi` (And a thing from the marshes that are adjacent to it). All these things are gathered for that Imam whose soul is in the process of origination.

`Wa mimma zukhira lahu mimma inhallah min awwal al-fitrah... ba'da awwal al-fatrah, ba'da tamam al-kawr al-mashhoor` (And from what was stored for him of what dissolved from the beginning of creation... after the first interval, after the completion of the well-known *kawr*). The thing that has been stored for him — dissolving from the first creation, from the first interval, after the completion of the *kawr* which is well-known — from that, the Imam's soul receives additions. From the moment the era was created, whatever treasures have been accumulating, from those the Imam's soul is enriched. Yes.

`Yajdharu minhu fa'idah... 'an idrees al-shurafa'... al-muwasilah min da'ihi... mishkat al-noor` (A benefit stems from it... for the noble ones... which is connected from his *da'i*... the niche of light). From it arises a benefit for the noble ones, connected through his *da'i*, who is the niche of light.

`Wa qad awdaha hadha al-ma'na... al-da'i... al-ajall al-'aleem hibatullah bin sayyidna ibrahim fi risalatihi al-mawsumah bi saqilat al-quloob min al-fikr, wa al-munawwirah lil-basa'ir, wa al-musfiyah lil-suwar` (And this meaning has been clarified... by the *da'i*... the most glorious, the most learned, Hibatullah bin Sayyidna Ibrahim in his treatise named *Saqilat al-Quloob min al-Fikr* [The Polisher of Hearts from Thought], and *al-Munawwirah lil-Basa'ir* [The Illuminator of Insights], and *al-Musfiyah lil-Suwar* [The Purifier of Forms]). This has been clarified by Sayyidna al-Da'i al-Ajall al-'Aleem Hibatullah bin Sayyidna Ibrahim in his treatise known as *Saqilat al-Quloob*, which illuminates insights and purifies forms.

`Fi jawab su'al al-thani 'ashar` (In the answer to the twelfth question). Sayyidna Hibatullah, in that book, in the answer to the twelfth question, has written: `Bi qawlin 'aleem al-ma'ani wa al-gharad` (With a speech of profound meanings and purpose) — authored with great and grand meanings. `Wa ma fihi abyan al-bayan` (And what is in it is the clearest of explanations) — in which there is the clearest explanation — `wa awdah al-burhan` (and the most evident proof) — and a clear proof.

`Wa mazkuruhu fi hadhihi al-mas'alah wajhayn fil-haqeeqah` (And its mention in this matter has two aspects in reality). Now this matter will be explained in two ways — that is, Sayyidna Ali is saying: I will explain to you the state of the Imam's soul. `Fi haqeeqah... fi wajhayn` (In reality... in two aspects). I will explain it in two ways. `Fi muqabalat al-shahadah` (In comparison to the *shahadah*) — in comparison to the word of the *shahadah*.

`Wa huwa anna al-asrar... allati la yattali'u 'alayha illa al-ahad min 'ibadihi` (And that is that the secrets... upon which none are informed except the unique ones from His servants). It is from the secrets which no one can know except those who have reached the rank of *ahad* (unique) from among the servants. `Wa dhalika anna fil-maqam 'alayhi al-salam... ma yuqabilu qutbayn al-shahadah fil-hukm` (And that is that in the station, upon him be peace... there is what corresponds to the two poles of the *shahadah* in its ruling). If we compare the Imam, peace be upon him, with the word of the *shahadah*, then — `li qawli asdaq al-qa'ileen... wa kullu shay'in ahsaynahu fi imamim mubeen` (according to the saying of the most truthful of speakers... "And We have enumerated all things in a clear Imam" [Qur'an 36:12]) — the most truthful of speakers says: `kullu shay'in ahsaynahu fi imamim mubeen`. We have enumerated every thing in a clear Imam. This verse appears in Surah Yasin: `Wa kullu shay'in ahsaynahu fi imamim mubeen`.

In this, two things merit reflection. First, the word `kullu shay'in` (every thing) — what is meant by `shay'`? This fan, this light, this carpet — these are also `shay'` (things), are they not? They too fall under the category of `shay'`. But there, by `shay'` is meant the form of the *mu'min* (believer). Wherever the word `shay'` has been used by Allah Ta'ala, it refers to the *mu'min* and the form of the noble *mu'min*. For example, `kullu shay'in ahsaynahu fi imamim mubeen`, or `innallaha 'ala kulli shay'in qadeer` (Indeed, Allah has power over all things), `wa innallaha bi kulli shay'in muheet` (and indeed, Allah encompasses all things), or `innallaha bi kulli shay'in 'aleem` (indeed, Allah is knowledgeable of all things) — these usages convey the same meaning. By `shay'` is meant the *mu'min* or the form of the *mu'min*.

The second point concerns `ahsayna` (We have enumerated). In `ahsayna`, what is significant is that `jama'na` (We have gathered) was not said. In gathering, things become jumbled. In `ahsayna`, each form retains its separate nature and its state remains distinct — meaning, despite being gathered, they retain their own individuality and can be separated out. As the Imams did: with their forms, with a single form, they made a manifestation. By what reason did this occur? Because if they had been gathered (*jama'*), they could not have been separated. But they were *ahsa* — enumerated in such a nature that the individuality of each remains in its own place.

[Side question: In this, the Imam cannot be encompassed except by the Imam. So does that mean the Imam can also do it through his own power? That enclosure — that everything is as it is, except by the power of the Imam — what does that mean?]

This is it, is it not? `Kullu shay'in ahsaynahu fi imamim mubeen`.

[Side comment: This is, of course, the Imam, but the Imam also does not come within its scope.]

Everything will come into it.

[Side comment: I will check it and then say.]

`Wa dhalika anna al-maqam fih nasut wa lahut` (And that is because the station has in it humanity and divinity). Now the comparison is being drawn with the word of the *shahadah*. In the Imam there are *nasut* (humanity) and *lahut* (divinity). *Nasut* is the collection of *riyat* (subtle essences). When a *mu'min* passes away, the *riyat* that emerge immediately after his passing are the higher *riyat*. The *riyat* that emerge after burial are the lower *riyat*. From the higher *riyat* that emerge — those discharged after the passing but before burial — the *nasut-e-zilli* (shadowy human forms) of the *nawasi* (successors) are made. And from the *riyat* that emerge after burial, which emerge only for three days — after three days what remains are vapors — the *nasut-e-tabi'i* (natural human forms) are made. Yes.

[Side question: The ones that come out first are more excellent?]

Yes, the ones that come out first are more excellent. There is also a tradition that the one who passes away should be buried as soon as possible — this is the guidance of the Messenger, that the deceased should not be kept for too long but buried promptly. Yes.

So these *riyat* correspond to *nasut* and *lahut*, and correspond to the negation and affirmation of the word of the *shahadah*. The *riyat* do not immediately become *nasut* — they must go through a process. Those *riyat* travel to the light of the moon, which draws them; then they go to Jupiter; then from Jupiter they go to the sun; then from there they return to Jupiter again; and their principal station is the moon. The shining that occurs in the sky on the 13th, 14th, and 15th nights is said to happen because of these *riyat* — their congregation becomes very large during those three nights, which is why that luminosity occurs.

Now, for the one who has passed away, the *riyat* that emerge over three days are giving a notice in a spiritual sense to the relatives: whatever you have to do in these three days, do it — whether giving charity, performing good deeds, hosting meals, reciting *sadaqallah*, holding *fatiha khwani* or *beeswa khwani*, arranging for Quran recitation, or giving in charity. Do it within these three days. After that, whatever you do will be for the noble form and for the *fuzala'* — I, the departed, will have already left and will not return for a thousand years. Yes. This is the state of the *riyat*.

The purified *riyat* then descend like drops of dew and settle on certain fruits — uncooked fruits such as apples and similar things. The Imam and the Imam's wife eat those fruits, and from that, conception takes place, and from that the *Farzand-e-Imam* (Child of the Imam) is born. As soon as the *Farzand-e-Imam* is born, the nourishment he takes is entirely *riyat*. He appears to be drinking milk or taking ordinary nourishment, but in reality he is receiving *riyat*.

One incident occurred in this regard. Mufaddal bin 'Umar — who was the *bab al-abwab* (gate of gates) of Imam Ja'far al-Sadiq — came to Imam Ja'far al-Sadiq's presence while the Imam was eating. The Imam said, "Come, join us in the meal." Mufaddal said, "Mawlana, I have just eaten." The Imam replied: "What you ate, and what we are eating — you have not eaten what we are eating. And what you ate, we do not eat." The indication was: what you eat are the external nourishments that Allah has created, while what we eat are *riyat* — and in that you should join us.

The *mu'min* also receives *riyat*. When does the *mu'min* receive them? During his *wudu* (ablution). As soon as he says Bismillah, and the breath he draws, and the water that touches his body and enters through the pores — these carry the *fuzala'* and *riyat* that the *mu'min* receives. This is one way. That is why it is said that *isbagh al-wudu* (performing *wudu* completely) is of great status. As has been stated in the traditions, some things become atonements for major sins, and among them is *isbagh al-wudu* `fil-burudat` — or `sabarat` as it is recorded in one place — meaning that he performs *wudu* with piety in extreme cold. Or *burudat* does not mean only that the weather is very cold — it also refers to the coldness of temperament, the inner reluctance when one's disposition is not inclined toward *wudu*, when there is laziness within. Performing *wudu* with completeness in such inner coldness — this is the inner meaning. In the outward sense it also means the time of cold weather; and in the inner sense, when there is coldness of temperament, doing it completely even then.

[Side question: The *riyat* that are received — are they for the common *mu'min* also?]

Yes.

[Side question: For the *mu'min* also?]

Yes.

[Side question: Whose *riyat* are these?]

The *mu'min*'s own *riyat* are received by him. The purified *riyat* that are ascending come into the *mu'min*'s share. Yes. And those *riyat* are in the moon. After emerging, they are gathered in the moon, and from there they are connected to the one who performs *wudu* and so on.

That is why it has been said in a tradition that after *wudu*, whatever water remains on the hand should be shaken off — for from every drop that is shaken off, an angel is created who will pray for one's forgiveness. Yes. And it has also been said that after *wudu* one should recite *salawat* (blessings) and Surah al-Qadr, `inna anzalna`, and then pray — that by doing so, one's prayer will not be rejected. This too has been mentioned among the blessings of *wudu*.

The second occasion on which the *mu'min* receives nourishment from the *riyat* is at the time of *iftar* (breaking fast) and *sahri* (pre-dawn meal). That is why it has been said that *sahri* should be taken at the last possible moment — not that one wakes at two in the night and eats, but that *sahri* is had as late as possible. And that the fast should be broken promptly — without waiting to first pray and then break the fast, but hurrying to break it. Because what is being received at those moments is of a very high nature, and one should hasten to obtain it. Yes.

[Side question: The *riyat* that are received — do they become part of the body?]

Yes, they become part of the body. They ascend. Yes. The precise details of how they become part of the body — whether they go to the heart or elsewhere in the body — I have not read. But they enter the body, as water enters through the pores or through respiration. As soon as one says Bismillah, the breath one draws after that — all of it carries nourishment from the *riyat*.

[Side question: Are they his own *riyat*?]

No, not his own *riyat* — he has not passed away yet. These are the *riyat* of the believers. The purified *riyat* of the believers that are gathered in the moon — from there, these things are obtained by him.


Now, once… whom did we visit in Iran? Who was he?

[Side comment: Avicenna.]

Avicenna. So, Ali ibn Sina — he was a great *da'i*, a very great *hujjat* (proof). With him was a student who was very dear to him. That student used to say to him: if you were to claim prophethood, people would not deny you. You are so great, and you have mastery over everything. What field of knowledge is there that you do not command? You have mastery over medicine, over everything. So he would smile and give no answer. Once he was traveling with this student in Iran during a time of snowfall. While traveling, they reached a certain place and saw that it was the time of *maghrib* (the evening prayer). From a distance, they saw a person — the snow had settled — and he was breaking through the snow to make water, so that he could perform *wudu* with it. Both of them watched this scene. Then Avicenna said to that student: you always used to ask me why I do not claim prophethood — that if I were to claim it, people would not deny me. Today I give you the answer. Three hundred years have passed since the passing of the Messenger, may Allah's blessings be upon him, and yet in his *shari'ah* (sacred law) there is such an effect — upon his very soul — that today this person, without caring for his own life, is breaking the snow and performing *wudu*.

And before that, the night prior, these two were staying at a traveler's inn. When they went to sleep that night, the master became thirsty. Avicenna became thirsty. He said to the student, "Please give me some water." The water pot was kept in the veranda — one had to go outside to fetch it. The student said, "Mawlana, it is very cold. You can drink the water in the morning." So the next day Avicenna mentioned this: you were saying that I should make such a claim — yet my own state is such that I could not get my dear student to rise and fetch me water. And this person, three hundred years later, following the *shari'ah* of the Messenger, is breaking the snow and performing *wudu*. That was the state of it.

So, *nasut* and *lahut* stand in relation to the negation and affirmation within the word of the *shahadah*. Just as negation and affirmation — `li 'izzat wal-jabarut` (for the Almighty and the All-Powerful) — belong to Allah Ta'ala, the possessor of might and power. `Wa lil-imam 'alayhi al-salam thalathat hujub` (And for the Imam, peace be upon him, there are three veils). The Imam, peace be upon him, has three veils. Fundamentally there are three, though they also make use of different veils. `Mathalu thalathat jawahir al-nizam` (The example of the three jewels of the order). Just as in the word of the *shahadah* there are three jewels — *alif*, *lam*, *ha* — so likewise the Imam has three veils. These point toward the three *nasut*: *nasut-e-tabi'i* (the natural human form), *nasut-e-zilli* (the shadowy human form), and *nasut-e-khas* (the special human form). `Wa kullu imam min mawlana husayn jama'a rutub al-arba'ah` (And every Imam from our master Husayn has combined the four ranks). Every Imam from Imam Husayn onward has combined the four ranks.

As for what happened with Imam Hasan — Imam Hasan had two ranks, and Imam Husayn had two ranks. In Imam Hasan's time, the two higher ranks were with Imam Hasan, and therefore he was the Imam. Whoever holds the higher ranks is the Imam. This is what occurred in the past as well: in the progeny of Sayyidna Ibrahim, whoever held the two higher ranks was the Imam of the time. Qaydar was there, Mawlana Isma'il was there, and the other Imams of those ages — all of them held the two higher ranks. The prophets of their time held the two lower ranks and were entrusted with the mission. But after Imam Husayn — after the passing of Imam Hasan — when the four ranks were combined, those four ranks did not get divided again. They were first gathered with Imam Husayn. But…

[Phone rings and short interruption]

Go ahead and reconnect.

[Interruption continues]

Yes…

[Side conversation: Does it take a forty-minute break?]

Yes.

[Interruption ends]

Are you back?

[Response: Yes, I'm back. We were talking about the three veils.]

Yes. So the three veils are *tabi'i*, *zilli*, and *khas*. And the discussion was that with Mawlana Husayn, when the four ranks were combined, then after that all subsequent Imams would have the four ranks combined. Before this, Imam Hasan had two ranks — of Imamate and succession — by virtue of which he was called the Imam. And the two lower ranks were with Imam Husayn, by virtue of which he was under the authority of Imam Hasan. In the past, too, from the progeny of Mawlana Isma'il, from the progeny of Sayyidna Ibrahim — there as well, Sayyidna Isma'il held the two higher ranks, while the prophet of that time, Is'haq, held the two lower ranks. That pattern continued through history.

But here, after Imam Husayn, when the four ranks were combined, those four ranks remained with all subsequent Imams. Yet it is also a fact that the first Imam with whom the four ranks were fully combined was Mawlana Ali Zayn al-'Abidin — because even with Imam Husayn, the two lower ranks had dominated over the higher ones. He, too, was subject to that dominance. But once the four ranks were combined with Mawlana Ali Zayn al-'Abidin, his station became elevated above all previous Imams. That is why he is called the *Adam-e-Aal-e-Aba* (the Adam of the Prophet's family) — just as Adam was the first of his kind, so Mawlana Ali Zayn al-'Abidin became the Adam of the Ahl al-Bayt (the People of the House).

[Side question: The Messenger also had the four ranks, did he not?]

[Reply:] The Messenger also had the four ranks.

[Side question: And they were given as a trust to Mawlana Ali.]

[Reply:] Yes. With Sayyidna Rasulullah, what happened was this: the two higher ranks were also gathered with him, but they were held as a trust (*amanat*). After Mawlana Abd al-Muttalib, the four ranks that had been with him were such that he gave the two higher ranks to Mawlana Abu Talib, while Sayyidna Abdullah had already passed away. A grandfather may bequeath to a grandson — so those two remaining ranks were kept as a trust with Sayyidna Abu Talib, on behalf of Sayyidna Rasulullah. Then, when the time came for the appointment of Sayyidna Ali as Imam, Sayyidna Abu Talib gave all four ranks to Sayyidna Rasulullah. The two ranks of mission and prophethood had been coming down from Sayyidna Abd al-Muttalib and were rightfully for Sayyidna Rasul. But the two ranks of Imamate — those were given to Sayyidna Rasulullah as a trust, that these are for Ali. For Ali. And so: `man kuntu mawlahu fa hadha 'aliyyun mawlahu` (Of whomsoever I am the master, Ali is his master). Whomsoever I was the master of — meaning, of these ranks — Ali is his master. That is the meaning.

`Wa mathalu arba'at rutub... al-muqatta'at al-arba'ah fil-kalimat al-shahadah: La, ilaha, illa, Allah` (And the example of the four ranks is the four sections in the word of the *shahadah*: La, ilaha, illa, Allah). `Wa istirahat al-maqam fis-sabi' min al-ayimmah` (And the resting of the station is in the seventh of the Imams). The seventh Imam is called the station of rest — with him, the accomplishments of the six preceding Imams are gathered, and so it is a station of rest. A restful station, where there is nothing further to add. The seventh becomes the *jami'* (the gatherer). `Min al-ayimmah al-qa'im... li man sabaqahu min al-ruh` (From the Imams, the *qa'im* (one who rises)… for those who preceded him of the spirit). For the past Imams, he becomes a station of the spirit. `Mathalu sab'at fusul fil-shahadah` (The example of the seven sections in the *shahadah*). There are seven sections in the word of the *shahadah* — when it is written in the Arabic manner, there are seven places where the pen lifts from the surface of the paper. Those seven liftings of the pen are an indication of the station of the seventh, the *sabi'*, who is the *jami'* — his rank is very high.

`Wal-imam majma' suwar ithna 'ashar jazirah bil-akhir` (And the Imam is the confluence of the forms of the twelve islands in the end). `Mathalu ithna 'ashar harfan fil-shahadah al-kabirah` (The example of the twelve letters in the great *shahadah*). The Imam is the confluence — the forms of the twelve islands, the twelve *du'at* (summoners), converge with the Imam at the end. Just as there are twelve letters in the word of the great *shahadah*. `Wa jamee' dhalika... thamaniah wa 'ishruna... suratan haddan... nizam al-'alam al-'ala sarmada` (And the total of all of that is twenty-eight forms as a limit… the order of the higher world, eternally). When we add them — two and three and four and seven and twelve — they become twenty-eight, which is the *nizam al-'alam al-'ala* (the order of the higher world), eternal. That is a discussion unto itself.

[Side question: There was mention of *falak-e-muheet*, which ascends without coming to the earth. Does it go and meet the *barzakh-e-'ashir*?]

[Reply:] That has not been mentioned, or I have not read it.

[Side comment: Because there is a verse of the Quran — `yanfudhuna la tanfudhuna illa bi sultan` (they pass through; they cannot pass through except with an authority). So whatever is ascending waits in the *barzakh-e-'ashir* until the authority comes.]

[Reply:] Yes, yes. So it is deduced from that verse that the same applies to *falak-e-'ashir*. It is not stated explicitly in the text, but the deduction leads there.

Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. We make this reading of the Fatiha a gift, a means of connection — lofty, pure, and pleasing — to the soul of Sayyidna Ali ibn Hibatullah, may Allah sanctify his soul, may his grace be upon us, and may Allah increase us in his blessings. O Allah, accept from us this benefit and the benefiting from it, for the sake of what is contained within it, for the sake of Sayyidna Muhammad and his progeny, upon them be peace. Al-Fatiha. Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi rabb al-'alameen. Ar-rahman ar-raheem. Maliki yawm al-din. Iyyaka na'budu wa iyyaka nasta'in. Ihdina al-sirat al-mustaqim. Sirat alladhina an'amta 'alayhim ghayr al-maghdubi 'alayhim wa la al-dallin. For the sake of Sayyidna al-Da'i al-Mutlaq, the chief of the two *da'wahs*, the *zill* (shadow) of the *hadd* of Sindh, the honor of the noble, the pride of the Arabs and non-Arabs, our master Sayyidna Muhammad Burhanuddin, may Allah sanctify his soul and his sacred mausoleum, may Allah elevate his station, protect him and keep him safe, help him and heal him from all diseases, pains, and calamities, increase his life, grant him strength, protect him with the divine protection, the magnificent — may Allah prolong his life, may his sacred station and ranks be elevated, and may He grant us and you the visit to his noble presence, and may we and you be with his blessings. For the sake of Sayyidna al-Da'i al-Mutlaq, Mawlana Mufaddal Saifuddin, may Allah prolong his noble life. Al-Fatiha. Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi rabb al-'alameen. Ar-rahman al-raheem. Maliki yawm al-din. Iyyaka na'budu wa iyyaka nasta'in. Ihdina al-sirat al-mustaqim. Sirat alladhina an'amta 'alayhim ghayr al-maghdubi 'alayhim wa la al-dallin.

<!-- lecture 5 -->


...that you do not make it, that your hands are tied to your neck. Nor should you open it completely — for if you do, you will be blamed and regretful. You will regret, thinking: "What have I done?" This is what the image of the hand upon the neck means. Do not let your hands be bound to your neck, and do not open them utterly wide. A parallel verse appears in Surah al-Furqan, in the passage concerning the `'Ibad al-Rahman` (the servants of the Most Merciful): *Walladhina idha anfaqu lam yusrifu wa lam yaqturu wa kana bayna dhalika qawama* — "And those who, when they spend, are neither extravagant nor miserly, and there is ever a just middle way between them." The meaning is the same as here in Surah Bani Isra'il: do not become so miserly that your hand never reaches into your pocket, and do not become so lavish in generosity that you leave nothing for your family or yourself, falling into blame and regret. The `'Ibad al-Rahman` are praised precisely because when they spend, they neither waste nor withhold; their conduct is the middle path. *Khayr al-umuri awsatuha* — the best of matters are those that occupy the middle.

Does this not create a tension, however? For we have been taught that the quality of the Prophet and the noble Imams was to give everything in charity. There is the well-known incident of the Prophet returning from a journey, having distributed all his provisions along the way, so that when he arrived home there was nothing left. That state belongs to the `Awliya' Allah` (Friends of God), the Rasulullah, and the `ma'sumin` (the infallible ones). Consider what once occurred with Amir al-Mu'minin: a wealthy man came to him with his two sons. The elder son spent freely — on others and on himself — and lived well. The younger son, however, took no pleasure in life, citing the example of Amir al-Mu'minin himself, whose clothing was coarse, whose food was spare and harsh. Amir al-Mu'minin replied that they should not take his example for their own lives, but rather should benefit from the blessings Allah had given them. He affirmed that what the elder son was doing was correct: when Allah has granted you blessings, you should make use of those blessings.

This is an established narration. There are many incidents of Mawlana 'Ali performing manual labour and giving away the earnings along the road, arriving home with nothing. The same was the state of the Prophet. In the incident being recalled, Sayyida Khadijah had already passed away. The Prophet would slaughter sheep often and in great numbers. He left his house with a goat, slaughtered it, and distributed it. When he returned, only one leg remained. Sayyida 'A'ishah said: *Ma baqiya illa hadha* — "Nothing is left but this. You have given everything in charity and alms." He replied in the same register, and his reply was most eloquent: *Kullun baqiya illa hadha* — "Everything remains, except this." What is given away endures; only what is kept is truly lost.

As for 'A'ishah and Hafsah and the troubles they caused the Prophet, this reached such a degree that a Quranic verse was revealed concerning them, in Surah al-Tahrim: *Laqad saghat qulubuhuma* — "Your hearts have indeed inclined [away from the right]." Their hearts had become crooked. Allah declared that He would not accept their repentance — even though Allah is *Tawwab al-Rahim*, the Acceptor of repentance, the Most Merciful. And if they were to come in opposition, then Allah was sufficient, and Jibril was sufficient, and the *Salih al-Mu'minin* — the righteous believers — and by this is meant 'Ali. *La tuzara hidayatuhu*.

<!-- page 22 -->

Up to this point we had read yesterday. Now: *Wa fi wajhin akhar* — "And in another aspect" — the 22nd page. In this second reason, *yuniru min ahl al-basa'ir al-suwar* — it illuminates the forms of the people of insight. *Fa-llahu ta'ala alima ma hadhihi sabila min hadha al-sirr al-ghamiz wa-l-ma'na* — "So Allah the Exalted knows what this path is of this obscure secret and its hidden meaning." And there is the Quranic verse: *Wa ja'alna al-bayta mathabatan li-l-nasi wa amna* — "And We made the House a place of resort for the people and a place of security." Two qualities are assigned to the Baytullah: it is a place of reward and `thawab` (divine recompense), and it is a place of `amn` (security).

*Wa fi al-qalb mithlu ma fi al-shahadah al-mu'addiyah li-qa'iliha ila dar al-sa'adah* — "And in the heart is the like of what is in the testimony of faith, which leads its speaker to the abode of felicity." In the heart resides that which is in the `shahadah` (testimony of faith) — the very thing that leads the one who says *La ilaha ill-Allah* toward eternal felicity. *Wa dhalika anna al-qalb al-haqiqi al-mutala'li' bi-l-nur huwa tahta hadha al-bayt al-ma'mur* — "And that is because the true heart, gleaming with light, lies beneath this Frequented House." The `qalb al-haqiqi` (true heart) is beneath the outer house that we circumambulate. *Alladhi hadha lahu ka-l-hijab wa huwa hakhadha ka-l-muhtajib* — "For which this outer house is like a veil, and that which lies beneath is like the veiled one." The outer house we circumambulate is the veil; what lies beneath is the veiled reality. *Fi jami' al-asbab, mithlu al-nafi wa-l-ithbat* — "In all the means, like the negation and the affirmation." Just as the `kalimat al-shahadah` (word of testimony) contains negation and affirmation, so too the Bait al-Ma'mur and the `bayt al-haqiqi` (true heart) exemplify that same pairing of negation and affirmation. In another passage it is stated that opposite the Bait al-Ma'mur there is a `qalb`, a Baytullah facing it — the outer house being the veil, and that inner reality being the veiled one.

*Al-shahadah allati man qalaha mukhlisan dakhala al-jannah* — "The testimony of faith, which whoever says with sincerity enters Paradise." This calls to mind an occasion during Hajj — during 'Umrah — when there was no space upstairs in the Masjid al-Haram, so prayer was offered in the lower level. Just as one may validly pray above the level of the Ka'bah, one may also validly pray below it. We are not within the Ka'bah itself, nor in a basement of the Ka'bah as such; rather, we are below its surface level — and this is permissible on either side, above or below.

*Allati u'iddat li-lladhina ahsanu al-husna wa ziyadah* — "Which is prepared for those who do good; for them is the best reward and an increase." Such is the Paradise prepared for those who did good deeds and increased in them. What constitutes a good deed? Amir al-Mu'minin says: *Al-hasanatu hubbuna wa-l-sayyi'atu bughduna* — "The good deed is our love, and the evil deed is hatred of us" — that is, love and hatred of the Ahl al-Bayt (People of the House). The Quranic verse *li-lladhina ahsanu al-husna wa ziyadah* thus points to those who, in this `karrah` (cycle of existence), took the `'ahd al-sharif` (noble covenant) and thereafter increased in their faith. In the next `karrah`, whatever rank they held, they will be granted a rank beyond it. *Li-lladhina ahsanu al-husna wa ziyadah* — for those who did good is the best reward and an increase. Their ranks will rise from one `karrah` to the next.

In another context it is said that a believer, at whatever rank he departs this life, it is incumbent upon the power of the Creator — *Bari' zubanuhu qudratuhu* — to bring him back to that rank in the next `karrah`, by one means or another. This is why we observe that some people progress rapidly and leap forward, apparently from far behind. The reason is that in their previous `karrah` they were already established at that rank; their journey now proceeds from that rank onward. An elder once had occasion to grant `fursat` (an appointment) to someone, and one of the `hudad al-da'wah` (ranks of the mission) remarked: "Mawlana, we did not think you would give him `fursat`." The reply was: "We do not grant `fursat` on the basis of present merit alone, but on the basis that the recipient may complete his `hasanat` (good deeds) and virtues." They were already established at that rank from a previous `karrah`; it was therefore incumbent to bring them to that place. Whatever progress follows thereafter is achieved through their own knowledge and action.

*Wa fi al-qalb thalatha tajwifat, mithlu al-thalathah al-jawahir* — "And in the heart are three cavities, like the three jewels." A person often does not himself perceive at what rank he is established. It is said that among the ranks surrounding the Da'i are many `hudad` of very high station who are not yet in a condition to be brought from potentiality to actuality. By way of illustration: among those appointed who have the first grave in Baroda, the small mausoleum of Mulla Karam 'Ali Sahab — in the time of Sayyidna Hibatullah Sahab, to whom these were appointed, the affair of Da'ud ibn Fi had occurred. Sayyidna Sahab wrote a treatise in which he clarified the rank-list of the `hudad`, and produced a formal register. Da'ud ibn Fi had claimed to be superior in knowledge and rank to the Da'i. He later repented, travelled to Najran, was forgiven by Sayyidna Sahab, and died as a believer — yet his rank was held at the level of a believer. Sayyidna Hibatullah bin Ibrahim Sahab compiled his register and placed Sayyidna Mulla Karamullah Sahab — who was the Da'i, or `mansub`, of two `jazirah` (islands), that is, Hind and Sindh — at the twenty-eighth position. Twenty-seven persons were superior to him in that list. The eighth position was held by Sayyidna Isma'il bin Hibatullah Sahab. And the very first position was Sayyidna 'Ali bin Hiba's — the author of the very book we are reading — upon whom `nass sharif` (noble designation) had been performed. But he passed away. So Sayyidna Sahab, passing over the seven above him, performed `nass sharif` upon the eighth: Sayyidna Isma'il bin Hibatullah Sahab.

Such things occur. In the time of Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan, *qaddasa Allahu ruhahu*, it happened that Sayyidna 'Ali bin Husayn al-Mukarrami, who was the `sahib al-waqt` (master of the time), was unable to attend the Friday gathering for some reason. The mosque was full. He sent his Abyssinian servant, Rayhan, with the message that Husayn bin Hasan would lead the prayer. But there were fifty men named Husayn bin Hasan in attendance. People asked Rayhan which one was meant. Rayhan returned to Sayyidna Sahab, who replied in a loud voice with the full lineage: "Husayn bin Hasan bin 'Abdullah bin Ahmad al-Mukarrami" — and recited it for ten generations. Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan was seated at the eighth or tenth position, with five `manasib` (high-ranking officials) before him, including the `mansub` of Yemen and Najran — Sayyidna Muhammad bin Jamali and the father of Sayyidna Ahmad bin Jamali. All the `manasib` were present. Yet Sayyidna 'Ali bin Husayn chose Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan to lead the prayer. When Sayyidna 'Ali bin Husayn later came and brought Sayyidna 'Abdullah ibn Hamid with him, announcing to all who were being taught by the `hudad` that he was departing for Hindustan and that Husayn bin Hasan would look after all affairs in his absence — the community understood this to be a `nass sharif`. It also became clear that Sayyidna 'Abdullah bin Hamid, who was being taken along, would not become the Da'i afterward, because it is a principle of the mission that the Da'i of the three `jazirah` cannot leave the `jazirah` of Yemen — which is the `qalb` (heart) — without leaving his `na'ib al-maqam` (deputy) there. Since Sayyidna 'Ali bin Husayn left that station and took Sayyidna 'Abdullah bin Hamid with him, and appointed Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan in that place, the meaning was plain.

On the matter of what lies beneath the Baytullah: there came a mention in another passage that beneath the basement of the Baytullah is the `sakhrah` (the sacred rock), which is a distinct and separate matter — not directly in the basement. In the `sakhrah` there are ten `abrar` (righteous ones). The last among these ten is Nimrud — he who claimed divinity, saying *Ana Rabbukum al-A'la* (I am your Lord, the Most High).

*Wa fi al-ithna 'ashar al-jazirah, ithna 'ashar qalban sharifan, mithlu al-ithna 'ashar harfan fi al-shahadah* — "And in the twelve islands are twelve noble hearts, like the twelve letters in the testimony of faith." The twelve `jazirah` each correspond to a `qalb` (heart); every `jazirah` is itself a `qalb`. And this mirrors the twelve letters of *La ilaha ill-Allah*. *Min qawlihi al-jamil, jami' dhalika thamaniah wa 'ishruna 'addan* — "From his beautiful saying, the total of all this is twenty-eight in number." *Li-l-diyanah wa-l-huda. Fa-sun hadha al-sirr al-masun* — "For the religion and the guidance. So preserve this guarded secret." The number twenty-eight is always maintained. Among the `hudad`, for instance, the twenty-eighth position — such as that of Sayyidna Ja'far bin Sulayman — is of very high station. Similarly, the twenty-eighth Imam, Sayyidna Ma'add, Mawlana Ma'add, is of very high station. Yet the twenty-ninth is also of extraordinary station.

Consider what pertains to the Prophet: the `kalimat al-shahadah` appears in five forms in the Quran — *La ilaha ill-Allah*, *La ilaha illa hu*, *La ilaha illa ana*, *La ilaha illa alladhi*, and *La ilaha illa anta*. The last form, *La ilaha illa anta*, is rare, appearing only once in the Quran — in the glorification of Yunus, peace be upon him. The rest are repeated. And *Muhammadun Rasulullah* likewise appears only once in the Quran — unrepeated — in Surah al-Fath, in its twenty-ninth verse. Which Rasul was he in the sequence? The twenty-ninth. Janab 'Ali, peace be upon him, was the twenty-eighth — and the twenty-eighth holds a very high station. The twenty-ninth, whether among the Imams or among the `du'at` (missionaries), is rare; he enters into the sequence, disrupting its ordinary flow. Rasulullah, *sallallahu alayhi wa sallam*, entered the line of Imams — not as an Imam in the ordinary sense, for after Imam Janab Abu Talib it should have been Janab 'Ali. But he entered, and 'Ali became his follower, because in him all four ranks were combined: `nubuwwah` (prophethood), `risalah` (messengership), `imamah` (leadership), and `wasayat` (succession). He thus became the master even of 'Ali. This was a station established for Janab Rasul in the `alam al-ruhani` (spiritual world).

The same pattern holds among the `du'at`: Sayyidna Ja'far bin Sulayman is the twenty-eighth, and the twenty-ninth is Sayyidna Muhammad bin Faraj. Although Sayyidna Muhammad bin Faraj was a `da'i ghayr munfarid` (non-absolute missionary), he became `munfarid` (absolute), having been established in the rank of the `munfaridin`. Sayyidna Ja'far passed away during his lifetime, yet Sayyidna Muhammad bin Faraj was established in the rank of a `hujjah` (proof) — one who is the equal of thirty `du'at`. After Sayyidna Ja'far, every Da'i who has come has been established in the rank of `hujjah`, without exception, until today. This is the status of the `qa'im al-du'at` (established missionaries). When Sayyidna Ja'far bin Sulayman was established in the rank of `hujjah`, the `da'i al-balagh` (missionary of communication) of the three `jazirah` — that rank — became vacant, and `nass sharif` was performed upon it. Therefore he is retained in the sequence.

This sequence will be found in the old `sahifah` (books) — those of Sayyidna Ghulam Sahab, Sayyidna Farhat Ali Sahab, and Sayyidna Ziyayi Sahab Muqaddas, *Zul Ma'ani* — where no mention of Sayyidna Muhammad bin Faraj appears. When the new `sahifah` was compiled, his mention was likewise omitted in accordance with the older books. But when Sayyidna 'Ali bin Husayn reviewed the manuscripts — for three days the unprinted manuscripts remained with Sayyidna Sahab, held by Nasrullah Sahab Muqaddas and 'Abdullah Sahab Muqaddas — he studied them carefully, declared that everything was correct, and raised two or three points. One of those points concerned Sayyidna Muhammad bin Faraj.

The status of Sayyidna Muhammad bin Faraj is that he is the twenty-ninth. And the twenty-ninth, wherever he appears, enters into the sequence — just as Rasulullah entered the sequence as the twenty-ninth. Surah al-Fath embodies the same principle: nowhere in the Quran did Allah Most High mention Rasulullah by name in his capacity as Rasul — except in Surah al-Fath, and there in the twenty-ninth verse. The twenty-ninth verse of Surah al-Fath opens with *Muhammadun Rasulullah* — Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah.

The `muqatta'at` (disjoined opening letters of the Quran) are twenty-nine in total. Of the 6,666 verses of the Quran, the letters may be classified in more than one way. One classification distinguishes dotted from undotted letters. Another distinguishes `nurani` (luminous) from `zulmani` (tenebrous) letters. The twenty-eight letters of the Arabic alphabet are arranged in a scheme of four groups of seven: the first seven are `nurani khafifah` (lightly luminous), the second are `nurani thaqilah` (heavily luminous), the third are `zulmani khafifah` (lightly tenebrous), and the fourth are `zulmani thaqilah` (heavily tenebrous). In Surah al-Fatihah — the summary of the entire Quran — one `zulmani khafifah` letter does appear. But there exists one location in the Quran where no `zulmani khafifah` or `zulmani thaqilah` letter appears at all: the `muqatta'at`. Not a single `zulmani` letter — whether lightly or heavily tenebrous — occurs in the `muqatta'at`. This is why their status is very high, and why the `du'at` have used them as a `wasilah` (means of supplication) in `ta'wizat` (amulets) and in certain prayers. In the brief daily supplications of Imam Ja'far al-Sadiq, the second supplication employs *Kaf-Ha-Ya-'Ayn-Sad* as a `wasilah` — and this entire supplication has been translated. The `muqatta'at` function as a `wasilah`, and there are twenty-nine of them in all.

Among the `muqatta'at`, some are repeated and some are unique. For example, *Alif-Lam-Mim* appears many times, whereas *Nun* appears only once, and *Ya-Sin* only once. From the non-repeated `muqatta'at`, a single sentence can be formed — and only one sentence: *Siratu 'Aliyyin haqq, numsikuhu* — "The path of 'Ali is truth; we hold to it." No other sentence can be formed from them. It will be found that in translations of the Quran produced for general audiences, the `muqatta'at` are either passed over, or accompanied by a note that only Allah knows their meaning, or at most that His Prophet knows. This is because those translators did not remain attached to the Ahl al-Bayt, and so the knowledge did not reach them. Even the Shi'a, who profess love for the Ahl al-Bayt, express their `walayat` (devotion) fervently, and genuinely love them — even they do not possess the knowledge that we hold. The realities of `lahut`, `na'ut`, `nasut`, the `nafs-i-imam`, and matters of that nature are entirely absent from their understanding. This reveals that with Imam Ja'far al-Sadiq two dimensions were always present: an outward following, whose adherents were his Shi'a in the common sense, and a `batini` (inner) following, which was separate and distinct. The `batini` Shi'a moved forward gradually — steadfast in their faith, firm in their silence and constancy.

Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi rabb al-'alameen. Wa-l-salatu wa-l-salamu 'ala sayyid al-mursalin. Wa 'ala alihi al-tayyibin al-tahirin. Wa 'ala hudaat dinihi al-mayamin. Wa-l-'anatu 'ala a'da'ihim ajma'in ila yawm al-din. Amin. Rabbana taqabbal minna innaka anta al-sami' al-'alim. Wa tub 'alayna innaka anta al-tawwab al-rahim. Sayyidna wa Mawlana al-Mansur al-Mutlaq fi al-Dunya wa al-Din, Sayyidna wa Sharaf al-Dalil, al-'Alim al-'Azim wa al-Haj al-Sharif, Sayyidna Ghulam Husayn Sayyidna al-Mansur al-Muqaddas, wa walid Nasrullah 'Abdullah Hibatullah bin al-Mansur al-Muqaddas, *qaddasa Allahu ruhuma*. Wa *tawwala Allahu baqa* Mawlana 'azz ta'ala, yaquruhu wa yahmihi wa yu'idduhu wa yukfihi wa yushfihi, min sharri jami' al-amrad wa-l-asqam wa-l-awja' wa-l-alam, min sharri al-ghilal wa-l-anzam, wa min sharri al-nuhusat wa-l-khusufat wa-l-taslisat wa-l-tarbi'at wa-l-takhayil wa-l-mawazir, wa an yudim nasrahu wa yahfazahu wa yakhtam 'umrahu wa yublighahu maratib al-saniyah wa yahmihi fi al-hidayat al-ghurrah wa-l-shakhsiyyah, wa matta'ana bi-tuli hayatihi wa baqqa li-maqamih wa darajatihi, wa ahshurna iyyakum fi sa'at al-da'wah wa bi-jawari hudud al-qudsuna, bi-jahi Sayyid al-Mursalin, Sayyidna wa Mawlana al-Imam al-Mubin, wa Ahl Baytihi al-Tayyibin al-Tahirin, salawatullahi wa salamuhu 'alayhim ajma'in. Al-Fatihah. Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi rabb al-'alameen. Al-Rahman al-Rahim. Maliki yawm al-din. Iyyaka na'budu wa iyyaka nasta'in. Ihdina al-sirat al-mustaqim. Sirat alladhina an'amta 'alayhim ghayril maghdubi 'alayhim wa la al-dallin. Amin.


<!-- lecture 6 -->

Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Wa bihi nasta'in wa sallallahu 'ala Rasulihi Sayyidina Muhammadin wa aali Muhammadin wa barik wa sallim (In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful. And in Him we seek help, and may Allah's blessings and peace be upon His Messenger, our Master Muhammad, and the family of Muhammad).

These two are the counterparts of the Kalima-e-Shahadat that had been mentioned. Sayyidna Ali bin Hibatullah has said: "fa-sun hadha al-sirr al-masun" — guard this preserved secret. "yasunaka Allah" — Allah will protect you. "wa-hfaz ma ulqiya ilayka" — preserve what has been cast upon you. "yahfazaka Allah" — Allah will protect you. "wa hadha min al-asrar al-'izam allati la yakad yastur bi'l-aqlam" — this is among the great secrets that can scarcely be set down by pens. These are matters of the most magnificent secrets, of a nature that cannot be committed to writing. "wa dhalika mimma ajrahu" — this is from what He has caused to flow. Whatever has been written here flowed upon the tongue by the grace of the Mawla. Sayyidna Hibatullah himself said: "wa lam yakun qabla dhalika tara fi qalbi wa janani" — before this, these matters had not arisen in my heart or my soul. "wa hiya nuqtah min bahri 'ulumihi wa hibatihi" — it is a single drop from the ocean of his knowledges and his gifts. "wa tuhfatun min tuhafihi" — a gift from among his gifts. "wa sadaqa min sadaqatihi" — a charity from his charities, bestowed upon me. "wa qad saqatha ayyuha al-... ilayka hadiyah mubaradah bi-ghayri ta'abin 'alayka" — these things have reached you, O brother, as a cooling gift — one that brings peace — without any toil on your part. You have made no effort, and yet this has come to you. "wa mathaluha la yahsul illa bi'l-ibtila" — its like is not attained except through trial. "wa'l-hijr wa'l-hijrat" — through separation and emigrations. "wa'l-firaq li'l-ahl wa'l-awtan" — through leaving one's family, one's cities, and one's homelands — only then are such things attained. Yet for you, they have been attained while sitting here. "fa-ahmad mawlaka fi ghuduwwika wa masaka" — so give thanks and praise to your Mawla in the morning and in the evening.

"al-su'al al-thalith" — the third question. "qad 'alimtu mimma jawwaba Zain al-'Abidin 'ala Umm al-Nada min qabli... 'umr al-dunya" — I have already taken note of what Mawlana Zain al-'Abidin answered to Umm al-Nada. Umm al-Nada was a woman of great standing; she was among the Hudud al-Da'wat and a woman of deep learning. She came to Imam Zain al-'Abidin 'alayhi al-salam and said, "Mawlana, tell me how much time has passed over the world, and how much remains." He replied, "I will tell you how much has passed, but I will not tell you how much remains — you do not have the istihqaq (merit) for that." He then told her that one mawr had passed, and that they were living within the kawr, the dawr, of the second mawr.

"wa qad qala Sayyidna al-Qadi Sarim al-Din qaddas Allahu nafsahu wa razaqna shafa'atahu" — Sayyidna al-Qadi Sarim al-Din — that is, Sayyidna Ibrahim bin Muhammad Faad, who is your grandfather — may Allah sanctify his soul and grant us the provision of his intercession, "bi-ridaka 'anna" — and may he be pleased with us — has said: "anna al-rukn min fudulat Amir al-Mu'minin al-qadimah fi al-akwar al-ula" — that the Rukn is from the ancient remnants of the Commander of the Faithful in the first cycles. The Rukn al-As'ad — alif, sin, 'ayn, dal — is the most blessed corner of the Baytullah, the corner in which the Hajar al-Aswad is set. It is said that this Rukn al-As'ad is composed of the fudulat (remnants) of Amir al-Mu'minin from the first akwar (cycles). This was set down "fi waraqatayni bi-khatti Sayyidi Safi al-Din Ahmad bin Isma'il" — in two pages in the hand of Safi al-Din Ahmad bin Isma'il, who was among the Hudud of that era. "fi awwal al-jussah al-mudhamah" — in the first great corporeal form. In those first akwar, Amir al-Mu'minin underwent the raj'at (return) repeatedly — born, then passing away — and through successive cycles, their accumulated fudulat formed the Rukn al-As'ad. The question then arose: "wa fi ayyi kawr zahara amruhu" — in which cycle did his affair appear? "wa kayfa kana zuhuruhu" — and in what manner did it manifest? "wa hal yaqi'u 'alayhi al-subhaniyyah" — and can the term Subhaniyyah be applied to him? That is, when we say "Subhan Allah," is it permissible to apply the word "Subhan" to Amir al-Mu'minin as well? "wa kam min hadha... 'umr al-dunya" — and how much of the age of the world has this encompassed?

[Side discussion begins]

[Questioner:] Regarding the fudulat of Amir al-Mu'minin —

[Speaker:] — arising from repetition —

[Questioner:] Amir al-Mu'minin and the A'immah are all ri'yat. From the perspective of the purity of their din, fudulat are not even generated in their case.

[Speaker:] That is a good question. By fudulat here, we do not mean what fudulah signifies in the case of an ordinary Mumin. In their case, all such matter becomes ri'yat and departs. Within three days it is confined to the grave, and the rest becomes flowing — it becomes ri'yat. What is meant here by fudulat is their remaining parts. As was explained in a previous lesson, their ri'yat — which are stored — are housed in the Baytullah and divided into five portions. One portion goes to the Hudud al-Da'wat, through which they arrive at correct decisions, are preserved from evils, and their standard in taqwa (piety) is elevated. That one-fifth portion of their ri'yat is also called fudulat in one sense, and it is returned when the Imam passes away — those things return to the Baytullah. So by fudulat here is meant their ri'yat in a progressed state, and those progressed ri'yat come to the Baytullah.

[Questioner:] But this has taken a material form.

[Speaker:] Yes.

[Questioner:] It has taken a material form. For ri'yat to take a material form —

[Speaker:] No — it is not in a material form.

[Questioner:] I mean, the Rukn al-As'ad has been formed, a stone has been formed, so it has become material, has it not?

[Speaker:] No. The nature of the Rukn al-As'ad itself is lofty — it is not material in that sense. The blessings within the stone are what is being pointed to.

[Questioner:] As we are taught, the blessed stones — 'aqiq (carnelian), yaqut (ruby) — are all fudulat of the Du'at Kiram. That is readily understandable. But the fudulat of Mawla Ali and the A'immah Kiram are all —

[Speaker:] No, fudulat —

[Questioner:] — ri'yat —

[Speaker:] — fudulat in the ordinary sense are not even generated in their case. In one sense they are called fudulat — the remaining matter left from their bodies. Sometimes ri'yat are also called fudulat. And those fudulat that progress through the moon and ascend to Jupiter — one part of them descends and forms the body of the coming Imam, and some part comes to the Baytullah, where one-fifth of it is received by the Hudud al-Da'wat. And it also occurs that the fudulat of Amir al-Mu'minin, which have progressed and exist in a purified state, come and — the Baytullah's Rukn al-As'ad —

[Audio interference]

[Speaker:] The voice from the Baytullah's Rukn al-As'ad — yes, the voice is coming — Husnain Sahab, is the voice coming?

[Husnain Sahab:] Yes, it is coming. Assalamu 'alaykum wa rahmatullahi wa barakatuh.

[Audio interference]

[Speaker:] Your voice is not clear. When I was reading this, I too had a question about why the word fudulat was used. By fudulat here, ri'yat is what is meant.

[Side discussion ends]

"wa qad tabaddala al-mashriq maghriban wa'l-shimal junuban" — the east has become the west, and the north has become the south. "tafaddalu bi'l-jawab al-shafi" — so please do us the favour of giving the answer that will be sufficient. "tawwala Allahu subhanahu bi-a'marikum" — may Allah, glory be to Him, lengthen your lives. "al-jawab 'ala dhalika al-su'al, naqulu bi-'awn Allah al-Kabir al-Muta'al" — the answer to that question: we say, with the help of Allah, the Great, the Most High, "wa minnat Wali al-'Asr wa'l-Zaman" — and with the grace of the Master of the Age and the Time, "'alayhi salawat al-Rahim al-Rahman" — upon him be the blessings of the Merciful, the Compassionate — and with the support of Mawla al-A'lam and his brilliant and magnificent secret, that is, the support of Hibatullah Sahab — "anna ja'a... awrada Sayyidna Sarim al-Din" — this matter has been presented by Sayyidna Sarim al-Din, that is Sayyidna Hibatullah, may Allah sanctify him in 'Illiyyin — "bi-anna al-Hajar al-Aswad min kathif Amir al-Mu'minin al-muwajjad" — that the Hajar al-Aswad is from the kathif (dense substance) of Amir al-Mu'minin al-muwajjad, the one who is made present. By kathif is meant those ri'yat which are purified and which find a place in various noble stations. One such noble station is that they become the very body of the Imam — the body of the Imam who is to come. Throughout his life, the food consumed by the present Imam, and the food consumed by the future child, all of it is ri'yat. And because the Mu'minin are connected to those Imams and profess their walayat (allegiance), they receive a share of that sustenance. As in Surah al-Furqan, in the verses concerning 'ibad al-Rahman (servants of the Merciful), it is said: "wa-j'alna li'l-muttaqin imama" — and make us an Imam for the righteous. A man was once circumambulating the Baytullah reciting this verse — for it appears among the verses of tawaf (circumambulation). Imam Ja'far al-Sadiq, or Mawlana Ali Zain al-'Abidin, said to that man: "You have asked Allah for something far beyond your station. You cannot become the Imam of the righteous; you do not have that standing" — even though it is a Quranic verse. When making du'a (supplication), one should instead say: "wa-j'alna min al-muttaqin" — make us from among the righteous.

"fa-dhalika huwa al-haqq alladhi la shakka ya'tarihi" — that is the truth which no doubt can befall. "wa'l-sirr al-khafi alladhi la maryata fihi" — it is a hidden secret in which there is no ambiguity. "wa amma qawluka, wa fi ayyi kawr zahara sallallahu 'alayhi wa 'alihi al-ghur" — as for your question about in which kawr Ali 'alayhi al-salam and his luminous family appeared — "faqad qala al-Nabi 'alayhi al-salam, ana ibn al-karrat wa mumtahin al-awqat" — the Prophet 'alayhi al-salam has said: "I am the son of the recurring cycles and the one tried by the times." We have been born across various recurring cycles and tested across various ages. In one narration he said: "Allah tested me seven times before the risalat (prophethood), and seven times after." Amir al-Mu'minin likewise says: "Rasulullah tested me seven times before making me his wasi (successor), and seven times after." He recited the full list of all fourteen tests in a single gathering, asking, "Did this not happen?" — and the entire gathering testified: "Yes, you speak the truth; it happened just so." The Awliya Allah (friends of Allah) all pass through such tests.

"faqad su'ila Mawlana al-Sadiq haythu qala" — and Mawlana al-Sadiq was asked, whereupon he said — testing is intrinsic to the system of the da'wat (mission). Until a person is made to pass through a test, he is not established in a rank. Sayyidna Yusuf bin Sulayman — the very first Da'i in Hindustan and the grandfather of Sayyidna Sulayman Sahab — was a man of great standing and magnificence. The last Da'i of the Bani Anf was Sayyidna Da'i Muhammad Husayn, after whom no further da'is came from that line. Sayyidna Muhammad Husayn put him through a most severe test. Yusuf bin Sulayman lived in a distant village and would come early each morning to study with Sayyidna Sahab. One day he arrived, and the hajib (chamberlain) — the guard of Sayyidna Muhammad bin Husayn — told him to go back, saying the Sayyidna did not wish to receive him. For forty days he came continuously at his usual hour, and each time he was turned away. On the fortieth day the hajib said, "Go away." But at that very moment, the Da'i called out from his veranda: "Let Yusuf come to me." He came, and the Da'i embraced him. This was the test. In this way, through various such trials, he was made ready.

Sayyidna Husayn bin Isma'il once remarked that there were two periods in the da'wat when the Hindustani muhajirin (emigrants) outnumbered the muhajirin of Yemen and Najran. The first was at the time of Sayyidna Yusuf bin Sulayman, and the second was at the time of Sayyidna Hibatullah Sahab — at both those times, the Hindustani muhajirin were the more numerous.


"wa qad su'ila Mawlana al-Sadiq haythu qala, kunna fi'l-adwar al-madiyyah wa'l-huqub al-khaliyah" (And Mawlana al-Sadiq was asked, whereupon he said, 'We were in the past cycles and the bygone eras'). In response to this question, he gave this answer: "We were in the past cycles." The cycles that have passed — we were present in them, "wa'l-huqub al-khaliyah." "wa qala lahu al-sa'il, ya Mawlai, kuntum bi-ashkhasiukum am bi-a'yanikum" (And the questioner said to him, 'O my Master, were you there in your persons or in your representatives?'). As for Mawlana Aristotle — what did he say about him? That "Kana Aba Muhammad Aristatalis minna Ahl al-Bayt" (Aba Muhammad Aristotle was from us, the Ahl al-Bayt). Imam Ja'far al-Sadiq even mentioned his kunyah (patronymic): that Aristotle — Aristatalis — whose title was Aba Muhammad, was from the Ahl al-Bayt. Just as he said of Salman al-Farsi: "Salman minna Ahl al-Bayt, yusalla wa yusallam 'alayhi" (Salman is from us, the Ahl al-Bayt; blessings and peace should be sent upon him) — just as you send blessings and peace upon us. The station of Janab Salman is therefore very lofty, for he is the only person in the entire da'wat, throughout the entire Dawr al-Sitr, who became the Bab al-Abwab (Gate of Gates) for both the Nabi and the Wasi. He became the Bab al-Abwab for the Prophet, the Master of the Universe. Janab Khadijah had also become a Bab al-Abwab, but Salman al-Farsi 'alayhi al-salam similarly held that rank — and he likewise became the Bab al-Abwab of Janab Ali 'alayhi al-salam.

"fa-qala lahu al-sa'il, ya Mawlai, kuntum bi-ashkhasiukum am bi-a'yanikum" (So the questioner said to him, 'O my Master, were you there in your persons or in your representatives?'). Meaning: were you there with your real selves or with your hijabs (veils)? "fa-qala lahu bal bi-ashkhasiina wa a'yanina" (So he said to him, 'Rather, in our persons and our representatives'). We were present both with our real selves and with our hijabs.

"wa amma al-Hajar al-Aswad wa'l-Rukn al-As'ad fa-qad yakun wallahu a'lam anna qawl Sayyidna Sarim al-Din innahu ittasala bihi shay'un latif mimma dakhkhiratuhu al-'inayat al-ilahiyyah min hujub Amir al-Mu'minin al-mukanna 'anhu bi'l-'ayn al-'azimah al-nuraniyyah" (As for the Black Stone and the Most Fortunate Corner, it may be — and Allah knows best — that the statement of Sayyidna Sarim al-Din is that a subtle substance connected with it from what the divine providence stored from the veils of the Commander of the Faithful, who is alluded to as the Great, Luminous 'Ayn). That is, a subtle substance — from what was stored by the divine providence, namely the jussah-e-ibtidaiyyah of 'Ashir — drawn from the hijabs of Amir al-Mu'minin, who is called the 'Ayn al-'Azimah (the Great 'Ayn), al-nuraniyyah (the luminous one), was connected to his real self.

"wa amma qawluka, wa hal yaqi'u 'alayhi al-subhaniyyah" (As for your statement, 'And does the term Subhaniyyah apply to him?'). Can the word "Subhaniyyah" be applied to Amir al-Mu'minin? "faqad sabbahatuhu al-mala'ikah al-qudsaniyyah" (For the holy angels have glorified him). The holy angels have indeed glorified him — they have said "Subhan" — "haythu tashawwaqat ila tasawwur shakhsihi al-gharr" (when they yearned for the perception of his brilliant person). The angels yearned to perceive his brilliant person, to behold him. In the spiritual realm, to have the didar (vision) of Amir al-Mu'minin — for though he is corporeally present on earth, to behold him in the spiritual world and in the heavens was what they sought from Allah. "fa-awjada Allah ta'ala subhanahu fi'l-samawati mithaluhu" (So Allah the Exalted, glory be to Him, brought into existence his likeness in the heavens). Allah thus created a likeness of him in the heavens. "fa-kanat tasbihuhu wa taqdisuhu laylan wa naharan 'ashiyyatan wa abkara" (So they glorified and sanctified him night and day, evening and morning). The angels glorified and sanctified him morning and evening, night and day. "fa-kana hadha mithal 'azim" (And this was a great example). And this is a great example. "fa-kayfa bi-khalifat al-'Ashir al-Hakim" (So how is it with the Caliph of the 'Ashir, the Wise?). Just as the Quranic verse says: "fi'l-sama'i ilahun wa fi'l-ardi ilah" (In the heaven is a god, and on the earth is a god) — this points to the state of Amir al-Mu'minin: in heaven there is an Ali and his likeness, and on earth there is an Ali whose likeness is in heaven.

"fa-kayfa bi-khalifat al-'Ashir al-Hakim wa ma-ndimamuhu fi dimn al-Qa'im al-hashir al-nashir" (So how is it with the Caliph of the 'Ashir, the Wise, and his inclusion within the Qa'im, the Gatherer, the Resurrector?). His inclusion took place with the Qa'im; he became the Bab al-Abwab for the Qa'im. The A'immah who came before — from Janab Hunayn to Amir al-Mu'minin — the thalathah sufarah (the three emissaries), namely Mawlana 'Abd al-Muttalib, Mawlana 'Abdullah, and Mawlana Abu Talib, their inclusion did not occur through Amir al-Mu'minin. Rather, they remained in the barzakh (intermediate state) of the Janab-e-Qa'im, and the Qa'im absorbed them directly, not through Amir al-Mu'minin. They hold a very high status. Up to Janab Hashim, Amir al-Mu'minin gathered the hayakil (structures) of the sufarah-e-nayyirah (luminous emissaries). And after that, he gathered the hayakil from Janab Hasan to Abul-Qa'im — they became the Bab al-Abwab of Qa'im 'alayhi al-salam. It has never happened that an Imam gathered the A'immah who came after him; yet this is among the extraordinary miracles of Amir al-Mu'minin, that he gathered the hayakil of the Imams who came later as well.
