His nafs-e-hissiyah (sensory soul) was so powerful and spiritual that it was greater than the hayakil of others — for only one whose nafs-e-hissiyah surpasses another's haykal can gather it.

His hashr (gathering) was therefore with the Qa'im. The point is that he will become the caliph of the 'Ashir, the Mudabbir. By 'Ashir here is meant not that 'Ashir, but the jussah (body) of this kawr — yawman ma (one day), through a progression in the line that has been formed, he will become the khalifah (caliph) within it, the Caliph of the 'Ashir. "wa su'uduhu ila rutbat al-'Ashir" (And his ascent to the rank of the 'Ashir). His ascent will be as the 'Ashir; he will be the jussah-e-ibtidaiyyah in which he will come, yawman ma.

"sabbahathu al-mala'ikah al-ruhaniyyah bi-a'la min dhalika al-shan" (The spiritual angels glorified him with a station higher than that). The spiritual angels glorified him with a station higher than any station he had held before — higher than what he had attained in the various recurring cycles. In the seventh dawr (period), in the seventh zuhur (manifestation) — this manifestation of his was the seventh, which was higher than all previous manifestations. It was because it was his seventh manifestation that he performed those extraordinary miracles. The angels glorified him with a glorification surpassing all previous manifestations, "li-man fatana al-ma'ani al-rabbaniyyah" (for one who comprehends the Lordly meanings) — for the one who reflects deeply upon the Lordly meanings.

"wa amma qawluka kam hatta tabaddala al-mashriq maghriban wa'l-shimal junuban" (As for your statement, 'How long until the east has changed into the west, and the north into the south?'). When will it be that the east becomes the west and the north becomes the south? "fa-yakunu dhalika 'inda tarakhi al-rubatat" (So that will be at the loosening of the ties). This will happen at the tarakhi al-rubatat (loosening of the ties) — that is, at the end of the kawr. "wa takunu al-tabdilat wa'l-istihalat" (And the changes and transformations will occur). Very great changes will occur, and major events will take place, such that things will be entirely transformed. "wa amma 'iddat dhalika fa-qad dhakarahu Sayyidna Humam" (As for the period of that, Sayyidna Humam has mentioned it). The duration of that has been mentioned by Sayyidna Humam al-Din — Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan — and one should, with the permission of the Wali al-Amr, consult those matters. "wa fi aqwal dhil-jaddayn ma yujli al-sada wa'l-rayn" (And in the sayings of Dhul-Jaddayn is that which clears the rust and the stain). In the sayings of Dhul-Jaddayn — namely Sayyidna Husayn bin Ahmad — is that which removes the rust settled upon hearts. He is a figure of immense stature: he wrote the tafsir (exegesis) of the Quran in seventy days and nights, on the command of Sayyidna Ali Sahab, who commanded him because he was the son of Sayyidna Ali Sahab's sister. He was thirty-three years old when the command was given. Sayyidna Husayn bin Ahmad also performed Hajj on behalf of Sayyidna Ja'far — for Sayyidna Ja'far, despite his great status as the twenty-eighth Dai and the first among the shumukh du'at (lofty dais), did not himself perform Hajj. He had once set out for it but had to turn back due to certain difficulties. Sayyidna Ali Sahab therefore commanded him: "You perform the Hajj of Sayyidna Sahab as a qada' (make-up) and complete it." He did so. And when Sayyidna Ali Sahab commanded, "Husayn, write the tafsir of the Quran," he wrote it in seventy days and nights — even while traveling throughout that period. When he had completed it, a very swift scribe from Yemen took eight months to copy it. It fills three large volumes. This is a magnificent state that Sayyidna Husayn bin Ahmad achieved. Although he was not a munfarid (solitary) dai, he holds immense standing — the du'at take tawassul (intercession) through him — and Sayyidna Isma'il bin Hibatullah Sahab himself refers to him as "Sayyidna Usama" in several places within the Kawakeb al-Durriyah, that great and magnificent book. One should therefore seek help from the sayings of Dhul-Jaddayn, which remove the rust that has settled upon hearts.

"al-su'al al-rabi' 'an al-bata'ih allati tahta al-Ka'bah, kam hiya" (The fourth question, concerning the lowlands that are beneath the Ka'bah — how many are they?). The fourth question concerns the bata'ih (lowlands) that lie beneath the Ka'bah: how many are they? "wa min ayna abwabuhum" (And from where are their gates?). Which are their doors? "wa ma tasir wa yasiru ilayha" (And what travels and goes towards it?). What things go up to them? "wa ma yadharu minha" (And what appears from it?). What things manifest from these bata'ih? "wa Bir Dhat al-'Alam alladhi qala al-shakhs al-'azim" (And the Well of Dhat al-'Alam about which the great person spoke). As for Bir Dhat al-'Alam — this well figures in the Battle of Bani Mustaliq. When the Prophet, salawatullahi 'alayhi, was returning from that expedition with his army, they reached a place whose people said: "We have a well in which there are shayatin (devils) who trouble us greatly, and all the people here are in distress." The Prophet commanded his army: "Who among you will go and fight them?" Two young men stood forward. He said: "I give the guarantee of jannah (paradise) to them" — whoever fights these shayatin, I guarantee him paradise. The two young men fought and were martyred. Then the Prophet said to Ali, "Qum ya Ali" (Stand, O Ali) — "Ali, you stand and fight them." Janab Ali thereupon fought them at Bir Dhat al-'Alam. When he emerged, blood was flowing from his sword. Janab Rasulullah asked: "O Ali, how did you fight them?" He replied: "I fought them with my sword, and I fought them by reciting and blowing the verses of the Quran upon them" — meaning the Quranic verses were so potent that he vanquished them through recitation and breath. He also mentioned a third method: "This is how I fought."

[Phone rings]

[Speaker on phone:] Hello. Yes, I'm doing the ring. Hello. Yes. I'll call you.

[Resumes lecture]

This Bir Dhat al-'Alam is thus the name of a well at which Amir al-Mu'minin fought. In some book, a question was posed to a dai: were all the jinn that Amir al-Mu'minin fought at Bir Dhat al-'Alam destroyed? The dai answered: "No, they remain — but the coming Qa'im will bring them to an end." Just as with the matter of 'adhab (punishment) and thawab (reward) — the matter of punishing the enemies of the din — the A'immah cannot make that final determination. They leave it to the Qa'im, who is the Hashir and Nashir (Gatherer and Resurrector). The decision of whom to send to jannah and whom to send to hell is left partly to Amir al-Mu'minin, who is the Qasim al-Nar wa'l-Jannah (Distributor of the Fire and the Garden), and ultimately to the Qa'im, who will make the final judgment. He will cast the azdad (opposites) into Sakhr, and whatever decision he makes, he will make it. One can gauge from our 'ulum (knowledge) that when the Qa'im makes his zuhur (appearance) — at the occasion of Hajj — he will say to the Mu'minin: "On the yawm al-nahr (day of sacrifice), the day of 'Id, you kill the azdad." The Mu'minin will kill them, and this will continue for three days. After one or two days, such a stench will spread through the valley of Mina that the Mu'minin will find it difficult to breathe. They will plead to the Qa'im: "We cannot endure this state, and yet we must remain in the valley of Mina until the thirteenth." The Imam will then command the winds, and winds will blow; the manafidh (outlets) of Sakhr — Sakhr has four manafidh — will draw those things away toward Sakhr. Sayyidna Ali Sahab has also listed the names of those four manafidh in one of his books.

[Questioner:] Where is this well?

[Speaker:] I have not been able to determine where it is.

And among the miracles of Amir al-Mu'minin from the perspective of the Qa'im-e-Juzwi (partial Qa'im), this Bir Dhat al-'Alam is likely also counted.


"Inni dhahabtu ila tahti mizab al-Ka'bah fi qissat qital al-jinn" (I went under the water-spout of the Ka'bah in the story of the battle with the jinn). Amir al-Mu'minin has said, "I went under the mizab (water-spout) of the Ka'bah." And "wa'l-mawaqif al-arba'" (and the four stations) — the mawaqif are four. "wa min ayna manafidh al-bata'ih" (And from where are the outlets of the lowlands?) — from where are the manafidh of the bata'ih, the windows? "wa mannu bi'l-jawab fa-antum ahl al-sawab" (So grant the favor of an answer, for you are the people of correctness) — give the answer, and do us the favor, for you are the people of correctness.

"al-jawab 'ala dhalika al-su'al, naqulu bi-'awn Allah al-Kabir al-Muta'al" (The answer to that question: we say with the help of Allah, the Great, the Most High). The answer to your question we offer with the help of Allah, the Great, the Most High, and with the grace of the Wali al-'Asr wa'l-Zaman, 'alayhi salawat al-Rahim al-Rahman (upon him be the blessings of the Merciful, the Compassionate). "wa ta'yid Mawlai al-A'lam wa sirrihi al-ba' wa'l-mu'azzam" (And with the support of my Master, the Most Learned, and his brilliant and magnificent secret) — with the support of Sayyidna Hibatullah, and your magnificent and luminous secret.

"anna al-bata'ih allati hawla al-qalb al-gharr hiya arba'" (that the lowlands which are around the brilliant heart are four). The bata'ih surrounding the luminous qalb (heart) are four. "kama sabaqa min al-qawl" (as has been mentioned in the previous statement) — as stated before, "li-man haqqaqa al-nazar" (for one who has verified the observation). "wa qawluka innaha tahta al-bayt al-munir fa-laysa dhalika kadhalik bal hawlahu" (And your statement that it is beneath the luminous House — it is not so; rather it is around it). Your saying that it is beneath the Bayt al-Munir (Luminous House) is not correct; rather, it is around it, on its peripheries. "'ala amrin jalilin khatir" (upon a great and momentous affair) — a great and momentous affair, in that the bata'ih are on its peripheries, not beneath it.

"wa amma qawluka min ayna abwabuhum wa majariha" (As for your statement, 'From where are their gates and their channels?') — which are its doors and from where are its channels for flowing? "wa amma ma yas'adu minha wa yasiru ilayha" (As for what ascends from it and what goes towards it) — things ascend and descend towards it. "fa-'alam anna tahta al-qalb ma'un latif" (So know that beneath the heart is a subtle water) — beneath the qalb there is a subtle water. "wa fi wasatihi" (and in its center) — in its center too there is subtle water. "wa minhu yanba'u ila tahti tilka al-bata'ih" (And from it gushes forth to beneath those lowlands) — from those waters, things ascend towards the bata'ih "bi-majari" (through channels) — and there are channels which open — "wa manafidh" (and outlets) — windows too are there — "'ala amrin sharif" (upon a noble affair).

"wa hawla hadhihi al-bata'ih ahwiyah" (And around these lowlands are atmospheres) — around these four bata'ih there are atmospheres. "al-sharifah al-shaffafah al-munirah" (The noble, transparent, luminous ones) — transparent and luminous. "wa kadhalika hawla hadhihi al-bata'ih ma'un latif" (And likewise, around these lowlands is a subtle water) — around the bata'ih there is also a very subtle water. "wa la yazal yas'adu minha bukhar sharif" (And a noble vapor does not cease to ascend from it) — noble vapors continuously ascend from these bata'ih. "wa huwa al-mizaj" (And it is the temperament) — this is also called the mizaj (temperament). "alladhi dhakarahu Sayyidna Hamid al-Din, alladhi takawwanat minhu nufus al-nuqa wa'l-a'immah al-mayamin" (which Sayyidna Hamid al-Din mentioned, from which were formed the souls of the pure ones and the blessed Imams). This mizaj — rising from the subtle water, becoming vapor — was mentioned by Sayyidna Hamid al-Din al-Kirmani: from it, from these vapors, the nufus (souls) of the nuqa (pure ones) and the A'immah al-Mayamin (blessed Imams) are formed.

"wa amma qawluka Bir Dhat al-'Alam" (As for your statement, 'the Well of Dhat al-'Alam') — your question about Bir Dhat al-'Alam — "wa qatluhu Amir al-Mu'minin al-mu'azzam al-jinn" (and the killing of the jinn by the magnificent Commander of the Faithful) — and the killing of the jinn by Amir al-Mu'minin. "alladhina zaharu lamma da'ahum sallallahu 'alayhi fa-wallaw wa ankaru" (who appeared when he — peace be upon him — called them, but they turned away and denied) — when Amir al-Mu'minin extended da'wat (invitation) to them, they denied it and turned their backs. "fa-dhalika huwa al-qawl al-wadih al-sarih" (So that is the clear, explicit statement) — this matter is true, clear, and explicit: Amir al-Mu'minin had given da'wat to these jinn and they had denied it — whereas in Surah al-Jinn it is stated that the jinn accepted faith. Those were other jinn; their states have been described in Surah al-Jinn.

"wa'l-hadith al-marwi al-sahih" (And the authentic, transmitted hadith). "wa qatala minhum man zahara fi suwar al-haywaniyyah" (And he killed from among them those who appeared in animal forms) — those who appeared in suwar-e-haywaniyyah (animal forms), meaning in corporeal forms, you killed them. "wa akhmada man tajarrada minhum fi surat al-zulmaniyyah" (And he extinguished those among them who were divested in the form of darkness) — those who were in the zulmani (dark) form, not the haywani (animal) form, who were mujarrad (abstract) among them, you destroyed and rendered motionless. "bi'l-hujaj al-wadihah" (with the clear proofs) — with your clear hujaj (proofs) — "wa'l-barahin al-laihah" (and the evident demonstrations) — you also established hujaj against those zulmani forms. "wa aslama minhum kathir" (And many among them submitted) — many among them also accepted faith and became Muslim. "wa la yunabbi'uka mithlu khabir" (And none can inform you like one who is well-acquainted) — no one else will convey these matters as they truly are.

"wa amma al-mawaqit fa-fiha asrar batinah wa zawahir kaminah" (As for the miqats, in them are hidden secrets and latent manifestations) — the miqat (appointed places for entering ihram) hold hidden secrets and latent manifestations.

How many miqats are there? Five. The most excellent is Dhu al-Hulayfah, which is also called Bi'r Ali and Masjid al-Shajarah. It is the highest miqat, and the Hajj that the Prophet, 'alayhi al-salam, performed — he performed only one Hajj, in the tenth year of Hijri — was commenced from this miqat. Great events are also connected to it. The Prophet had taken Sayyidah 'A'ishah with him, and she had her period at that time. She lamented greatly, and the Prophet said, "When Allah has made you weak and has made you deficient, then you must bear it," admonishing her firmly. He then moved forward, and many incidents occurred with his companions at the miqat.

At the miqat, all our ri'yat and fudulat pass through it. It is said that if a person does not enter ihram at the miqat and proceeds beyond it, he must turn back, return to the miqat, and make the 'aqd-e-niyyat (firm intention) there. Such is the importance of the miqat: Hajj or 'Umrah cannot take place without it — the miqat is the bab (gate) to both. The sixth miqat is a temporary one — that is Tan'im, which is actually Masjid Ali. It has become very grand now; when we first went, it was a very small masjid. This is a temporary miqat in the sense that if a person arrives on the eighth of Dhu al-Hijjah with no time to reach the distant miqats such as al-Sa'il, he may make his niyyat (intention) at Tan'im and join the pilgrimage. And if he has still less time, he should proceed directly to 'Arafat, for standing at 'Arafat is what completes the Hajj. If a person does not reach 'Arafat, his Hajj is incomplete — it has not been performed.

'Arafat is attributed to the Prophet, 'alayhi al-salam. We go to three stations: Mina is our main station and belongs to Amir al-Mu'minin; 'Arafat belongs to Mawlana Rasulullah; and Muzdalifah belongs to Sayyidah Bibi Fatimah al-Zahra.

Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Allahumma ashraf al-fatiha hadiyyatan wa wasilatan saniyyatan zakiyyatan mardiyyatan ila arwah al-khamsat al-ashbah al-athar wa ila ruh Sayyidina Ali bin Hibatullah qaddas Allahu ruhahu wa anwara darihahu wa da'afa 'alayna min barakatihi wa lazzatihi wa ta'yidatihi wa taqabbal minna ya Allah al-ifadah wa'l-istifadah bi-ahsani ma yakun bi-haqqi Sayyidina Muhammadin wa alihi ajma'in wa sallim. Al-Fatihah. [Recitation of Surah al-Fatihah] Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. [Du'as and Fatihas are recited].

<!-- lecture 7 -->

The wilah of a person is accepted in the end.

So, Mawlana Zayd — regarding his saying — a questioner has asked about it. "'alayhi salawatullahi 'alayh fi du'a'i yawm al-arbi'a'" (upon him be the blessings of Allah, in the supplication for Wednesday). In the supplication for Wednesday, as it says: "wa bayanihi fi al-manzilah" (and his statement in the station) — and in the station, those who were second — "wa irsalihi wa muqasimuhu fi kulli sha'n" (and his sending and his divider in every matter). What does this mean? The questioner asks about its meaning. This phrase appears in a supplication, and he asks for its explanation.

"wa hayat al-manqul fi hayatihi" (and the life of the one who is transferred in his lifetime) — the passing of the one who passes during your lifetime. "ma'nahu Harun" (its meaning is Harun) — its meaning is Harun. "wa kana nuqlatuhu Harun qabla Musa" (and his transfer was Harun before Musa) — the meaning of 'the one who is transferred' is Harun, for Harun passed away before Musa. And the Prophet, sallallahu 'alayhi wa sallam, says: "'Aliyyun minni bi-manzilati Haruna min Musa" (Ali is to me in the station of Harun to Musa). The Prophet says that Ali holds the same station in relation to him as Harun held in relation to Musa. "fa-la shakka anna nur al-'azim 'Aliyyun intaqala fi hayatin nabiyyihi al-karim" (so there is no doubt that the great light, Ali, passed in the life of his noble Prophet) — it has therefore become clear, beyond doubt, that the great light of Ali passed away during the life of the Prophet.

Sayyidna Ismail ibn Hibatullah has also noted that the awsiya' (legatees) pass during the lives of their anbiya' (prophets). This pattern holds for the a'immah as well: the passing occurs within the life of the imam or prophet whom they serve.


So, the passing of the honorable Ali also occurred in the life of the Prophet. Outwardly it did not happen in this way, but in reality — what is its reality? `*bi-tafaddul wa-tatawwul* (with favor and bounty)`. So, grant me favor and do me a kindness. `*fa-qad akthartu* (for I have asked much)`. I have begun to ask you many questions. The questioner says, `*wa ana as'aluka...* (and I ask you...)` — do not give the answer in a single sentence, otherwise doubt will remain upon me. `*wa maqal al-summa'* (and a deafening statement)` — and your word, which will be a concise phrase, will not satisfy me. `*Wa man yafta'ir sa'il* (And who is it that asks but a beggar)`. The one who is a beggar is the one who asks. `*Wa man sa'ala dhalla* (And he who asks is humbled)`. And the one who asks is humbled. `*Wa anta al-mukhtar fi al-i'ta'i wa al-man'a* (And you have the choice in giving and withholding)`. You have the choice to grant or to refuse — that is, if you wish, answer my question, or do not answer it. `*Wa bayyin li...* (And clarify for me...)` — explain to me the supplication of Maulana Zayd, the aforementioned *du'a* whose praise I have mentioned, confirmed by his actions, pertaining to the fourth circle of power. You have written that you gave aid in his actions within that fourth power. What, then, are these circles? `*Hal hiya min al-'uqul* (Are they from the intellects?)`. And what does this point to? `*Wa qawl al-natiq li-Kalilah, istaqbili* (And the saying of the Natiq to Kalilah, 'Come forward')`. The saying of the *Natiq* — that is, Rasulullah's saying — to Kalilah, meaning to the jackal: what you, my master, have said: "Come to me." `*Al-'aql al-khamis, sahib al-nur al-a'zam al-anwar, mufid al-khayrat wa mu'ti al-fada'il* (The fifth intellect, the owner of the greatest, most luminous light, the bestower of good things and the giver of virtues)`. The fifth intellect, which is the owner of the greatest and most luminous light, is the bestower of good things, the one who benefits with charities, and the giver of graces and great boons. `*ila an qala... zaharat al-qudar al-Fatimiyyah* (until he said... the Fatimi powers appeared)`. And the Fatimi powers appeared. `*Wa minhu ashraqat badayi' al-suwar al-musawwarah* (And from him, the splendors of the pictured forms shone forth)`. And from it shone forth the splendors of the pictured forms, its new matters. `*Wa qad kharajtu fi su'ali min ma'na fi ma'na* (And I have digressed in my question from one meaning to another)`. I have strayed in my question from one meaning to another. `*awjaba al-hal... kasrat al-su'al* (the situation necessitated... the abundance of questioning)`. My state is such that I ask more and more. `*Wa antum min ahl al-ijzal* (And you are among the people of generosity)`. And you are among the people of great generosity. The writer is also very accomplished.

`*Al-jawab 'ala dhalika al-su'al* (The answer to that question)`. Sayyidna Ali ibn Hibah says: the question you have asked — here is my answer to it. `*wa naqulu bi-'awnillahi al-kabir al-muta'al* (and we say with the help of Allah, the Great, the Exalted)`. I say it with the help of Allah, the Great, the Exalted, `*wa minnati wali al-'asr wa al-zaman* (and with the grace of the Master of the Age and Time)`. And with the grace of the *Wali al-'Asr wa al-Zaman*, `*'alayhi salawat al-rahim al-rahman* (upon him be the blessings of the Merciful, the Compassionate)`. And upon him be the blessings of the Merciful, the Compassionate. `*Wa ta'yid mawlaya al-'alam al-a'lam al-anwar al-sirr al-bahir* (And the support of my master, the most learned scholar, the most luminous, the manifest secret)`. And with the support of my master, Maulana Hibatullah — who is most luminous, whose secret is a luminous secret — through his means I give you this answer. `*Al-muwasil li-suwar* (The one who connects to the forms)`. Such a secret as is obtained by the forms. `*'an qawli Maulana Zayd... salamu 'alayhi 'alayhissalam, al-malik al-'ashir* (regarding the saying of Maulana Zayd... peace be upon him, the tenth king)`. And regarding your statement concerning Maulana Zayd, upon him be peace, the tenth king — `*al-malik al-'ashir* (the tenth king)` — what is meant by *malik al-'ashir* is the Sayyid Da'i. `*fa-dhalika huwa al-sirr al-khafi* (for that is the hidden secret)`. This matter pertains to a great and hidden secret, `*alladhi la yustar bil-aqlam* (which is not written with pens)` — it is not committed to writing. That is the nature of your question. `*Wa la yulqa illa min lisanin ila udhun* (And it is not imparted except from a tongue to an ear)`. It is transmitted only from tongue to ear and is not set down in writing. `*mimman 'ala lahum al-maqam* (from those whose station is high)` — from those whose stations have been elevated.

`*Wa dhalika annahu ilahun al-mufaddal al-awwal al-sabiqah fi al-adwar* (And that is because he is the favored god, the preceding first one in the cycles)`. He is such a one who is most favored, the first, the preceding — he has appeared across the cycles of old. As the Prophet said, `*ana ibn al-karrat* (I am the son of the recurring appearances)` — I kept appearing through the recurring appearances, and Ali was always with me, appearing alongside me. `*istakhlafat al-mim wa muwazin laha fi khafi al-asrar* (the M became the successor, and it is its equivalent in the hidden secrets)`. And the *mim* assumed his successorship `*fi khafi asrar-i-hi* (in his hidden secrets)`. `*Wa kadhalika al-'ayn idh ma istakhlafat al-qubbat al-Muhammadiyyah* (And similarly the 'ayn, when it became the successor of the Muhammadan dome)`. In the same way, the *'ayn* — the *'azimah* — became the successor of the Muhammadan dome. `*Wa raqa ila ufuq al-'ashir* (And he ascended to the tenth horizon)`. And then, having assumed the successorship, he ascended to the horizon of the tenth. `*Al-munfarid bil-ahadiyyah* (The one unique in his uniqueness)`. And became unique in his oneness — there is none like him.

Here a clarification arises: did the *'ayn* take the successorship of the *mim*? Yes, the *'ayn* took the successorship of the *mim*. The word there does not refer to the physical entity — sometimes it refers to the *hijab*, and sometimes when they speak of the *muhtajib* they mean the *hijab*. The *mim* took the successorship from the *'ayn*.

Because the *muhtajib* of Ali — whose name is *'ayn* — is superior (*afdal*) to the *muhtajib* of Muhammad the Arab, *salawatullahi 'alayh*. Yet among the veils (*hijabat*), the veil (*hijab*) of the Prophet is superior to the veil of Ali.

Maulana says regarding the three generations (*qurun al-thalathah*) that exist — they are the Messenger of God and Maulana Ali. He says that the honorable Ali gathered the *a'immah* (Imams), from Hunayd to Hakim, and from Hasan to the Qa'im — he gathered them all. This was a rare thing: before him, no Imam had gathered another Imam who would come after him. But this is the station of the honorable Ali — that he is the first and the last (*al-awwal wa al-akhir*). "I am the first, and I am the last." He gathered the first ones and also gathered those who would come after. Those three envoys (*thalathah sufarah*) he did not gather in this same manner. The honorable Prophet gathered the honorable Ali, and the honorable Ali too was gathered — it is a matter between them. The successorship of the *'ayn-i 'azimah* — the first to assume it was Maulana 'Abd al-Muttalib, after him the honorable Abu Talib, and with Abu Talib was the honorable 'Abdullah — `'indallah` (with God). After that, the third successorship was taken by the honorable Ali, or the honorable Rasul, the Messenger — the honorable Rasul took it. In this way these successorships continue, like a conjunction (*qiran*). 'Abdullah is said to be `'indallah` — 'Abdullah's station is with Allah, meaning with Abu Talib.

`*Wa ihtajab bi-hijabi mim al-Maryamah* (And he was veiled by the veil of the M of Mary)`. He adopted absence (*ghaybah*) through the veil of *mim-i Maryamah*. That is, the apparent passing of the Rasul was not truly the passing of the Rasul — rather, it was the passing of Ali's veil that occurred. In the remaining life that followed, the honorable Ali's veil was at work. After the apparent passing of the Rasul, the one who appeared as Prophet lived for a further thirty years, and there was a gap of thirty years between the honorable Prophet and the honorable Ali. Those thirty years during which he remained alive belonged to the veil of the honorable Prophet and not the veil of the honorable Ali. This is a rare matter.

`*Wa thabata al-mim bi-hijab al-'ayn* (And the M was established by the veil of the 'ayn*)`. And the *mim* remained established through the veil of the *'ayn*. This event occurred on the day of Ghadir.

Is there proof for this in the noble Quran? Yes — for example, the noble Quran states that the wife of the Messenger of God cannot remarry; no one else may marry her, for she is *Umm al-Mu'minin* (Mother of the Believers). Yet the honorable Prophet, peace be upon him, had given the honorable Ali the choice that after his passing, he might divorce 'A'ishah if the situation were as it is. Can one say this is possible? The intellect cannot grasp such things — not while he is working as the soul of the Rasul (*nafs-i Rasul*). For this reason, in the *Mubahalah* too: `*ta'alaw abna'ana wa abna'akum wa nisa'ana wa nisa'akum* (Come, let us call our sons and your sons, and our women and your women)`, `*wa anfusana wa anfusakum* (and ourselves and yourselves)`. For `*abna'ana wa abna'akum* (our sons and your sons)`, the honorable Rasul brought Hasan and Husayn. For `*nisa'ana wa nisa'akum* (our women and your women)`, there were several wives, yet he brought not one of them — instead he brought Fatimah al-Zahra, *salawatullahi 'alayha*, on his own accord. And for `*anfusana wa anfusakum* (ourselves and yourselves)` — "bring our own people" — `*anfus*` meaning one's own self — he brought the honorable Ali. This made it clear that he was the soul of the Rasul (*nafs-i Rasul*).

`*talbisan 'ala qasir al-suwar* (as a deception upon those of limited forms)`. This deception was created for those who were weak-minded — those of limited forms. `*wa-ghusnan 'ala fi midajihi qudur* (and a branch upon... in his disposition... powers)`. And those who had powerful dispositions did not understand what had occurred. These states could be understood only by the highest-ranking believers.

`*Wa kama azhara Maulana Ja'far al-Sadiq al-ghaybah bi-hijab waladihi Ismail* (And just as Maulana Ja'far al-Sadiq manifested the absence through the veil of his son Ismail)`. Just as Maulana Ja'far al-Sadiq manifested his absence through the veil of his son Ismail — this is a well-known matter. The honorable Ismail passed away, but in reality it was Maulana Ja'far al-Sadiq who passed away through his son Ismail. And in the gathering in which the honorable Ismail had passed away and his body lay there, peace be upon him, the honorable Imam Ja'far al-Sadiq said, "Who will become my ransom (*fidyah*)?" All the *hudud* of the *da'wah* who were present — very high-ranking *hudud* — came forward saying, "O Mawla, we will, we will." But Furat ibn Ahmad, a person of the *hudud* of the *da'wah*, sat with his head bowed in humility, thinking: what is my station and greatness that the Imam — addressing the gathering in which I am also present — asked who will become his veil (*hijab*)? This humility was not hidden from the Imam. So he said, `*Qum ya Furat* (Arise, O Furat)`. And at that moment he recited the verse of Surah al-Muddaththir: `*Ya ayyuha al-muddaththir, qum fa-andhir. Wa rabbaka fa-kabbir. Wa thiyabaka fa-tahhir* (O you who are wrapped in garments, arise and warn. And your Lord glorify. And your garments purify.)`. As soon as this verse was recited, people began greeting him: "Ya Imam al-Zaman" — "O Imam, upon you be peace." You see, then, that when the Imam recited a verse upon a *hudud* of the *da'wah*, that person's external garb was transformed. Consider, then, what a state must have been created when the honorable Jibril, peace be upon him, on behalf of Allah, came and recited this very verse upon the honorable Rasul. `*Kulla yawmin huwa fi sha'n* (Every day He is in [a new] matter)`. Every day you have a new station. And our master also says at one place that he never spent a single day in which there was no increase in his knowledge — the knowledge he had yesterday was increased today. This was the state of Rasulullah — a state of great magnificence.

Maulana Ja'far al-Sadiq said to Furat, at the end: "I am you, and you are me." In the same way, something similar occurred in another Imam's time. A Sindhi man had come for the *ziyarah* (visitation) of the Imam and was sitting in his presence. The Imam asked the gathering who would become his ransom (*hijab*), and everyone presented themselves and stood. But the Sindhi sat quietly. The Imam addressed him: "Why did you not stand?" And then he said, `*Anta ana wa ana anta* (You are me and I am you)`. At that moment the honorable Sindhi brother became a veil.

In Sindh too, if one examines the historical record, many scholars have passed, and remarkable things occurred. In the time of Sayyidna Da'ud ibn Ajabshah, a person came to Ahmedabad who was an Agha Khani in religion and a horse merchant by trade — a very wealthy man whose name was Lad bin Dhannu. (`Dhannu` is commonly written in that region; it means rich (*ghani*), *dhan-wala* — one with wealth.) Lad bin Dhannu came to Ahmedabad in connection with his horse trade. He was, however, a man of knowledge. Sayyidna Da'ud ibn Ajabshah met him and was greatly impressed by his character and his learning. When the time for prayer came, Sayyidna Da'ud said, "You may pray your prayer separately, and we will pray ours separately." But Lad bin Dhannu said, "No, Mawlana — I will pray with you, in your congregation." He prayed in that congregation, and afterward he joined the Ahl al-Sharif. Not only did he join, but as he deepened his study, he accomplished such a remarkable feat that it is said of him that he became the *da'i* for the Jazira of Sindh. And it is also said of him that he was the جامع (compiler and gatherer) of a great assembly of poets. These too are wondrous and remarkable states that have come to pass.

The input contains no `<!-- page N -->` markers (only the context-overlap block), so I output the refined prose with no page markers added.

---

And Maulana Ismail was seen in the veil of his father, and it became a mutual exchange. *Fi sirrin jalilin* — in a great secret. *Wa lam yaqif 'ala ahl al-haqa'iq tilka al-asrar* — the people of realities were not unaware of those secrets. *Wa kullu shay'in 'indahu... 'inda rabbika bi-miqdar* — and everything with your Lord is in a decreed measure. *Wa qawluhu fi hadha al-du'a' al-madhkur fi sha'n Musa al-mashkur* — and this supplication is in the station of the thankful Musa, that is, Maulana Zayd's supplication. *Bi-quwwat al-da'irah al-rabi'ah* — by means of the power of the fourth circle — *al-mutasha'shi'ah anwaruha al-sati'ah* — whose brilliant, radiant lights are spreading all around. This is because the honorable Musa was the fourth among the *nutuqa'*, and so the fourth is granted a special power. Among the Imams or the *nutuqa'*, the one who is second and the one who is fourth is granted a particular power. Thus, among the Imams, Imam Husayn ibn Ali was granted such power that he waged the greatest *jihad*. And the fourth Imam, the honorable Baqir, peace be upon him, was granted that power by virtue of which he caused all the sayings of the honorable Prophet to be made manifest, explaining their interpretations and inner meanings. "Ya Baqir, split open knowledge" — that is what was said to him. "Ya Muhammad, split open knowledge." Because of this he became known as Baqir. He conveyed this message to Jabir — to 'Abdullah al-Ansari — having said to him: my son's son, when you meet him, convey my peace to him. And conveying peace means conveying the *ta'yid* and *mawaddah* that you carry; you are the medium for it — deliver it to them. Those were the circumstances.

Sayyidna Jabir ibn 'Abdullah al-Ansari was an elder of the very highest rank. He became a companion of the honorable Prophet and held great love for the honorable Ali. His joining, I believe, was with the honorable Imam Baqir, and he lived an extraordinarily long life. On one occasion — and this is the incident of the honorable Prophet — the Prophet said, "Go to that place." Jabir submitted, "O Mawla, I do not know that place; how should I go?" So the Prophet gestured, pointing his finger, and from that finger a light emerged. He said, "Follow this light — wherever it is visible to you, go that way." By the guidance of that light, Jabir set out on his journey, which was very far. He reached that place and accomplished what he had been sent to do, delivering the Rasul's message and completing his task. Afterward, a thought came to him: in the past, when I had once lost my way and had valuable goods with me, I had buried those goods beneath a bush somewhere nearby. Let me see if that place is still there and if the goods remain. He went and found the place, dug the ground, and found the goods still there. He took them with him, brought them home, and then came before the Prophet. The Prophet said, "I sent you for one task, and you have returned having done two." This was the honorable Jabir ibn 'Abdullah al-Ansari. His grave is in Alexandria — a visitation was made there as well. In whose time did he pass away? In the time of the honorable Maulana Baqir, I believe. A very long life he had. He is visited to this day.

As for his son — 'Abdullah ibn Jabir — he is in Iraq, in the mausoleum of Maulana Salman Farsi. Yes, there are two buried there. Maulana Tahir is also there — Tahir, who was the son of Maulana Baqir, the brother of Imam Ja'far al-Sadiq. He too is there, in Salman Pak, alongside Maulana Hudhayfah al-Yamani. Yes, Hudhayfah al-Yamani is also there.

Hudhayfah al-Yamani holds a station of great significance. He was a companion of the Master and was present when, after the day of Ghadir — after the eighteenth, the day of Ghadir — Rasulullah undertook the journey toward Madinah. The hypocrites, the *munafiqin*, were burning with rage over the appointment of the honorable Ali, and they resolved to eliminate the Master himself. They formed a group and made their plan. It happened to be night, and as they were traveling through a valley, the conspirators had drawn their daggers in accordance with their plan. But by the power of God, Allah commanded the lightning to flash, and it flashed so continuously and so brightly that the Master was able to see every one of their faces. Hudhayfah al-Yamani was with the honorable Prophet at that moment. The Prophet saw all their faces and said, "O Hudhayfah, did you see what I saw?" Hudhayfah replied, "O Rasulullah, what you saw, I saw." The Prophet said, "Keep this a secret; do not reveal it." And so he revealed it to no one. But the honorable 'Umar ibn al-Khattab would constantly ask Hudhayfah, "Was I among that group that intended to kill the Prophet?" And Hudhayfah would remain silent — until finally 'Umar himself said, *Wallahi, ana fihim* — "By God, I was among them." One may well reflect: people of that kind, who conspired to kill the Prophet — are they worthy of becoming caliph?

Now there is a treatise by Nasir al-Din al-Tusi — I can send it to everyone. It needs to be converted into a PDF, which can certainly be done. It is a treatise worth reading. The manner in which he has dismantled the caliphs — and then Mu'awiyah and Yazid after them — makes it essential reading. It is a short treatise, fifteen to twenty pages, but the titles he applies to Abu Bakr are such that they make one's hair stand on end. Likewise for 'Umar and for 'Uthman.

As for whether Abu Bakr is counted among the *da'is*: it is said that he was in the *da'wah*. The reality with Abu Bakr is that from ancient times — from the earlier *da'wah* — he had been a participant in the *da'wah* of Abu Talib. He continued in that *da'wah* and harbored a wish that he himself might be the awaited Prophet. But when the Prophet, peace be upon him, received prophethood and proclaimed the call of Islam, Abu Bakr accepted him. Yet hypocrisy remained in his heart, and its expressions surfaced many times. One expression was that the Master did not invite him along on the migration from Makkah to Madinah; the Prophet had intended to make that journey alone. But Abu Bakr came along under the pretext of buying camels. He had come to know of the plan because the Master, though he had kept the matter of the migration secret, needed a fast camel and asked Abu Bakr for one. Abu Bakr agreed — but charged the Rasulullah ten times the going rate for the camel.

There are various such incidents. On one occasion, Rasulullah was standing in the market and had struck a deal with a camel seller — someone who had come from another city. The Prophet paid the agreed price and took the reins, saying, "Hand it over." The man said, "I will not hand it over. This camel is mine." A strange situation had arisen: the man had received the money but would not give up the camel. At this point, according to the narrations, Abu Bakr and 'Umar were passing by. They asked, "O Rasulullah, what is the matter?" The Prophet explained: "This man came to sell a camel. I liked it, I paid the price, and now he will not hand it over." Abu Bakr and 'Umar then asked the Rasul, "Are you quite sure you paid the price?" — and at that the Prophet fell silent. By coincidence, the honorable Ali also passed by and asked what was happening. The Master explained the same thing to Ali. Without another word, Ali seized the man by the collar and said, "Return this camel." Afterward the Master asked Ali, "Why did you not do as the two Shaykhs did — treat me the way they treated me?" Ali replied, "O Rasulullah, we have believed in you without seeing. We have not seen paradise, we have not seen hell, we have not seen the angels, we have not seen the hereafter — yet we have believed in all of it solely upon your word. Would we then not believe you in so small a matter as this?"

There are many such incidents. Take, for example, the incident of the cave. A great deal is made of the fact that the honorable Rasul was with Abu Bakr in the cave and that Abu Bakr cradled the Prophet on his lap — all manner of things are said that do not hold up. The Qur'anic verse in Surah al-Tawbah says *idh huma fi al-ghar* — that they both were in the cave. This is acknowledged by Allah. And then: *fa-anzalallahu sakinatahu 'alayh* — Allah sent down His tranquility upon him. Now there were two people there, and tranquility descended upon one. No Muslim would dare to say that the tranquility — that special form of divine assistance — descended upon Abu Bakr while the honorable Prophet was left restless and helpless. Tranquility, then, descended upon the Rasul.

One might object: the Rasul is the Rasul, and Abu Bakr is Abu Bakr, and they are different. But consider: elsewhere, in Surah al-Fath and indeed in Surah al-Tawbah itself, Allah says in another verse, *anzalallahu sakinatahu 'alayhi wa 'ala al-mu'minin* — Allah sent down His tranquility upon him and upon the believers. So tranquility descends upon the believers as well. If Abu Bakr had been among the believers, tranquility would have descended upon him too. A *da'i* by the name of Shahriyar has demonstrated this conclusively: since tranquility descends upon the believers — as another verse makes plain — and no tranquility descended upon Abu Bakr in the cave, he was not even within the circle of faith. How, then, could such a person become caliph?

And then, when Rasulullah passed away, a gathering was convened at Saqifah Bani Sa'idah — while the body of the Rasul lay unburied and without shroud. As Shaykh Sa'di writes in his couplet: the dogs of the world, in their greed for worldly power, left the Prophet without his due.

There is also this point: as long as Muhammad ibn Abi Bakr was still in his father's loins, certain things were as they were. As for Abu Bakr's other son, 'Abd al-Rahman — according to the narrations, both were absent in those moments. The narration specifically concerns Muhammad ibn Abi Bakr.

There is also the matter of the gap between the event of Ghadir and the Prophet's passing — two, three, perhaps four months — during which period I have read in history that the Prophet dispatched Usamah ibn Zayd on a military expedition, and these very men were placed under his command. They objected to being sent under his leadership, even though Usamah was — some say seventeen, though the correct figure is twenty-eight and a half years old — the son of Harithah. They did not go. And Rasulullah, in his illness, kept asking by name: "Has Abu Bakr gone? Has 'Uthman gone? Has 'Umar ibn al-Khattab gone?"


And the oath of allegiance was taken at a place called the Saqifah Bani Sa'idah — a place where thieves and robbers, after looting goods, would gather to divide their spoils among themselves. At that place, they decided the matter of the caliphate. While the discussion was still underway, 'Umar ibn al-Khattab immediately came forward and gave his oath of allegiance, declaring, "Whoever does not give the oath, my sword is on his head." This is how the oath was taken. God forbid. Today that place has been turned into a public bathhouse.

<!-- page 117 -->

It has been said that if this was a democracy, then in all three successions the principle of democracy was destroyed — in one case through nomination, in another through a council of six. It was all done in enmity toward Ali. Now, the *da'is* of high rank — were they not aware of all these matters? The ones the Prophet spoke of were indeed aware of all the sciences. Especially Abu Bakr. But the greed of the world and the lust for rank led them to do all of this.

Consider what happened to Muhsin bin Ali. He was so learned, so well-read — words fail to describe it. In the time of Sayyidna Husayn ibn Hasan, Sayyid 'Abdullah ibn Muhammad had been appointed, and Muhsin bin Ali stood behind Sayyidna Husayn ibn Hasan in prayer. Sayyid 'Abdullah ibn Muhammad would stand in such a way that he would draw Muhsin close — half the space for himself, half for Muhsin. Such was their closeness. Sayyid 'Abdullah ibn Muhammad had such great friendship with Muhsin bin Ali that he would always seat him at his side.

On Fridays, Sayyid Muhammad al-Jamali would come — for he came only on Fridays, praying in his own mosques the rest of the time. Being senior, he would sit to the left of Sayyid Husayn bin Ismail, behind the Imam.

<!-- page 118 -->

That was the time of Sayyidna Husayn. When we came again, Muhsin was sitting behind him, and after Muhsin was Sayyid 'Abdullah. Once, when the summers began, we went and prayed in the courtyard. Sayyid Husayn bin Ismail led the Maghrib prayer. Then, in the interval between the two evening prayers, he sat awhile, rose, and went into the prayer niche. We all followed. He called Sayyid 'Abdullah Muhammad and said something to him, to which Sayyid 'Abdullah replied, "You pray your prayer, I will pray mine." At that point, Muhsin led us in prayer.

The affairs deteriorated considerably after that. Yet such was the friendship between Sayyid 'Abdullah Muhammad and Muhsin. When the clarification came, and Sayyid sahib forgave Sayyid 'Abdullah Muhammad — after testing him and Sayyid Ahmad al-Jamali for many years — he performed the *nas* (appointment) and forgave him. So when Sayyid 'Abdullah ibn Muhammad became a *da'i*, Muhsin came to him on the third day. He did not offer the full *musafahah* (handshake of recognition) — he merely shook hands and said, "You and I were at the same station." Sayyid 'Abdullah ibn Muhammad replied, "Yes, it is correct that you and I were at the same station. But from my station, I have built a building. And you — you have remained on that same station." Meaning, you stayed where you were, while from my station I have built a great edifice of faith.

<!-- page 119 -->

Abu Bakr was not among the confidants of the era of 'Isa. They are other individuals — outside the list of the righteous. Among the six names, there are also common names. The teachers of the Rasul, and among them the confidants of the era of 'Isa — the six confidants who existed between the Rasul and 'Isa — were those who gathered the precious treasures of the teachings of 'Isa. Their joining occurred with the Rasul. Although they were the confidants of the era of 'Isa, they are also among the chosen ones of the *dawr* and hold a great station.

*Fa-hwa yushiru 'ala anna kulla 'aqlin min al-'uqul al-sab' al-'izam yanzuru ila man qabalahu min al-rutab al-nutuqa' al-sab'a fi al-sarb wa al-nizam* — this indicates that every intellect from the seven great intellects looks toward the one opposite it from the seven ranks of the *nutuqa'*, in their series and order. The seven intellects: setting aside the First Intellect and the tenth, how many remain in between? Eight. But in that count the first emanated one — the one who became unique — is also excluded. So after the first emanated one, and before the tenth, there are seven intellects. Each of those seven intellects gives material support to the one opposite it among the *nutuqa'*. The third intellect gives support to the first among the *nutuqa'* — that is, Adam, peace be upon him. The fourth intellect, the eternal one, gives support to the honorable Nuh. And so it proceeds.

<!-- page 120 -->

*Wa amma qawl al-natiq li-Kalilah, thumma istaqbili al-'aql al-khamis, dhu al-fadl wa al-fadilah, sahib al-nur al-a'zam al-anwar, mufid al-khayrat wa mu'ti al-fada'il, ila an qala 'alayhissalam wa al-salam minhu zaharat al-qudar al-Fatimiyyah* — as for the saying of the Natiq to Kalilah: "Then come forward to the fifth intellect, the possessor of grace and virtue, the possessor of the greatest and most luminous light, the bestower of good things and the giver of virtues" — until he said, peace be upon him: "from him the Fatimi powers appeared."

*Fa-l-'aql al-khamis 'arrafna fi hadha al-wajh wa al-ma'na huwa al-Husayn ibn 'Ali Amir al-Mu'minin, al-khamis min al-Nabi* — the fifth intellect, understood in this aspect and meaning, is al-Husayn ibn 'Ali Amir al-Mu'minin, the fifth from the Prophet. That is, within the *Panjatan-i Pak* (the Holy Five) — the honorable Prophet, the honorable Ali, the honorable Fatimah al-Zahra, Hasan, and Husayn — Husayn was the fifth. It is a reference to this.

*Wa al-qudar al-Fatimiyyah, hum al-a'immah min awladihi* — and the Fatimi powers are the *a'immah* from his progeny, that is, from the progeny of the honorable Imam Husayn. They are called the Fatimi powers.

*...wa awlad Maulana Fatim al-huda* — the children of our master Fatim, who guides.

*Wa minhum ashraqat badayi' al-suwar al-musawwarat* — and from them, meaning from the *a'immah*, new and wondrous forms shone forth; their advent radiated light.

*Wa bihim taharrakat al-mutaharrikat* — and by them, the movers moved. These are all spiritual matters and joinings to which reference is made here.

<!-- page 121 -->

This passage is found in *Kanz al-'Ulum* — that book written by Sayyidna Ibrahim ibn Muhammad al-Fad. One should note that there is also *Kanz al-Walad*, which is a different work. *Kanz al-Walad* was written by Sayyidna Ibrahim ibn Husayn al-Hamidi — the second *da'i* after the first *da'i* Sayyidna Dhu'ayb — and he wrote it for his son Sayyidna Hatim, to instruct a son who reaches the station of a *da'i* in the things he must know. The *Kanz al-'Ulum*, however, was written by Sayyidna Ibrahim ibn Muhammad al-Fad, who explained therein that the *a'immah* who appeared in succession from the progeny of the honorable Husayn ibn Ali are in one sense also called *A'immah Fatimiyyin* — because they stand in awe and reverence of the station of the honorable Bibi Fatimah al-Zahra.

It is also said that the honorable Rasulullah, whenever the honorable Fatimah would come to him, would often rise and seat her in his own place. The reason was the gathering she carried with her — a gathering of such station that when the Master of the Universe perceived it, he would rise out of joy and to honor that gathering. And what was that gathering? It was the gathering of the *muhtajibin* of the *awsiya'* — a most magnificent gathering. The honorable Fatimah had assembled the *muhtajibin* of all the *awsiya'*, which caused the station of the *hijab* to increase. The states that belonged to Fatimah bint Muhammad were the states of the *hijab*; and when the station of the *muhtajib* increases, the station of the *hijab* increases accordingly.

<!-- page 122 -->

Wherever the footsteps of the Rasul fall, there the footsteps of Fatimah will fall. The *khilafah* was established. And then the *du'at* stand in awe and reverence regarding who should be appointed in the station of the *'ayn*: Bibi Fatimah al-Zahra or Ibrahim Khalilullah? Among them, who is the successor in the *khilafah* of the *'ayn* after the Rasul? They have not clarified this matter. One of the *da'in* — Sayyidna 'Abdullah al-'Ali, I believe — has written a book in which, as the brother Mukarram Saifullah sahib had transcribed, he clarified that the successions of all the Imams until the Day of Judgment have been explained: who will become the successor of the *'ayn-i 'azimah* after whom. A most magnificent book, in which every single point has been made clear.

*Wa bihim tajalla al-'ayn al-'azimah, 'ilmuhu, 'ulumuhu, wa biyutuhu, wa maqamatuhu al-fahimah* — and through them, through the Imams of purity, the Great *'Ayn* manifested: His knowledge, His sciences, His houses, and His exalted stations. The honorable Ali became the *muhtajib* for all the Imams, and they, the Imams, are His *hijab*. Such is the state, such is the station of the Master of the Universe.

<!-- page 123 -->

This matter — the station of the Master of the Universe and the station of the Imams — has been clarified in Surah al-Fatihah in the following way. Sayyidna Hasan Khan bin 'Abd al-Malik observes that Allah began His word, after Bismillah, with *Al-hamdu lillahi rabb il-'alamin* — All praise is due to Allah, the Lord of the worlds. *Al-hamd* is the *ma'rifah* (definite) form; its *nakirah* (indefinite) form is *hamd*. The word *hamd* is composed of the letters *ha*, *mim*, and *dal*. Their numerical values are: *ha* is eight, *mim* is forty, and *dal* is four — giving a total of fifty-two. What does fifty-two signify? It signifies the three — Muhammad, Ali, and Fatimah — together with the forty-nine Imams who extend to Abu al-A'immah. After Abu al-A'immah, the era of manifestation (*dawr-i kashf*) begins: the Mahdi of the end of time will come, and the manifestation will occur. The honorable Muhammad, peace and blessings be upon him, the honorable Ali, and the honorable Fatimah al-Zahra — these three — together with the forty-nine Imams, make fifty-two. It is as though in reciting *Al-hamdu lillahi rabb il-'alamin* we are recognizing and affirming them.

*Wa-'bud rabbaka hatta ya'tiyak al-yaqin* — And worship your Lord until there comes to you the certainty. Where the word *yaqin* (certainty) is used, it also carries a reference to Amir al-Mu'minin. In the literal sense, *yaqin* means death — worship Allah until your death. But it also means: until you meet Ali, for after passing away, the meeting will be with Ali, and he will ask you questions.

<!-- page 124 -->

As it is in the Quran — in Surah al-Zumar, the thirty-ninth surah — when the group is taken toward the heavens, they will be stopped and questioned. The commentators, both Sunni and Shia, hold that this question is the question of the love and *walayah* of Ali. That is established by proof. In your life, did you hold the correct *walayah* for Ali or not? That is the question that will be asked.

As the Master has said: if a person spends his entire life in worship in the blessed gap between the Rukn al-Asad and Maqam Ibrahim — such that his very joints become loose from the exertion — yet has no love for Ali in his heart, then Allah will cast him into hellfire headlong. For the essence of the matter is this: once, Rasulullah asked his companions, "What is the essence of faith?" Someone said prayer. The Rasul said, "No, prayer is not the essence of faith." Another said fasting, another said Hajj, another said jihad. For each the Rasul said, "Not this." Then the Rasul declared, "It is *walayah* and love" — meaning the *walayah* for Ali is the essence of faith.


Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. O Allah, convey the blessings of these holy offerings — a gift and a means, pure and pleasing — to the souls of the Holy Five, our masters Muhammad, Ali, Fatimah, Hasan, and Husayn. Accept from us this benefit and utilization. May Allah sanctify the soul of our master Ali ibn Hibatullah, and accept, O Allah, this benefit and utilization in the best way possible, by the right of our master Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, and bless and grant them peace. Al-Fatihah. Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi rabb il-'alamin. Ar-rahman ar-rahim. Maliki yawm id-din. Iyyaka na'budu wa iyyaka nasta'in. Ihdina al-sirat al-mustaqim. Sirat alladhina an'amta 'alayhim ghayril maghdubi 'alayhim wa la al-dallin. On behalf of Sayyidna, our master, the absolute Da'i, the treasurer of the house of truth, Sharafuddin and Zeeshanuddin, Ghulam Husayn, son of Sayyidna Mansur the sanctified one, and for Walidna Nasrullah ibn 'Abdullah Hibatullah ibn Mansur the sanctified one — may Allah exalt his station, protect him and guard him, surround him and conceal him, suffice him and heal him from the evil of jinn and men, and from the evil of diseases, aches, pains, and calamities. From the evil of the treacherous workings of time and days, from the evil of the movements of hours and moments, and the glances of eyes, and the stumblings of feet, and the machinations of schemers. May He make good his end in goodness and well-being, protect him in the rest of his life, prolong his pure life, and guard him in the fortress of his lofty station. May He make his days pleasant in a good life and in the attainment of ranks and stations. O you who have been guided, may Allah grant us and you to be in the fortress of his da'wah, and may He grant us and you the blessings of his stations. By the right of Sayyidna and our master, the Imam, the manifest one with magnificent authority, upon whom and upon his pure forefathers be blessings and peace. Al-Fatihah. Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi rabb il-'alamin. Ar-rahman ar-rahim. Maliki yawm id-din. Iyyaka na'budu wa iyyaka nasta'in. Ihdina al-sirat al-mustaqim. Sirat alladhina an'amta 'alayhim ghayril maghdubi 'alayhim wa la al-dallin.

<!-- lecture 8 -->

In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful. In Him we seek help, and may Allah's blessings be upon His Messenger, our master Muhammad, and upon the family of Muhammad, and may He grant them blessings and peace.

The sixth question.

I reflected within my soul that I fell short of drinking from the spring's source — that is, from the presence of the Da'i. I was not in the presence of the Da'i, and I did not acquire knowledge directly from him. I know that this was a sin and a failing on my part. And so I ask: will I return, in the fudla al-thaniyah — that is, after my passing, when my raj'at (return) comes, in the second karrah (cycle) — in the same state as those who are present today? Will I be as I am now? Be gracious, then, and inspire me with that which will remove this uncertainty from me, open up this understanding, and make it clear. Lift from me every concealed veil.

I hasten to seek forgiveness and to repent for my sins. Be gracious unto me with guidance and with knowledge. Cause me to be allotted a Hajj — an opportunity to perform a Hajj of one of the Hudud (ranks of the Da'wah) — so that my sins may be forgiven. Or let me perform some action that will release me from the hudud (limits) in which I am bound. Or let me intend to go to the Baytullah (House of God) and reside therein for years, in which I may suffice and abide by the command. And we seek forgiveness from Allah, for I ask about a matter of which I have no knowledge. Unless You forgive me and have mercy upon me, I shall be among the ignorant. I seek refuge in Your pleasure from Your wrath, and in Your forgiveness from Your anger.

The answer to your question: we say, with the help of Allah the Great, the Most High, and with the grace of the Wali al-'Asr wa al-Zaman (Guardian of the Age and Time) — may the blessings of the Merciful, the Compassionate, be upon him — and through the support of our master, the most knowledgeable, the most luminous, who is most radiant, and whose secret flows to the subtleties of the forms.

Regarding your question about the fudla al-thaniyah: will you return just as you are today, as you were in the past cycles? The answer is yes. Just as you are in this karrah at the time of your death, so shall you be born in the future. You will return just as it was in the beginning. If he was a Bab — that is, Bab al-Abwab — in the previous karrah, then he will be born as a Bab in this karrah as well, for the people of the milla. If he was a Hujjah, then he will be at the rank of Hujjah here too. If he was a Da'i, then he will be born as a Da'i. He allots the Hujjahs and he allots the guided ones, and this matter clarifies the principle. If he was a Ma'dhun, then he will come as a Ma'dhun — though progressing in rank after being born at that station is a separate matter, for it is the work of Qudrat (Divine Power) to place one at the rank of Ma'dhuniyyat. If he was a Mukasir, then in this karrah too he will be a Mukasir, upon this foundation. And if he passed away as a Mu'min, then it is incumbent upon Qudrat that in the second karrah, when he is born, he will be brought to the level of a Mu'min.

Those whose raj'at occurs quickly die in childhood — yes, that is a different situation. It has been said that such souls did not get the opportunity to take the 'ahd-e sharif (sacred covenant), or their lifespan did not allow it. So when they come in future karrahs, they will be born in the state of iman.

This rank is not transgressed. One does not move from one rank to another — that is, born at one rank and then arrive at a different rank; it does not happen that way. There will be neither increase nor decrease in this pattern. Upon this very design, the vehicle will keep moving. And he will be born in the locality or town from which he originated — whichever city, whichever land it was where he was born and grew up. According to this course, according to the word of Allah the Exalted, your birth in this karrah will be in that same place. As Allah Ta'ala says: "From it We created you, and into it We will return you, and from it We will bring you forth another time." When we lower the deceased into the grave and place soil upon them, we recite this verse from the Holy Qur'an: "From it We created you" — We created you from this very soil. "And into it We will return you" — you return into this soil, into this grave. "And from it We will bring you forth another time" — We will bring you out from this very place.

Once, someone asked Amir al-Mu'minin, peace and blessings be upon him: "O Mawla, in the state of prayer, the two prostrations — the two sajdahs — that we perform: what is the reason for them?" He replied: "Have you read this verse of the Holy Qur'an?" And he repeated it: "From it We created you, and into it We will return you, and from it We will bring you forth another time." He explained that when Allah Ta'ala said, "From it We created you" — "I created you from this soil" — that is an indication toward the first prostration. And "Into it We will return you" — "I will merge you back into this soil" — that points to the second prostration.

You will be born in the era of whichever Imam al-Zaman's time it was in the previous karrah, and in the time of the Da'i of that era — the Da'i in whose time you were born. They will not respond except to the one who called them, and they will not have iman and will not respond except through the very one who had called them in the past. They will not obey anyone except the one who had guided them.

— As for increase or decrease — the same Imam al-Zaman and the same Da'i in whose time one is born — the nashist (era/sitting) that is... that nashist will not align precisely with the ages, will it?

— No, the point is that the Imam has progressed in rank, but his niyabat (representation) remains established. Whoever becomes his khalifah (successor) and his na'ib (deputy) — he is the Imam. That is the meaning.

— No, I understood that part. But the era and the nashist that will come — according to age — that nashist will not align exactly. The era and the age differ for each individual.

— Some died at a young age.

— Young age, old age — for example, the Imam's age, the Da'i's age, the Mu'min's age, or the age of the other Hudud-e Kiram — the nashist you speak of sitting according to time...

— ...will not align precisely with the period.

— For instance, if the raj'at happened after a thousand years, the Imam at that time would be different. You are saying it is through his niyabat —

— Yes, in his niyabat. It is possible that — he has not clarified it further — after a thousand years, with whichever Imam one appears, that one would be the khalifah of that Imam, or within the khilafat established among the Imams, it could be that.

— A statement came earlier that the thousand-year period of returning is a general principle. As the ranks increase, the period of their raj'at increases — one coming in a thousand years, or two thousand, or five thousand —

— No, that is fixed — for the Mu'min as well as for the non-Mu'min. That thousand-year period is fixed: after a thousand years, one must come again a second time. That is established. There is no change in it.

— The Ahl al-Nass will be separate, yes? The du'at — their raj'at will be delayed...

— No, if the raj'at is delayed, then what of the Mu'minin? The Mu'minin will be born in the era of the Imam or Da'i with whom they had their 'ahd (covenant) or wilayat (allegiance) — in that very era. In the previous era, with whoever they had iman, on his hand...

— For the du'at, their ranks increase very quickly and their time is short — is their raj'at also after a thousand years?

— Yes... for the du'at — though their khalasi (liberation) happens very quickly. As has been said, the Imam, upon him be peace, will not remain more than three days — his raj'at will be within three days, no longer. Similarly, the du'at remain for thirty years; their period in the grave is stated as thirty years, and after that they return. For the Mu'minin it is three hundred years. This is the situation. But that concerns their progress. As for the raj'at itself — because not all things of the du'at progress completely; some things are left behind, and then Qudrat brings them again for purification. It is the same for the Mu'min.

And poverty and honor — in poverty or in a state of honor, or disgrace, being lowly rather than exalted. And with extreme intelligence and cleverness, one lives life. And one becomes eloquent and articulate, or conversely, one's ability to speak and one's eloquence is diminished, one's intellect is light and weak, and the sharpness of the intellect is absent. All of this occurs according to the measure of what was present in him in the fudla al-ula (first form) — his actions cast their reflection here as well, and what his hands wrought and sent forth. According to the measure of the righteous deeds he has performed in obedience to the Hudud, his reward is from the One Worshipped — from Allah Ta'ala. And according to the measure of the good deeds he has done with his Mu'min brother — meeting him with cheerfulness and welcome, with extreme humility, teaching him whatever he possesses, guiding him, and striving to elevate him to the highest of ranks.


And according to the standing and the advice with sincere intentions. And the way this person stands — and with sincere intentions, the life he leads. And the service in the `Da'wahs` (missions). And in the `Da'wahs`, his — meaning in the `Da'wat-e Hadiyah` — the service he renders. And according to the measure of what the man does to the people of the ranks. And the people of ranks, with them this person does good deeds. The people of high ranks who are worthy, the `Hudud` of the `Da'wah`, with them he does some good deeds. According to the time and the moment. And the pressing need. And as the situations arise. And likewise the land-tax and the ransoms. And in this way, he gives his `fiqāqāt` (ransoms). Through which is salvation. Through these `fiqāqāt`, his salvation happens. And success and arrival at the permanent abode of felicities. And he is successful, and he reaches the abode of permanent felicities — the destination.

And the `fiqāq` is also of great importance. Because what happens with `fiqāq` is that our `nafs nāmiyah` (vegetative soul), `nafs hissiyah` (sensory soul), and `nafs nātiqah` (rational soul) — the defects and impurities within them, the flaws — are removed. So the `fiqāq` is also of great importance. Just as with `zakat` our wealth is purified and our souls are also purified, so with `fiqāq` too, our these souls — `nāmiyah`, `hissiyah`, and `nātiqah` — are purified.

And the `ahl-o-'ayal` (family) also — they stay together...

Yes...

Their `raj'at`, like everyone's `raj'at` is happening together... The `ahl-o-'ayal` and children also come together...

No, that is not mentioned here. But...


The matter will be the same. Yes, I am telling you. The same who had come with the Imam or Dai will also come. Yes, but he has not used the word. They — he is a *momin* (believer) and is on the *haqq* (truth) — for all those who are on the *haqq*, the matters that happen with the people of *haqq*... his matter, understand it as this: his track becomes completely different. Then how will his accounts be settled? When will he come, and when will he come again?

For this, what has been said is — look, for example, there is a person who is blind, who is a *kafir* (non-believer). Now, this *momin* brother sees that a *kafir* is crossing the road. So, considering it a work of *neki* (good deed), he helps him across the road. He does not ask him, "Brother, what is your name? What is your religion?" Rather, he performed a good deed from a purely human perspective. In this way, the various works that keep happening in our world, and such *nekiyan* (good deeds) keep accumulating — so in that, how will his full *hisab kitab* (reckoning) be settled? For such good deeds, because the good work that people did, its *jaza* (reward) is to be given to the *momin*. So how is this completed? It is said that for those who are *ghair-e-iman* (without faith), in the second realm — in the other chamber — their good deeds... sometimes it also happens that they had performed good deeds for the *momin*. So from the good deeds of such *ghair-e-momineen* (non-believers), water is made. And after making water, it is brought into his use — into the use of *wudu* (ablution) and other purposes — and in this way, the accounts between them are settled. This has also been said. So the matters that happen at the hands of the Imams — how many such matters happen — the accounts are fully settled. But this is the positive side: he was helped. There are also negative ones. *Qisas* (retribution) also happens. Yes, negative ones also happen. *Wa lakum fil qisasi hayatun ya ulil albab* (And for you in legal retribution there is life, O you of understanding). Meaning, for you, in *qisas* is your life. O people of understanding. You will see how great a verse of the Holy Quran this is — *wa lakum fil qisasi hayatun*. The point is that *qisas* is a very large circle, and our life is running on a very small track, a small line. The circle of *qisas* is vast.

Just as Imam Ali Zayn al-Abidin is famous for this — that he said, "I am afraid of Allah that I may not get caught in small sins." And the example he gave: "I used to watch my mother light a fire using small pieces of wood, from which the fire would ignite. In that same way, I am afraid that I might be caught by small, small sins." Yes. Although the Imam is *ma'sum* (infallible) and has no mistakes or sins, this then becomes a *seerah* (exemplary way of life) — the Imams' *seerah* is formed.

<!-- page 145 -->

Okay. Another matter — we had the wife of an Imam whose name was Aziza Bu. Her grave is also present in the mausoleum of Maulana Shahid Saheb Muqaddas, inside the *rawdah* (tomb). Who is Aziza Bu? She is Aziza Bu bint Qasim Khan, and she is the *zawjah* (wife) of Muhammad Ali bin Swaleh Badri, who is the first *mansoob* (high-ranking official), buried in Ghash. She was so wealthy that she had trade in seven cities. This was from the time of Sayyidna Hibatullah. She used to send her *fitrah* (religious dues) every year, and whatever the *fitrah* was prescribed at that time — whatever the Dai had prescribed — she would send a thousand times that amount. A thousand times more. And in this way, for several years, she sent *fitrah* to Sayyidna Saheb. The *mansoob* of that time was told that he should take counsel from her and keep her with him in consultation. It was said to Shaykh Yasin — who was the *mansoob* in that era — that he should not carry out any affairs of the *da'wat* (mission) without the counsel of Sayyidati Aziza Bu. Yes. Meaning that because he went astray by not heeding his wife's counsel, he was ultimately removed from the *da'wat*. Yes — removed. Such a great station she had. Yes.

For those who go astray, Amir al-Momineen has a saying: do not look at how those who went astray went astray, but see how those who were saved were saved. This is a saying of yours, and in a poem you have said: see how those who were saved were saved — pay attention to that.

So there are many such incidents. Now look at Dawood bin Fee. Dawood bin Fee was a very great scholar. But this was his flaw — he said, "I am greater in knowledge than Sayyidna Hibatullah Saheb." Yes. The *nass* (designation) for Sayyidna Hibatullah Saheb was performed by whom? By Sayyidna Muhammad bin Isma'il. Muhammad bin Isma'il — Sayyidna Hibatullah Saheb was his uncle. Sayyidna Muhammad bin Isma'il bin Muhammad bin Ibrahim bin Muhammad bin Fahd — Sayyidna Ibrahim's son was Isma'il, and he had performed *nass* on him, but Sayyidna Isma'il passed away in your lifetime. So *nass* was performed on your grandson, Muhammad bin Isma'il, who has a very great station. He is the only Dai who, among the 54 or 55 *Du'at* (plural of Dai) we have, led prayers outside the Arabian Peninsula (*kharij-e-jazirah*) — except Sayyidna Muhammad bin Isma'il, who led prayers in Habasha (Abyssinia) by the Imam's command. So Sayyidna Muhammad bin Isma'il had designated his uncle, Sayyidna Hibatullah bin Ibrahim, performing *nass* on him.

At that time, this Dawood bin Fee was in Najran, and his claim was that he was greater in knowledge. Then Sayyidna Hibatullah Saheb had written a letter to be read out to the *ikhwan* (brethren). But Dawood bin Fee did not make that letter public — he did not read it out. Then from Sayyidna Saheb's side, some *ikhwan* went from there; Arabs came here, because in the time of Sayyidna Hibatullah Saheb there was much coming and going, as there were many migrants. So it was found out that he had not read out the letter at all. *Itab* (censure) was then sent down. And he later repented. Dawood bin Fee repented, and Sayyidna Saheb also forgave him, keeping him in the order of the faithful — among the *momineen*. He was given leave to return, and while going back, he passed away at a place called Wasit. He passed away on the way.

<!-- page 146 -->

Abdul Majid and others — in the time of the Imams — went astray. How did they go astray? Abdul Majid, Dawood Qutub — these were people of great station. Look at how they went astray. And the one who progressed and was saved was Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fahd. Look at this: Sayyidna Sulayman Saheb performed *nass-e-sharif* (noble designation) on Sayyidna Ja'far, but he did not write the name of the *mustawda'* (trustee). He left the name blank, saying, "I will clarify it later." And when he performed the second *nass-e-sharif*, he then wrote the name of Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fahd. Yes. Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fahd's hallmark was that he took charge of the *da'wat* and remained steadfast — his feet did not waver at all. For this reason, he is called *Ghadab fil Da'wat* (Zeal in the Mission). And the *jaza* given to him was this: in recognition of the way he cared for Sayyidna Ja'far and safeguarded the *da'wat* — for Sayyidna Ja'far was only eleven years old at the time of Sayyidna Sulayman Saheb's death, and he taught him, educated him, and made him capable — the *jaza* given was that until *Qiyamah* (the Day of Resurrection), there would be *Du'at* in his progeny. This *jaza* was given.

Just as with Sayyidna Ali bin Muhammad bin Walid — who is our fifth Dai, I believe. Fifth. Yes, fifth Dai. They were very great elders. So the *jaza* given to him was this: Sayyidna Ali bin Hatim studied with him. That era had many wars — with the Zaydis, there were many wars. In those wars, what happened was that Sayyidna Hatim, *qaddasallahu rawahahu*, performed *nass-e-sharif* on Sayyidna Ali bin Muhammad Walid. You said to him, "Maulana, please reconsider — why not perform *nass* on your own son?" So Sayyidna Hatim said, "Brother, you know that we did not get a chance to prepare our son for this position, because all our time was spent in wars and their concerns — in that preparation the time passed." You said, "You should at least test your son." So the Dai administered a very difficult test. They asked Sayyidna Ali bin Hatim very high-level questions, and they saw that he possessed great substance (*johar*). He gave all the answers. Sayyidna Hatim bin Ibrahim was left speechless. He asked Sayyidna Ali bin Muhammad Walid, "How did he acquire this knowledge?" You said, "Maulana, during the wars that used to happen, we were occupied by day, but there was not a single night — not a single night — that he slept without my teaching him and his studying." A Dai was prepared on the battlefield. And because of this state, Sayyidna Hatim then performed *nass*. And then Sayyidna Ali bin Hatim performed *nass* on you — on his own teacher. Yes. And the goodwill you showed, with sincerity of intention — its *jaza* was that in your progeny, the *Du'at* of Bani Anf continued. Seventeen *Du'at* came — just imagine, seventeen *Du'at*. And today's Dai is approximately the nineteenth or twentieth, from the progeny of Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fahd. Yes. Sayyidna Ahmed is also from your progeny — from Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fahd's progeny. Sayyidna Ali bin Hasim is also from his progeny — the twentieth or twenty-first Dai. Sayyidna Taher Saifuddin used to recite this verse often for them: "*Antum al-anf wa ghayrukum al-dhanab*" (You are the nose, and others are the tail). Yes, he would often recite it for them.

<!-- page 147 -->

And why did the name *Anf* (nose) come? *Anf* means nose. In the time of Maulana Ma'add al-Mustansir — our eighteenth Imam — a person used to come from Yemen who had great closeness to the Imam. He would stay with the Imam for a few days and then return to Yemen. The ministers (*wuzara*) there — because they had a kingdom, they were ministers — were astonished: "We are so close to the Imam, but who is this person who becomes even closer to the Imam than us? The Imam honors him so much." So one day they asked the Imam, "Who is this person whom you honor so highly and show such regard?" He said, "*Hadha anf min Yemen*" — "This is a nose from Yemen." His name was Ali bin Ibrahim, but the name Anf came from the Imam's own words. Yes. And his progeny were called Bani Anf. Now, that was in the time of the eighteenth Imam, and many centuries later these became Bani Anf. Yes.

Their story is also this: the Rasulullah was engaged in a *jihad* (battle) on a high place, on a mountain, with his companions. He saw that in the oasis below, in the valley, the army of the *kuffar* (unbelievers) was gathering slowly. So he told his companions, "Look, these *kuffar* are coming to confront us. Among them, the one who is visible on a red camel" — and he mentioned his name, perhaps it was Walid — "that person you are seeing is a very base and vile person who has come to confront the Rasul of the time. But in his progeny, very righteous and pious people will be born." Look at the vision of the Rasulullah! This was recorded in history.
