An investigation then began as to whose progeny this was, and it was found that Ali bin Ibrahim is from his progeny — and then the *Du'at* of Bani Anf are from his progeny. How God gives *jaza*. Yes.

But now there is no one remaining from their progeny. There are two houses in Najran which belong to Bani Anf — the entire Bani Anf became Zaydi. You can imagine: a family from which seventeen *Du'at* came, and the state of their descendants was such that all of them went astray and became Zaydi. So — *Allahu yahdi man yasha' wa yudillu* (Allah guides whom He wills and leads astray whom He wills). This is the absolute truth, and it happened so. And there are two houses in Najran which belong to Bani Anf; the rest all became Zaydi.

During the time of Sayyidna Yusuf, when he was appointed as Dai, one from Bani Anf sought to become the Dai — something of that sort happened. In your time there was Sayyidna Muhammad bin Husayn. Yes — Sayyidna Muhammad bin Husayn, in Yusuf's time — who is the last Dai of Bani Anf. Yes, the last one. He is our twenty-fourth Dai, the last Dai of Bani Anf.

<!-- page 148 -->

Maulana, and another — I cannot remember the Dai's name, but they are also from Yemen and they are bankers. I have seen his name in the *Du'at*, his surname is on it. I cannot remember the name, but it is a very fine name. He is very well known — that banker. NCB, National Commercial Bank — he is among its owners. Okay.

Yes. That is also in *Mahfuz* — it is in *Mahfuz*.

Yes.


Is he there? Has he come? He's come, he's come. It is in *Mahfuz*. It is in *Mahfuz*. It is there. It is in *Mahfuz*. No, no — I had mentioned it before that they are also in *Mahfuz*, aren't they? What's his name, the whole... His name is Ali bin Hanzala al-Mahfuz. Yes, Ali bin Hanzala al-Mahfuz. Ali bin Hanzala is a very high-ranking *da'i*, and the *risalah* (treatise) that he wrote — what is its name? *Simt al-Haqa'iq*. *Simt al-Haqa'iq*. And there is another *risalah* in which... there is only one such *risalah* in which the punishments to be visited upon the *kuffar* (unbelievers), the *mushrikin* (polytheists), and the enemies of the *Awliya Allah* (friends of God) are mentioned. Yes. There is only one such *risalah*; I am telling you its name. So Ali bin Hanzala... and Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan — when he would perform the *ratiqah*, he would recite the names of all the *du'at*: from Sayyidna Dhu'ayb bin Musa all the way to Sayyidna Jamali Ali bin Husayn al-Makhrami, he would take their names with their *alqab* (honorific titles). So the *ratiqah*s would be very long — half an hour at a stretch. And when Sayyidna Ali bin Hanzala's name would come, he would say it thus: Sayyidna Ali bin Hanzala, *Sahib Rutbat al-Fadl* (The Possessor of the Rank of Grace). I remembered that he would use that *laqab*.

So in the matter of *fiqah*... Look, Sayyidna Ibrahim, *`alayh al-salam*, Muqaddas — when he went there, to Najran — no, it was not Najran then, it was Badr at that time — after he reached the *da'i*, a few days later the *da'i* said that he had to pay his *fiqah* (religious offering). He was very, very poor. So he said, "Mawlana, I have not paid the *fiqah* yet." And the *da'i* said, "You have to pay the *fiqah*." He had only two sets of clothes: one kept for ordinary days, and the other kept for Jumu'ah (Friday) and special occasions. So he presented that good set of clothes as *fiqah* to the *da'i*. You can imagine — this is also a historical matter — that fine clothing was presented as *fiqah* to the *da'i*, and only one set of clothes remained, which he would wash and wear. In such hardship he lived and pressed forward. The manner of his passing does not come into... yes... how he passed away... he fell ill. At that time there were also epidemics and the like. Yes, it is possible.

Similarly, it has been recorded of Sayyidna Ghulam Husayn Muqaddas that when he would be present with Sayyidna Ali bin Muhsin Shawam, his eyes would be watering — that is, tears would keep flowing from his eyes and they would be red. So Sayyidna would ask, "Is your health all right?" He would say, "Yes, it is all right" — that he had a cold. This went on for several days. Later the *da'i sahib* came to know that where he had his *iqamah* (residence), below it lived those *Udayni* migrants who cooked their food on wood, causing smoke to rise and trouble him. But he had not told Sayyidna sahib that the *Udaynis'* cooking was burning his eyes. You can imagine this. For this reason, in the *Nass al-Sharif* (sacred text of designation), the *laqab* used for him reads: `al-walad al-adib dhi al-khulq` — `al-adab al-allaf al-arib dhi al-khulq al-ajib` (the most well-mannered, intelligent one of wondrous character). A brother of great, great qualities, possessed of most wondrous qualities, the owner of noble morals. He said `dhi al-khulq al-ajib`.

`wa al-fawz wa al-falah fi da'im al-sa'adah. Wa amma al-hajj ila Bayt Allah al-Haram, fa kafa bi qawlihi ta'ala 'alayh` (And triumph and success in everlasting bliss. As for the Hajj to the Sacred House of Allah, the saying of the Exalted suffices concerning it). And regarding the performance of Hajj towards the Sacred House of Allah, the saying of Allah is sufficient, and so too the saying of the Messenger, *`alayh al-salam*: `man arada khayr al-dunya wa al-akhirah` (whoever desires the good of this world and the Hereafter) — whoever intends the good and well-being of both worlds — `fa l-ya'umma hadha al-bayt` (let him intend to visit this House). He should intend to visit this House of Allah. `aman ba'da am` (year after year) — one year after the other. `wa al-istighfar bi khalis al-niyyat` (and seeking forgiveness with pure intentions) — and he should seek forgiveness with pure intentions. `wa yamhu al-dhunub` (and it erases the sins) — that seeking forgiveness with pure intentions erases the sins completely, wipes them out. `wa al-sayyi'at` (and the evil deeds) — and the evil deeds likewise. `Waffaqana Allah wa iyyaka ayyuha al-akh li mardatih` (May Allah grant us and you, O brother, success in what pleases Him) — O brother, may Allah grant us and you success in attaining His pleasure. `wa nazamana fi silk majami' al-anwar` (and may He organize us in the thread of the assemblies of lights) — and may He place us within the assemblies of lights, which are the assemblies of the Imams of the religion, within their illumined gatherings. `wa du'atih` (and His callers) — and of their callers. `wa la yu'akhidhna bi mubighat al-dhunub` (and may He not take us to account for the gravest of sins) — and may Allah the Exalted not hold us accountable for our major sins. `wa satara 'anna jami' al-'uyub` (and may He conceal from us all faults) — and may He conceal all our faults. `wa waffaqana wa saddadna fi jami' al-af'al wa al-aqwal` (and may He grant us success and make us upright in all deeds and words) — and may He grant us success and uprightness in all our deeds and words. `bi haqqi Muhammad wa alih khayr al-al` (for the sake of Muhammad and his family, the best of kin) — yes, `khayr al-al`, those who are the best of kin.

Mawlana, a question: the *salawat* we recite — in that *salawat* we say, `Allahumma salli 'ala Sayyidina Muhammadin wa 'ala ali Sayyidina Muhammadin kama sallayta wa sallamta wa barakta wa rahimta wa tarahhamta 'ala Ibrahima wa 'ala ali Ibrahim` (O Allah, send prayers upon our master Muhammad and upon the family of our master Muhammad, just as You sent prayers and peace and blessings and mercy and compassion upon Ibrahim and upon the family of Ibrahim). So upon Janab Ibrahim, *`alayh al-salam*, what manner of *salawat* was sent down — the one concerning which we say `kama sallayta wa sallamta 'ala Ibrahim`? Is that defined anywhere? No, look — for any Imam, a specific formula of *salawat* has not been transmitted. That distinction was not there for any Imam. But it happened for Janab Ibrahim. Yes.

And this is also there — we say `Muhammadun wa 'Aliyun khayr al-bashar` (Muhammad and Ali are the best of creation) and `wa 'itratuhuma khayr al-'itar` (and their progeny are the best of progenies), and we repeat it, saying it two times. So what does Sayyidna Ali bin Sulayman say regarding this? He says: Muhammad and Ali are the best of creation — the first utterance of this refers to the Imams who passed in ancient times up to Sayyidna Hunayd, and declares that the Muhammad and Ali of the present time are the best of creation among all those past Imams up until *kashf* (unveiling), until Janab Hunayd. Then it is said a second time: `Muhammadun wa 'Aliyun khayr al-bashar wa 'itratuhuma khayr al-'itar` — and this second utterance declares that the Muhammad and Ali who have now appeared, and their progeny that has appeared, are better than the Imams from Janab Hunayd up to Janab Hashim. So it refers to the Imam of the time. In this way we say this thing twice.

As far as I can recall, upon the seventh Imam Muhammad this also... The seventh Imam who is Muhammad bin Isma'il — upon him also comes the other... what? The `Muhammadun wa 'Aliyun khayr al-bashar, wa 'itratuhuma khayr al-'itar`. That which is *mawsul* (connected) upon Sayyidna Muhammad bin Isma'il, *salawat Allah 'alayh* — that when we give the *shahadah* (testimony of faith), meaning `ashhadu anna Muhammadan rasul Allah` (I bear witness that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah), that is one. And the other testimony we give is for Mawlana Muhammad bin Isma'il, who is the *Sahib al-din al-tahir* (Master of the Pure Religion). And look — the one who is the *Sabir* (the seventh), who is the *Sahib al-din al-tahir*, he abrogated the *shari'ah* of the Prophet 'Isa. So he had the authority to abrogate the *shari'ah*, and in the blink of an eye (*chashm-e-zadan*) he abrogated it and then reinstated that same *shari'ah*. Sayyidna Muhammad bin Isma'il. And he holds a great station — for being the seventh, and in this regard, there are four verses in Surah *Qul Huwa Allahu Ahad*. Surah *Qul Huwa Allahu Ahad* has four verses, and in one narration five, since `Lam yalid wa lam yulad` is counted as two verses. But there are four verses. In those four verses, corresponding to the *dawr al-nabawi*, the *dawr al-tayyibi*, and the *dawr al-qiyamah*, there are four Imams who are the very essence, and they are attributed to the verses of *Qul Huwa Allahu Ahad*. The first, `Qul huwa Allahu ahad` (Say, He is Allah, the One), indicates Mawlana Muhammad bin Isma'il. The second, `Allahu al-samad` (Allah, the Eternal Refuge), is Sayyidna al-Tayyib, Mawlana 'Ali bin al-Tayyib. `Lam yalid wa lam yulad` (He neither begets nor is born) is our 35th Imam, Sayyidna Ahmad, who is the *waqih* Imam of Sayyidna Sulayman sahib. And the fourth, `wa lam yakun lahu kufuwan ahad` (Nor is there to Him any equivalent), indicates our 49th Imam — the Imam of today, whose name has not been revealed, whom we call Abu al-Qa'im, the Qa'im of the Imams. So it refers to these four Imams, attributed to the four verses of *Qul Huwa Allahu Ahad*.

The *Maghrib ki tatawwa'* also originates from him, does it not? Yes, the *Maghrib ki tatawwa'*. For the *Maghrib tatawwa'*, there is the saying of Amir al-Mu'minin: `man lam yatatawwa' fa laysa minna` (Whoever does not perform the *tatawwa'* is not from us) — meaning, whoever leaves the *maghrib tatawwa'* is not from us, establishing its great importance. And these four *rak'ahs* of the *maghrib tatawwa'* are attributed to Sayyidna Mawlana Muhammad bin Isma'il. Yes.

And look — in the progeny of Sayyidna Muhammad bin Isma'il, there were three who were *muhtajibin* (veiled) and were also *wasi*s. What a station! His son Yad'a became one, and Ali became one, and then Abu Talib — no, Ali became one. So they all became *muhtajibin*. And then their *hujjat*s were appointed: 'Abd Allah, Ahmad, and Husayn.

*Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim*. *Sharf al-Fatihah* to the souls... Sayyidna Mawlana 'Alim bin Hibatullah, *qaddasa Allahu ruhahu wa nawwara zarihahu wa ja'alana min barakatihi wa a'azatihi wa ta'idatihi wa taqabbal minna al-ifadah wa al-istifadah bi ahsani ma yakun bi jah al-mursalin wa Sayyid al-awwalin wa al-akhirin wa al-Qur'an al-'Azim wa Ahl al-Bayt al-Tayyibin al-Tahirin salawat Allah 'alayhim ajma'in*. *Al-Fatihah*. *Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi rabb al-'alamin. Al-rahmanirrahim. Maliki yawmid-din. Iyyaka na'budu wa iyyaka nasta'in. Ihdinas-siratal-mustaqim. Siratal-ladhina an'amta 'alayhim ghayril-maghdubi 'alayhim wa lad-dallin.*

[Du'a in Dawat ni Zaban/Gujarati begins, very fast and unclear. Only fragments are discernible.] `...Sayyidna Mawla 'Ali...`

*Al-Fatihah*. *Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi rabb al-'alamin. Al-rahmanirrahim. Maliki yawmid-din. Iyyaka na'budu wa iyyaka nasta'in. Ihdinas-siratal-mustaqim. Siratal-ladhina an'amta 'alayhim ghayril-maghdubi 'alayhim wa lad-dallin.*

Shukran. Please sit. Shukran. Shukran. Shukran, Mawlana. Yes, just pray. God willing. Shukran sahib, Nazar sahib shukran. Yes, shukran. Yes, *Allah hafiz*.

Yes. Whose phone is it? It's my phone. Yes, I'll take it.

<!-- lecture 9 -->

As-salamu alaykum, Bidayat Sahab.

A'udhu billahi min al-shaytan al-rajim. Bi-fadhli Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim, wa bihi nasta'in. Wa salla Allahu 'ala rasulihi, Sayyidina Muhammadin wa 'ala aali Muhammadin wa barik wa sallim (I seek refuge in Allah from the accursed Satan. By the grace of, in the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful, and in Him we seek help. And may Allah's blessings and peace be upon His Messenger, our master Muhammad, and upon the family of Muhammad).

Today is the 21st of Rabi' al-Awwal. When the 21st of any month falls, it is among those seven dates in which inauspiciousness (*nahusat*) is gathered — it is one of those dates. But this du'a within it is a very rare (*nadir*) du'a. Imam Ja'far al-Sadiq must have composed these du'as with great subtlety, keeping in mind — look at its tone — that he asks: fulfill my needs, forgive my sins, and I will not rise from this spot until You do this for me. What a strange and wondrous tone it is, just as a child is insistent with his father and says, "Get me this thing," making his father agree one way or another. The tone is precisely like that: do this for me, do that for me, and until You do it I will not rise from this spot. This is a very strange and wondrous tone to this du'a, which is recited on the 21st.

It is also the day marking the passing of the *awsiya'*. Yes.

Generally, the mistake people make in Ayat al-Kursi — I feel there are several points of error. In the beginning there is *bi-'urwatillahi al-wuthqa* (by the firmest handle of Allah), and many mistakes occur there. Then the second place is *li wa lakum wa li-walidayya* (for me and for you and for my parents), where people also say *li wa-likum* — it is not *wa-likum*, it is *wa-lakum*, with a *zabar*. Yes. And *wa man ya'mal mithqala* (and whoever does an atom's weight)... `shahida Allahu annahu la ilaha illa Huwa wa al-mala'ikatu wa ulu al-'ilm... la ilaha illa Huwa al-'aziz al-hakim. Inna al-dina 'inda Allahi al-islam... wa man ya'mal... su'an` (Allah witnesses that there is no god but He, and the angels and those of knowledge... there is no god but He, the Almighty, the Wise. Indeed, the religion in the sight of Allah is Islam... and whoever does... evil). Yes, it is *wa-man ya'mal*. People read it as *wa-min ya'mal*, but the tone of the Quran Majid is *wa-man ya'mal*. It appears in two places — written differently in each. Yes, two places: in the shorter form it differs, in the longer form it differs. In the Quran Karim it is *wa-man ya'mal*, but the one that comes later is *fa-man ya'mal*, while the one at the beginning is *wa-man ya'mal*. The one that appears at the beginning is part of the du'a in the *wazifa*, not from the Quran Majid itself; the one that comes later is the Quranic verse.

And *wa yastaghfirullah* — no: *yastaghfirillah*. Look, in both cases down below it is *yastaghfirun* — *yastaghfarun*.

Another point: a very significant mistake is made when people say *'Aliyyun al-'Azim*, whereas it should be *'Aliyya al-'Azim*. That is the most serious error. Yes, yes. And it comes in three forms in the Quran: *'Aliyyun al-'Azim*, *'Aliyya al-'Azim*, and *'Aliyyi al-'Azim* — *zabar*, *zer*, *pesh* — all three have come, but each in a different context and place.

Here, in Ayat al-Kursi, at the end, when we perform *istighfar* seven times, it is *al-'Aliyya al-'Azim* — with a *zabar*. In the *ad'iyah*, *'Aliyyan* is recited at the beginning. Yes, yes.

And when we recite seven times at the end — saying *astaghfirullah al-'aliyya al-'azim* (I seek forgiveness from Allah, the Most High, the Most Great) — there are several reasons for this. Among them is that in our seven *dawr al-sitr* (cycles of concealment), this existence of ours is the seventh existence — the seventh *zuhur* (manifestation). So we are performing *istighfar* for the six *zuhurs* that have passed, and then for the seventh we do *istighfar* as *al-'aliyya al-'azim*. These are the seven *istighfar*s that we perform.


Another thing, in a previous *majlis* I heard someone say that 'Ali — the name of Mawla 'Ali — is in the Kalima Sharif. Well, that appears in many places: *'Aliyyan waliyyu Allah* (Ali is the friend of Allah). He said *'Aliyyun* is in Surah Maryam. Yes, yes. *'ala siraatin mustaqeema...* (upon a straight path). It is *'Aliyyan mustaqeema*. So the *zer* and *zabar* were changed, but the intention was directed towards Janab 'Ali.

What is the verse? *Inna hadha siraatu 'aliyyin mustaqeem* (Verily, this is the path of 'Ali, a straight one). This is it. *Sadiqun 'aliyyu... sadiqun 'aliyyu*. No, *siraatun*. On that point he said that it is Mawla 'Ali's own testimony that you were *sadiq* (truthful). *Sadiqun 'aliyyu*. He proved it with that. But the word *sadiq* is not used there. No, *sadiq 'aliyyun* or something like that — I will look at the verse in the Quran Majid. *Wa'j'al li lisana sidqin 'aliyya* (And grant me a tongue of truthfulness, an exalted one). Yes, that is a different verse. But this one is *inna hadha siraatu 'aliyyin mustaqeem*. Yes, *sidqin 'aliyya* — on *sidqin 'aliyya* he made that point.

*Al-Su'al al-Sabi'* (The Seventh Question). *Qala ba'du al-mufidina, jazahum Allahu 'anna afdhala al-jaza'* (Some of the benefactors said, may Allah reward them with the best of rewards on our behalf). Seventh question: some benefactors said — may Allah reward them on our behalf with the best reward — *wa kadhalika fi kitab Kalilah wa Dimnah* (and likewise in the book Kalilah and Dimnah). This is the book Kalilah wa Dimnah, which became very famous. It is an Arabic translation of the book Panchatantra, and this work was written drawing on that material. Ibn al-Muqaffa' wrote it. Ibn al-Muqaffa' was a great scholar; he wrote several books — *Al-Adab al-Kabir* and *Al-Adab al-Saghir* are among them — and he also wrote this Kalilah wa Dimnah. But since he was a person living after the time of the Prophet, he also wrote some things concerning the Hudud. So they say that *yawma sa'ala al-natiq* (on the day the Natiq asked) — the mention of which will come — that the north (*al-shimal*) is the path (*masir*) and destination (*ma'ad*) for the opponents (*azdad*) of the A'immah and their followers in the west (*fi al-maghrib*). The north — what north? The peripheries. There is the northern pole and the southern pole, those parts of the earth that are covered in extreme snow. So in the extreme north and the extreme south, the path (*masir*) and place of return — for whom? For the opponents of the A'immah (*azdad al-a'immah*) and the followers of those opponents. Their destination will be there, in the North Pole. Those who are opponents of the A'immah and their followers. *Wa fi al-maghrib wa azdad al-anbiya' fi al-junub* (and in the west, and the opponents of the prophets in the south). The followers of the opponents of the A'immah — their destination is in the west. And the opponents of the Anbiya' (Prophets) are in the south (*al-junub*). *Wa al-tabi'ina bihim fi al-mashriq* (and their followers in the east). The followers of the opponents of the Anbiya' are in the east (*al-mashriq*). This is what has been said. But there is also another explanation: that the opponents — the opponents who are opponents of the A'immah and the Hujjat and the Hijabs of the A'immah — are gathered in *ra's wa dhanab* (head and tail). The *dhanab* (tail) is a more evil place than the *ra's* (head). These are all *wahmi* (imaginal) realities, which lie beyond the celestial sphere (*nitaq al-qamar*). *Ra's wa dhanab* are also called Rahu and Ketu. In the *dhanab*, in the tail — whose destination is it? The opponents of the A'immah and their Hijabs. And in the *ra's* (head) are the opponents of the Anbiya' and the *Du'at*. That is their destination. This is what is said.

That is why in the North Pole and such places, there is no namaz. Yes.

What is meant by *mashriq* and *maghrib*? Those are places, but they are very cold. The north and south are very cold, but *mashriq* and *maghrib* — the extreme east and extreme west — are in a similar state. There is snow there too. They fall within the world's map, do they not? Yes, within the world. That is, within the world, the north, the south, the east, and the west — the extreme boundary points — are all described as bad. It is a very widespread misconception to think otherwise, meaning it is not in our area, because we also come within the east and west if you look at it broadly. No, that east and west refers to the end corners — places like Tibet and Alaska. Tibet is there and Alaska is there — extreme east and west. Alaska is far up there. I mean the extreme, faraway places: where our east ends, or our west ends, or north or south ends. Those places are being discussed — the places always covered in snow, where there are six months of day and six months of night. That would be up in the north and south, where that situation exists. As for the extreme east and west, there are many islands in the Pacific, around the dateline, on both sides — on the Japan side and the America side. Those are extreme positions. The volcanic islands of Hawaii and others are there, but they are all in the middle of the Pacific Ocean.

*Fa-kayfa yunsakhu ma yunsakhu ila hadhihi al-mawadhi' al-nufus wa'l-ajsad?* (So how is that which is transmigrated transmigrated to these places — the souls and the bodies?). So how do the transmigrating things reach there, to these places? And do the souls (*nufus*) of the opponents of the A'immah or Anbiya' reach there, or do their bodies (*ajsad*)? The questioner asks this. And since Sayyidna Abdullah Sahab always used to say that one should rely on the answer, not the question: *A'udhu bika min masiri hadhihi al-hussad* (I seek refuge in You from the destiny of these envious ones). We seek refuge in Allah from the destination — the place — of these envious ones. *Wa a'tasimu bika min fi'li ahl al-fasad* (And I take refuge in You from the actions of the people of corruption). And we seek *'ismah* (protection) that we may remain free from the actions of these people of corruption. *Wa 'ala kam mudadin yulhaquna bi-tilka al-diyar?* (And for how long a period are they attached to those lands?). And for how long do these people — the opponents of the A'immah and the opponents of the Anbiya' — go and remain there? *Wa hal al-riyah yuwassiluhum bi-shay'in wa al-riyah shimal wa junub wa dabur wa saba?* (And do the winds connect them with something, and the winds are north, south, west, and east?). And do winds blow that reach them? And are the winds — which are named north, south, *dabur*, and *saba* — available to them? *Fa-tawallaw 'alayya* (So they turned away from me) — just have mercy on me. *Wa a'udhu bika wa bi-Sayyidina Muhammad ibn Isma'il, wa bi-waladika Sayyidina 'Ali ibn Hibatullah min amrin taqulu 'anni atasawwaru ma laysa yu'dhanu li* (And I seek refuge in You and in our master Muhammad ibn Isma'il, and in your son our master 'Ali ibn Hibatullah from a matter about which you would say of me that I imagine what is not permitted for me). And I seek refuge in You, and in Sayyidna Muhammad ibn Isma'il, and in your son Sayyidna 'Ali ibn Hibatullah, that I may not have such a conception for which I am not given permission — that is, for which I am not permitted. *Wa as'aluka bi-man ya'lu 'alayka ila babika al-'azim wa khalifatika al-karim, an tasturani wa tamhunani ridaka wa sitraka wa 'awnaka wa safhaka* (And I ask You by the one who is exalted before You to Your great door and Your noble successor, that You conceal me and grant me Your pleasure, Your covering, Your help, and Your forgiveness). And I ask You by the one who is exalted before You, up to Your great door (*babika al-'azim*) and Your noble successor (*khalifatika al-karim*), that You keep my faults concealed, that I may attain the honor of Your pleasure, that You keep my affairs covered, that Your help may be with me, and that You forgive me. *Wa anta al-mukhyyar fi al-'ata'i aw al-man'* (And You are the one with choice in giving or withholding). And You have the choice to grant or to refuse — in the answer to my question.

*Al-jawab 'ala dhalika al-su'al, naqulu bi-'awnillahi al-kabir al-muta'al wa minnati waliyyi al-'asri wa'l-zaman* (The answer to that question: we say with the help of Allah, the Great, the Exalted, and the grace of the Master of the era and time). The answer to your question — we say with the help of Allah, the Great, the Exalted, and with the grace of the *Wali al-'Asr wa'l-Zaman* (Master of the Era and Time), by his blessing. *'Alayhi salawat al-rahim al-rahman, wa ta'idi mawlaya al-'alam al-anwar wa sirrihi al-sari ila lata'if al-suwar* (Upon him be the blessings of the Merciful, the Compassionate, and the support of my master, the luminous banner, and his secret which flows to the subtleties of the forms). Upon him be the blessings of the *Rahim* and *Rahman*. And with the support of my master the banner — that is, Hibatullah Sahab — who is very luminous, and whose secret flows towards the subtleties of the forms.

Now the answer is this: *inna intiqaala azdad al-a'immati wa al-nutaqa', wa al-tabi'ina bihim min al-'awam, awan al-ayyam, ila tilka al-atraf al-mash'umah wa al-jihat al-mal'umah, tarmihim ilayha al-'inayat al-ilahiyyah* (Verily, the transmigration of the opponents of the Imams and the Natiqs, and their followers from among the common folk, at the time of the days, to those inauspicious peripheries and the cursed directions — the divine providence throws them there). The opponents of the A'immah and the Nutaqa' — who are very great — their transmigration, and likewise the transmigration of their followers from among the lowly people, leads to those peripheral places which are very bad, and those directions which are very cursed. The divine providence (*'inayat ilahiyyah*) — that is, the divine arrangements, meaning he who has become the viceroy of the Tenth in the earth, that is, the *hudud siddiqiyyah* — throws them to these places. *Wa al-qudrat al-rabbaniyyah* (and the lordly power) — the lordly power carries out this action. *Fa-yakununa fiha 'ala mithali hayawan wa'l-jibal* (So they are in it in the likeness of animals and mountains). And there they exist in the likeness of animals and mountains. As it is said in another book, there are such large chunks of ice, and it turns out that under that chunk is a vast animal which the ice has completely covered. Or there are large mountains inside which their filthy refuse accumulates.

*Wa al-hissu mawjudun fihim* (And sense perception is present in them). And in whomever these things are happening, sense perception is present in them — yes, they feel it. Just as it was said that the person who does not take the *'ahd sharif* (noble covenant) has his *nafs al-hissiyyah* (sensory soul) pervading his body, because of which he remains in a state of torment after death and continues to feel those torments. And this same *nafs al-hissiyyah* — when we say *Allahu Akbar* and perform *dhabihah* (slaughter) — what is happening in that *dhabihah*? We are separating the *nafs al-hissiyyah* of the goat we are slaughtering from it, because of which it is elevated. One day it will come into a human form. Yes. And because of the separation of the *nafs al-hissiyyah*, its meat becomes lawful (*halal*). But it has also been said that one should not eat the meat raw even after the *nafs al-hissiyyah* has been separated from it — it should be cooked and eaten. And even after cooking, the Rasoolullah says that when the steam rises, it should be allowed to escape and the meat should not be eaten in a hot state, because whatever remainder of the *nafs al-hissiyyah* is left is escaping through the steam. Yes.

*fihim, bila intiqal, muqabbalina bi'l-salasil* (in them, without transmigration, shackled with chains). And they are wearing fetters — *wa'l-aghlal* (and shackles) — handcuffs as well — *mu'adhdhabina bi'l-'adhab al-ha'il al-muhin* (tormented with the terrifying, humiliating punishment). And they are punished with a great, dreadful, and horrifying punishment — *bi'l-harri wa'l-bard* (with heat and cold). With extreme heat and extreme cold they are punished. And as it is said, those who reach *saqar* (hellfire) are given the light of the moon during the day as punishment, and the light of the sun at night. Yes.

*al-mu'lim al-nakil. Wa amma qawluka wa hal hiya ajsadun am al-nufus al-marmiyyah fi al-bilad wa al-bu's* (the painful, exemplary punishment. As for your statement: "Are they bodies or the souls cast into the lands and misery?"). Your question was: in these places, do their bodies reach or their souls reach — those that are thrown there with calamity and hardship? *Fa'lam, fa-inna-ma yufariqu ahl hadhihi al-tabaqat illa al-tasawwur al-khabith al-qa'im min nasb li-awliya' Allah wa'l-adawat* (So know, for nothing separates the people of these classes except the vile conception arising from enmity towards the friends of Allah and the enmities). Then know: what is it that separates them from these classes? Their vile conception separates them. The one who harbors a wrong conception — that vile conception reaches those places. *Al-qa'im min nasb li-awliya' Allah wa'l-adawat* (arising from enmity towards the friends of Allah and the enmities) — that which is done in enmity with the friends of Allah. *Wa amm al-nufus fa-hiya tashrif fi tilka al-ajsad al-radi'ah* (As for the souls, they are ennobled in those base bodies). As for the souls — they pervade within their bodies, which are base. *Wa yakunu minha fiha al-wayl wa al-thubur* (And from it, in it, is woe and destruction). Because of this there is ruination, destruction, and affliction for them. *Wa takunu akthafa min al-ajsad li-'adhami ma a'taw* (And they become denser than the bodies because of the greatness of what they were given). And they become denser than bodies — because what the Qudrat (Power) had given progresses and, after a period of time, makes his soul from his bodies. And then this person, out of his own foolishness, is putting his soul back into his body. The favor that the Qudrat bestowed, he did not accept that favor. As it is said: *al-shirku zulmun 'azim* (verily, shirk is a great injustice). Shirk is a great injustice. And Allah ta'ala will forgive every sin — from the greatest to the greatest — except for shirk. As it is said: *inna Allaha yaghfiru al-dhunuba jami'an* (Verily, Allah forgives all sins). Allah has the power to forgive every sin, but He will not forgive shirk. Yes.

Here the state is *la yahya wa la yamut* (neither living nor dead). Yes — here, when they go into punishment, it is *la yahya wa la yamut*. Just as in Surah al-A'la it is said: *la yamutu fiha wa la yahya* (he will neither die therein nor live). They neither live nor die. It has been said about them that in their punishments, every day they are killed a thousand times and then revived a thousand times. Yes, this is the state. How immense the torment must be, and how much pain they are in.

This writer must not have been a great scholar — he has made many mistakes. The correct reading is *wa al-wayl wa al-thubur*, but here *shol* is written. Amazing. Yes, I also notice that many mistakes have been made by the scribes in their transcription.


*Wa amma qawluka 'ala kam mudad yulhaquna...* (As for your statement, for how long a period are they attached...). Now here, *kam madad* is written. See? It is *mudadd*, meaning the plural of *muddat* is *mudadd*. And *madad* is a different thing. For how many ages will they be attached to those peripheries? That is, when will they reach there? *Fa-ba'da an yaktatifu adhraw al-silsilah fi kulli nawmin minha sab'ina qamisan bila khilaf* (So after they have snatched the arms of the chain — in each sleep of it, seventy shirts, without disagreement). And what I have learned from it is in the books of the *awliya' Allah* and the *hudat*, upon them be the salutations of their Imam, and blessings be upon them. As for your statement about the winds, they are different in all states. Among them are some ascending, noble ones. And among them, some winds also ascend. *Ittasalat bi-kulli latif* (connected with every subtle thing) — which meet with certain subtleties. *Wa ma kana min al-habitin ittasala bi-ma yula'imuhu min al-zalimin* (And what was of the descending ones connected with what suits it from among the oppressors). And among them there are also descending winds, whose connection is with the oppressors. *Wa qad yattasilu al-salihu bi'l-talih* (And the righteous may connect with the corrupt). And until the righteous connects with the corrupt — I do not know what this means — *wa yakhussu bi'l-qawl al-wadih li-mujibat qadhat ba'da asbabin* (and he specifies with the clear statement the requirements that some causes have decreed). For what reason it happens, Allah knows best. *Min ahl al-jahl wa al-iktisab. Fa-khudh dhalika bi'l-wajiz min al-qawl* (from the people of ignorance and acquisition. So take that with the concise statement). So take this from the concise statement I have mentioned. *Wa lillahi subhanahu al-quwwah wa al-hawl* (And to Allah, the Glorified, belongs the power and the might). For Allah alone is the power and the might.

And we who say *la hawla wa la quwwata illa billahi al-'aliyyi al-'azim* (there is no power and no strength except in Allah, the Most High, the Most Great) — this is not a verse of the Quran Majid. The verse of the Quran Majid is, as it appears perhaps in Surah Bani Isra'il, *la quwwata illa ma sha' Allah* (there is no power except what Allah wills). That is it: *la quwwata illa ma sha' Allah*. But when the Master of the Universe began using *la hawla wa la quwwata illa billahi al-'aliyyi al-'azim* in his du'as, this saying became so renowned, and Allah ta'ala liked it so greatly, that He made it common in du'as. You will observe that in du'as — very often, at their conclusion — this phrase appears: *la hawla wa la quwwata illa billahi al-'aliyyi al-'azim*.

The realities of it that have been explained are as follows. One meaning of *la hawla wa la quwwata* is: I have no strength to refrain from evil and to do good, except what Allah ta'ala grants. This is the outward meaning. The other meaning is that there is no power, no might, except that which is through Allah, *al-'Aliyy al-'Azim*. And in that, what has been said? That there is no power in the external realm (*bab-i zahir*), nor in the internal realm (*bab-i batin*), to be able to form a *haykal* (spiritual form). That is, the *suwarat al-nayyirah* (luminous forms) do not reach the Imam. The form of the deceased, one day, through the *hudud daniyyah* and *hudud 'aliyyah*, gathers with the *da'i*, and continues upward until it reaches the *Bab al-Abwab* (Gate of Gates), which is the *bahr al-zakhar* (overflowing ocean), also called *fasl al-khitab* (the decisive speech). There, all the forms of the believers gather with the *Bab al-Abwab*. It is a vast gathering. Yet within that gathering, despite its immensity, it does not possess the form of a *haykal*. A *haykal* is that which is in human form. But when this gathering is prepared, the present Imam, 'alayhis salam, receives it and grants it to his son. The son, who until that moment was Imam *bi'l-quwwah* (potentially), becomes, through this gathering's connection to him, Imam *bi'l-fi'l* (actually). The present Imam then passes away, and his *Bab al-Abwab* also passes away. The *qalb al-haykal* (heart of the *haykal*) of the son — who has now become the Imam — is formed by the *Bab al-Abwab*. That is to say, the highest form within the *haykal* that the son of the Imam now holds is that of the *Bab al-Abwab*, who rendered a great service, being the greatest servant of the Imam by gathering such an immense assembly through his arrangements.

Now, what is being said here? *La hawla wa la quwwata illa billah*. There is no power and no strength except in Allah, by His grace. So who is meant by Allah? He is the Imam of the era and time. And who are *'Aliyy al-'Azim*? They are the *ayn al-a'immah* (essence of the Imams), who are the manifestation of 'Ali, 'alayhis salam. All the A'immah are therefore the *hijabs* (veils) of *'Aliyy al-'Azim*. And who is meant by *'Aliyy al-'Azim*? The *ayn al-a'immah*. All the A'immah are the *hijabs* of the *ayn al-a'immah*. In this, the entire picture emerges — from the believer to the *ayn al-a'immah* — revealing the process that is unfolding.

*Ya Allahumma, ashrah al-fatihah hadiyyatan wasilatan suniyyatan zakiyyatan mardiyyatan ila ruhi Sayyidina 'Ali ibn Hibatullah, qaddasa Allahu ruhahu wa anwara darihahu wa da'afa 'alayna min barakatihi wa ta'idatihi wa 'inayatihi wa taqabbal minna al-ifadah wa al-istifadah bi-haqqi Sayyidina Muhammadin wa 'alihi wa barik wa sallim. Al-Fatihah.*

<!-- lecture 10 -->

*A'udhu billahi minash shaitanir rajim. Bi fadli Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Wa bihi nasta'in wa salla'llahu 'ala rasulihi Sayyidna Muhammadin wa 'ala ali Muhammadin wa barik wa sallim.*

*Al-su'al al-thamin 'an al-jawahir wa'l-yawaqit* (The eighth question, concerning jewels and rubies). The eighth question concerns jewels and rubies — the stone — and is related to that.

Now, every thing has a *ghayah* (ultimate point), meaning its limit and highest expression. So *ghayat al-ahjار* (the ultimate point of stones), *ghayat al-ma'dan* (the ultimate point of minerals) — that is, among the minerals found inside the earth, or among stones, what is their *ghayah*, the most noble among them? It is the ruby (*yaqut*). Among all jewels, all minerals, and all stones (*ahjar*), their *ghayah* is the ruby.

In the same way, *ghayat al-haywan* (the ultimate point of animals) — among all animals, their *ghayah* is the elephant (*fil*). It has been said that the reason is the elephant's intelligence, for the elephant is the most intelligent among animals, and by virtue of that intelligence it becomes the *ghayah*.

Likewise, *ghayat al-insan* (the ultimate point of man) is the *mu'min* (believer). The *ghayah* of man is the believer. Consider the lofty station of the *mu'min*: that Allah Ta'ala has used this single word for us believers as well, including us in the rank of those honored with *Ahl al-Jalis*. And wherever in the Glorious Quran the phrase *Ya ayyuha'lladhina amanu* (O you who believe) appears, its primary intention is Amir al-Mu'minin and those who lead the believers, the *amir*.

Allah Ta'ala Himself has called Himself *Mu'min*. Among His names is *al-Mu'min*. So there are ranks. This is the condition of the matter. And *awwalu man amana* (the first who believed) — the final two verses of Surat al-Baqarah are extremely noble, and the Rasul has said that one should recite them daily before sleeping. For when the Rasulullah, sallallahu 'alayhi wa alihi wasallam, journeyed on the Mi'raj, Allah bestowed two things upon him — things that, as he himself said, had not been bestowed upon any prophet before him. What were those two things? One was Surat al-Fatiha, which was bestowed upon him; and the second was the last two verses of Surat al-Baqarah. These were bestowed. Although we, in *sadaqa'llah*, recite the last three verses, the specific mention is of the last two. Within them, the phrase *awwalu man amana* has been used — the first who believed. The numerical value derived from *awwalu man amana* equals the numerical value of 'Ali ibn Abi Talib. And all the interpretations pointing to Janab 'Ali — look at them. The numerical value of *awwalu man amana* and the numerical value of *'Ali ibn Abi Talib* are equal.

Concerning the jewels (*jawahir*) and rubies (*yawaqit*), a question was asked: *hal hiya tashtami'u ila mahallin ma'ruf wa makanin mawsuf* (Do they gather in a known place and a described location?) — does their gathering occur at a special, famous, described place? *Wa tanhallu wa tanqasimu 'ala'l-kawakib* (And do they dissolve and get distributed upon the planets?) — or are they ground up and distributed among the *kawakib*? *Wa idha kana yashtami'u fa-'ala kam muddatuha* (And if it gathers, then for what duration?) — if their gathering occurs in the *kawakib*, for how long does it persist? *Wa kayfa intiqa'uha* (And how is its selection?) — how is the selection within the *kawakib* carried out? *Hal hiya ta'udu ila mawdi'iha'l-qadim* (Does it return to its ancient place?) — after being ground and entering the *kawakib*, will these jewels and rubies gather again in their original state? This is what the questioner has asked. *Tafaddalu 'alayya* (Please favor me) — favor me, for you are worthy of it. *Fa'l-'abdu yas'alu malikahu min fadlihi* (For the servant asks of his master from his bounty) — and the servant asks his master concerning his bounty.

*Al-jawab 'ala dhalika's-su'al* (The answer to that question). The answer: *naqulu bi-'awni'llahi'l-Kabiri'l-Muta'al* (we say with the help of Allah, the Great, the Most High) — we say this with the help of Allah, who is the Great, the Most Exalted. *Wa mannat Waliyyi'l-'Asri wa'z-Zaman 'alayhi salawatu'r-Rahimi'r-Rahman* (And by the grace of the Master of the Era and the Time, upon him be the blessings of the Merciful, the Compassionate) — and by the grace, meaning by the gift, of the one who is the *Wali al-'Asr wa'l-Zaman*, may the blessings of Al-Rahim and Al-Rahman be upon him. And by the *ta'id* (aid) of *Mawlaya al-'Alam, 'Alam al-Huda* — by the aids of Sayyidna Hibatullah Saheb, who are most luminous. *Wa sirruhu'l-bahir al-sari ila lata'ifi's-suwar* (And his manifest secret, flowing to the subtleties of the forms) — and from his manifest secret, which flows and streams toward the subtleties of the forms — *anna'l-yawaqit wa'l-jawahir al-hisan tuhfazu fi khaza'ini'l-muluk* (that the fine rubies and jewels are preserved in the treasuries of kings). Fine jewels and rubies have their protection in the treasuries of kings. *Wa tusan* (And they are protected) — there, their complete protection is maintained. *Thumma tansha'u mata sha'a'l-mudabbir* (Then it arises whenever the Disposer wills) — they remain for a period, set in crowns and placed at positions of honor, and then, when the Disposer wills, they begin to be ground up and enter a state of dissolution. *Wa yasiru fi'l-amakinish-sharifah* (And it travels in the noble places) — they then travel to noble and exalted places of distinction. *Wa mata abala'l-aflak wa'l-kawakib sarat hadhihi li-taghliba fi'l-khidmah* (And when the celestial spheres and planets wore out, these traveled to prevail in service) — and when the *aflak* and *kawakib* fall, when each must ascend toward the spiritual realm, every ascending thing must touch the earth before its ascent — except the *Falak al-Muhit* (the encompassing sphere), which, from where it already stands, becomes the Universal Intellect (*'Aql al-Kulli*) and enters the spiritual realm directly. But all other things — whether the moon, the sun, or the *aflak* — must touch the earth, and only thereafter does their progress occur.

[The discussion notes that meteorites fall from above to the earth and become extremely valuable — this is consistent with the same principle. Regarding the elephant's status as *ghayah* among animals: its physical strength is also immense, but it is by virtue of its length, breadth, and shrewdness that it is honored. The horse, too, holds its own place, but the elephant possesses a greater shrewdness than the horse.]

So these *aflak* and *kawakib* ascend — when they fall, they ascend. And what takes their place? The jewels and rubies assume their *khilafat* (vicegerency) in service. *Bi-hikmatin muharriki'l-'awалim* (By the wisdom of the Mover of the worlds) — with a wisdom belonging to the Mover of all things. *Wa baqiyat muddata'l-kawni'l-adami* (And it remained for the duration of the Adamite existence) — for the duration of a *kawr* of Adam, they remain. That is, *khilafat*s are established: when the *aflak* and *kawakib* progress, the jewels and rubies take their *khilafat*, and for one *kawr* of Adam, their duration persists. *Ila an tawaffa ma 'alayha min al-khidam* (Until it fulfilled the service that was upon it) — until their services are completed. *Wa yajri'l-amru 'ala hadhihi'l-khutiyyah hatta yас'ada'l-'alamu bi'l-kulliyyah* (And the matter proceeds along this path until the world ascends in its entirety) — and in this way the matter continues until the entire world ascends, which happens at the end of a *kawr*. *Li-qawli asdaq al-qa'ilin* (As per the saying of the most truthful of speakers) — as Allah Ta'ala says: *kama bada'na awwala khalqin nu'iduhu* (As We began the first creation, We will repeat it) — just as We began the first creation, *wa'dan 'alayna* (a promise upon Us), this matter is Our promise, *inna kunna fa'ilin* (indeed We are the performers of it) — and We will do it again. This coming and going of the *kawr*, all these things will keep unfolding.

The verse from Surah Ya-Sin also speaks to this — that He will create again. *Awa laysa'lladhī khalaqa's-samawati wa'l-arda bi-qadirin 'ala an yakhluqa mithlahum, bala wa huwa'l-khallаqu'l-'alim* (Is not He who created the heavens and the earth able to create the like of them? Yes, and He is the knowing Creator). He is the *Khallaq al-'Alim*. Did not Allah create the heavens and the earth? He is able to create them again. The same system will be recreated. After each *kawr*, a new system comes.

<!-- page 237 -->

*Al-su'al al-tasi' 'an ziyarat al-qubur* (The ninth question, concerning the visitation of graves). Concerning the visitation performed at graves — *kayfa dhalika* (how is that?), *wa ma yahduru'l-qasidina li'l-ajsman* (and what attends those who visit the bodies?) — when visitors present themselves at the graves of those buried therein, *kayfa shu'uru'l-maqsud* (how is the feeling of the one intended?) — do the people whose graves are being visited perceive that a visitor has come to them?

And concerning Sayyidna 'Abdullah ibn Thаmir — he was a very high-ranking elder, a *mubashshir al-nabi* (one who gave glad tidings of the prophet). He lived before the Prophet, 'alayhis salam — his birth and death both preceded the Prophet — and he gave glad tidings of the coming of the Huzur. He died in Najran, at the place of *Ukhdud*, which is mentioned in Surah al-Buruj. At that place his death occurred. In that era, we saw his grave, and it was left in a very poor condition — merely a mound of earth, with no care given to it. But later, I saw in pictures that it has been beautifully restored. When we used to travel for *da'wat*s in the time of Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan and had to pass through *Ukhdud*, Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan would always recite an *iradah* (a devotional prayer) for him — he would recite a du'a, recite an *iradah*.


So, `wa qad ṣāra lahu hādhihi'l-muddah al-madīdah` (and for him this long period has passed). A long period has passed, the questioner says. I have started asking you many questions and have gone beyond my means, but my state is such that I am deeply troubled. So the questioner continues asking. And you are from the people of favor and bounty (`ahl al-faḍl wa'l-nawāl`) — people of generosity and givers of gifts.

`Al-jawāb 'alā dhālika's-su'āl` (The answer to that question). My answer to your question is that `naqūlu bi-'awni'llāhi'l-Kabīri'l-Muta'āl` (we say with the help of Allah, the Great, the Most High) — we speak with the help of Allah, who is the Great, the Most High. `Wa mannat Waliyyi'l-'Aṣri wa'z-Zamān` (And by the grace of the Master of the Era and the Time), and by the gift of the Master of the Era and the Time, `alayhi ṣalawātu'r-Raḥīmi'r-Raḥmān` (upon him be the blessings of the Merciful, the Compassionate). `Wa tā'īdi Mawlāyī al-'Alam` (And by the aid of my Master, the distinguished one) — by the aid of my Master, Mawlana Sayyidna Hibatullah. `Wa sirruhu'l-bāhir bi-nūri'n-naẓm` (And his manifest secret with the light of the order) — and from your manifest secret, `anna ziyārat al-qubūr sirrun mastūr` (that the visitation of graves is a concealed secret), `wa huwa'l-muttaṣil bi-dhālika'l-ḥaddi 'ala'l-ghurūr` (and it is connected to that rank over deception). The concealed secret of the visitation of graves is that the one who visits is receiving emanations (`fuḍūl`) from the rank of the one whose grave is being visited — a high rank indeed. `Li-yashfa'a lahu 'inda jāmi'i'ṣ-ṣuwar` (So that he may intercede for him at the gathering of the forms) — so that the one visited may intercede for the visitor in those gatherings of `ṣuwar` where he is present. The higher the rank, the greater their spiritual powers. In life, the `hudūd` of the Dawah were angels `bi'l-quwwah` (in potential), but after death they become angels `bi'l-fi'l` (in actuality). It has also been stated elsewhere that when a visitor visits, the one visited senses the visit. And as has been said here, the luminous form (`ṣūrat nayyirah`) of the one being visited is always turned towards its `fuḍalā'` (spiritual particle). Just as we, wherever we may be sitting, keep our thoughts on our home and the things within it — even when we are far from them, our minds remain with what is most precious to us — so too the one who has passed away keeps their luminous form oriented towards what is most precious to them: their `fuḍalā'`, which is advancing. They desire that this `fuḍalā'` should progress as swiftly as possible and ascend into the `aflāk`. Therefore, through their luminous form, which resides within the `hiyākil` (spiritual structures) or accompanies the `hudūd 'āliyah` (higher ranks) on its way toward the `Bāb al-Abwāb` (Gate of Gates), an `ishrāq` (illumination) is continuously emanating. When a believer enters the circle of that `ishrāq`, he too receives benefit, and the one visited intercedes for him.

As the Master of the Universe stated: when children visit the graves of their parents, the parents sense their arrival and are as joyful as they were when their children were small and would come and sit in their laps. That same joy is felt by the mother and father. This is the saying of the Rasul — that parents become extremely happy upon the visitation of their children and feel pleasure. And when the children pray for them, recite Yasin, or perform `naẓr` and `niyāz`, the parents perceive all of this. This connects to the verse in Surah Bani Isra'il: `Rabbirḥamhumā kamā rabbayānī ṣaghīrā` (My Lord, have mercy upon them as they brought me up [when I was] small). People often mispronounce it as `rabbiyānī`, but the correct form is `rabbayānī`. So: O Allah, have mercy on them just as they showed mercy to me when I was small. This `du'ā` should be recited both during their lifetimes and after their deaths. Mercy is not reserved for after death alone — when parents are still living, children should pray for them with this Quranic supplication: O Allah, have mercy on them as they had mercy on me in my childhood.

[Side-discussion begins]
If the visitor is of a higher rank, then the matter is reversed — the aid and help flow from the higher visitor to the lower one, just as water always flows from a higher surface to a lower one.
The grave of a great elder, a prophet — for them there are holy traces. Their `fuḍalā'` is no longer there, I understand. But there are traces. A trace would still signify something?
Yes, traces remain. Even if there is no `fuḍalā'`, the `āthār` endure. And it is on account of the `āthār` that the blessings of the grave persist. Just as a bottle of `'iṭr` (perfume), after the perfume has been emptied out, still retains the fragrance — in the same way, the analogy is given that even after the `fuḍalā'` has progressed onward, its `āthār` remain at the grave. Indeed, the very site of the grave will become the place of its future `futteh`.
Yes, yes.
[Side-discussion ends]

`Wa li-yashfa'a lahu 'inda majāmi'i'ṣ-ṣuwar, wa li-yakshifa 'anhu mā 'arāhu min funūni'l-maṣā'ib` (So that he may intercede for him at the gathering of the forms, and so that he may remove from him the various types of calamities that have afflicted him). To the extent that the various calamities befalling the visitor are removed by the one visited. This connects to our understanding that we go to the graves of the `awliyā' Allāh` (saints of Allah) and say: you are the means (`wasīlah`) through which Allah Ta'ala may hear our prayers and help us — for Allah alone is the one who helps, but you have become the intermediary. This `wasīlah` is itself a profound matter, as it appears in the Quran: `Wa'btaghū ilayhi'l-wasīlah` (And seek the means of approach to Him). Generally, the misguided among the common people (`'awām al-nās`) misinterpret this: they claim that `Wa'btaghū ilayhi'l-wasīlah` means to reach Allah through the Quran. But this is not its meaning. `Wa'btaghū ilayhi'l-wasīlah` means to reach Allah through the means — that is, through the Rasul and the Ahl al-Bayt, or through their followers who are the `du'āt`, the `ḥujjat`, and the `imām`. Through them, one reaches Allah. For the `ma'rifat` (gnosis) of the Worshipped (`ma'būd`) is the `ma'rifat` of the `hudūd` (ranks of the Dawah). The `ma'rifat` of the Worshipped, of Allah, is contained within the `ma'rifat` of the `hudūd`.

`Wa mā 'alima bihi min jamī'i'l-ma'āqib` (And what he knew of all the consequences) — whatever affliction is befalling the visitor from various circumstances — `li-kawnihi yushrifu 'alā fuḍalatihi` (because he is overseeing his `fuḍalā'`). The one whose grave is being visited has their `ṣūrat sharīf` exerting `ishrāq` upon their `fuḍalā'`. `Wa yaḥummu 'alayhā` (And he cares for it) — he is deeply solicitous toward his `fuḍalā'`. `Wa yudabbiruhā` (And he manages it) — he is managing it, directing how to advance the `fuḍalā'` as swiftly as possible. `Wa yaḥḍuru'l-qāṣidīna ilayhā` (And the visitors attend to it) — the visitors present themselves in relation to this `fuḍalā'`. These blessings are then obtained by the visitors, because the `ishrāq` of the visited is directed toward their `fuḍalā'`, and within that circle the visitors arrive and benefit.

It is also the case that the lowest `ishrāq` occurring — beyond which higher `ishrāq`s also operate — is the `ishrāq` of a believer's own `ṣūrat sharīf` upon his body. Although his `ṣūrat sharīf` is not yet fully formed — it remains at the stage of `nafs nāmīyah` (vegetative soul), `nafs ḥissīyah` (sensory soul), and `nafs nāṭiqah` (rational soul) — nonetheless, because the `nafs nāṭiqah` is a `ṣūrat sharīf` `bi'l-quwwah` (in potential), its `ishrāq` is already operating upon the body. This is the lowest `ishrāq`. Above it come the higher `ishrāq`s of the Imam and others.

Consider that the believer has been placed within Surat al-Fatiha. How many verses does Surat al-Fatiha have? Seven verses. But its words (`kalimāt`) are twenty-five. And those twenty-five words correspond to the twenty-five prophets mentioned in the glorious Quran. Although there are 124,000 prophets, Allah Ta'ala has mentioned only twenty-five in the Quran — and Surat al-Fatiha likewise contains exactly twenty-five words. These twenty-five words indicate twelve `hudūd` of the `jazā'ir` (islands) and twelve `hudūd` of the `ḥaḍarāt` (presences) — that is, the `du'āt` of the `jazā'ir`, who are twelve, and the `du'āt` of the `ḥaḍarāt`, who are twelve. And their leader, the head of these twenty-four, is the Imam of the Time, making twenty-five in all. The twelve `hudūd` of the `jazā'ir` — the `du'āt` of the `jazā'ir` — each carry out their `da'wat` at their own places, in their own languages, individually. The `du'āt` of the `ḥaḍarāt`, however, operate where the manifestation of the Imam of the Time is present, and so their `da'wat` is a collective one, not individual. This is why, when we pray the `tahajjud` prayer, we offer two-`rak'at` sets — six `salām`s in all — because the `du'āt` of the `jazā'ir` are connected in pairs: each `dā'ī` has a companion. But the `du'āt` of the `ḥaḍarāt` carry out the `da'wat` collectively, and their example is reflected in `ṣalāt al-layl`: we pray its twelve `rak'at`s consecutively, all at once. And after the `ṣalāt al-layl` there is a `du'ā` — is there not?
There is, there is.
Yes, a very long `du'ā`.
Yes, the `du'ā` is very long.
After no other prayer is there such a `du'ā`.
Well — `ṣalāt al-layl` is the final prayer before `shaf'a`, and it indicates the `du'āt` of the `ḥaḍarāt`. They are of very high rank. For it is from these very `du'āt` — from their `fuḍalā'` and their `riyāḍ` — that the `Bāb al-Salsal` and the `Bāb al-Anbiyā'` are formed. These are people of the highest rank.

[Side-discussion begins]
Conscience — `zamīr`. My conscience does not allow it. I feel that my conscience is speaking to me. That is the guidance of the `nāṭiqah`. The `nāṭiqah` always seeks to keep its `nāmīyah` and `ḥissīyah` with it, and strives toward that end. The stronger the `nāṭiqah`, the more it exerts `ishrāq` upon its `nāmīyah`, `ḥissīyah`, and upon the body, seeking to elevate them. This is why some `hudūd` of the Dawah have been such that not only the `nāmīyah`, `ḥissīyah`, and `nāṭiqah` join together after death to form the `ṣūrat sharīf`, but even their internal organs and lower bodily parts ascend with them — on account of their nobility and the life of piety and purity they lived.
But conscience — everyone has a conscience. Yet everyone has taken an oath. Some are bound by it, some are not. So consciously, I say to my conscience: "No, I am going to do this wrong thing." I am performing the act, whether right or wrong, with full intention. It is not that merely being from the Ahl al-Silsilah guarantees one walks the straight path. Free will (`ikhtiyār`) exists.
[Side-discussion ends]

Free will exists. And this free will is a very great thing that nature has given to man alone. Angels have not been given free will, for angels cannot sin — as the Quranic verse states: `walā ya'ṣūn` (and they do not disobey). They do not sin. And consider Jannah and Dozakh (paradise and hell) — for whom have they been made? Only for humans. Among Allah's creations there are many: the moon, the sun, the `aflāk`, the earth, animals, minerals, and countless other things. But Jannah and Dozakh are for humans alone — because man is the only creature who has the choice (`ikhtiyār`) to do good and to do evil. Goodness is clear to him, and evil is clear to him; yet knowing goodness, he may incline toward evil, and knowing evil, he may incline toward goodness. He has complete freedom of choice. And because of this choice, if he does good, he draws near to Jannah, and if he does evil, he draws near to Dozakh. This condition applies to man alone, not to others. That is why...
For the one endowed with `nāṭiqah`, with `juzur`.
Yes. Among all of Allah's creation, it is man alone for whom Jannah and Dozakh have been created. And there is a `du'ā` in which it is said — the author of the `du'ā` urges — that one should ask for Jannah five times daily, and five times seek refuge from Dozakh: `Allāhumma innī as'aluka'l-jannah` (O Allah, I ask You for paradise). `As'alu'llāha'l-jannah` (I ask Allah for paradise). And `Wa a'ūdhu bi'llāhi min al-nār` (And I seek refuge in Allah from the Fire) — I ask Allah to protect me from Dozakh. It has been prescribed to do this five times.

[Side-discussion begins]
We use a slightly different tone — the `du'ā` after the `adhān`. Yes, `rabbanā ātinā...` — that is in its own place. But here we are asking Allah to grant us Jannah, and the tone is different. That `du'ā` is in its own place; it is, in fact, the `du'ā` of Amir al-Mu'minin — what we recite after the `adhān`. Five times — some recite it five times, at different times. And this one — five times together: five times to ask, and five times to seek refuge.
[Side-discussion ends]

The `du'ā` after the `adhān` is apt because the time between the `adhān` and the `iqāmah` is one of the five times when `du'ā`s are accepted. During that interval, supplications are answered. So we pray, as Amir al-Mu'minin prescribed: `Allāhumma innī as'aluka'l-jannah... wa an tuzawwajanī min al-ḥūri'l-'īn` (O Allah, I ask You for paradise... and that You unite me with the `ḥūr 'īn`). And who are the `ḥūr 'īn`? They are in truth your `hudūd 'āliyah` — the prayer is that my unification, my union, may be granted with the `hudūd 'āliyah`.


`Wa 'an Sayyidna 'Abdullah ibn Thāmir` (And concerning Sayyidna 'Abdullah ibn Thāmir). Concerning Sayyidna 'Abdullah ibn Thāmir, the question you have asked — `wa annahu lahu hādhihi'l-muddah` (and that for him has been this duration) — a period has passed for him. `Wa anna qad zāda 'ala'l-alfi fi'l-'iddah` (And that it has exceeded a thousand in count). A period of more than a thousand years has passed. `Wa in kāna dhālika wa qad ṣa'ida'l-fuḍalā'` (And if that is so, the `fuḍalā'` has indeed ascended). While his `fuḍalā'` has progressed after a thousand years — all of it (`jamī'uhā`): its flesh (`laḥmuhā`), its bones (`'aẓmuhā`), and its nerves (`'aṣabuhā`) — all have progressed. `Fa-'āda'l-āthār` (So the traces returned). Yet those `āthār` (traces) remain in that grave. `Wa't-turbatu'sh-sharīfah allatī'jtajanat bihā` (And the noble earth in which it was enveloped). The noble soil in which their `fuḍalā'` had been mixed remains. `Wa ishrāqu'ṣ-ṣuwar fī tilka'l-amākini'ṭ-ṭahārah` (And the illumination of the forms in those places of purity). The `ishrāq` that is occurring moves toward your `fuḍalā'`, toward its `āthār`, and toward that soil in which your body was hidden. In that soil too, nobility was created. `Wa alā qadri'z-zā'ir wa'l-mazūr tuqābiluhu'z-zubadu'ẓ-ẓāhirah` (And according to the rank of the visitor and the visited, the manifest quintessences meet him). According to the rank of the visitor and the one visited, they are met by the `zubad`.

What are the `zubad`? The `fuḍalā'`, after progressing, must travel to Baytullah. Our great grave — as distinguished from the small grave — is the great grave. The small grave is the one in which we are placed after death; the great grave is Baytullah. To journey to Baytullah, there are twenty-eight stages (`manāzil`) to cross. From the place where a person has passed away, the `fuḍalā'` moves gradually, coming first to the spot in the masjid where he used to pray. One who prays habitually makes a particular place his own; that place becomes the gathering-point for his `fuḍalā'`. From there it progresses to his `miḥrāb` (prayer niche), and from the `miḥrāb` it advances to the shrine of Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan.

The progression of the `fuḍalā'` continues in this manner. From Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan's shrine it departs and moves onward through various noble places (`amākin sharīfah`), until it reaches the `mawāqīt` (appointed stations) — the five stations from which pilgrims bind their intention for `umrah`. It is said that `fuḍalāt` and `riyāḍ` gather at those stations. Progressing further, from Anderganj Charkhan it arrives at the precincts of Baytullah — its `fuḍalā'` reaching the various `rukn`s (corners) and ultimately arriving at the `rukn al-aswad` (the Black Stone corner) of Baytullah. This process is ongoing and unceasing. From there it ascends.

The state of ascension is called `zubad`. While it rises, it remains in the state of `fuḍalā'`. But when the `fuḍalā'` arrives at the pillars of Baytullah, it is called `zakhā'ir` (treasures). And when it ascends to the `aflāk` (celestial spheres), it is called `zubad`. The `zubad` is a very exalted state of being.

The `zubad sharīfah` thus meet both the visitor and the one whose grave is visited, and they aid them as well. `Wa aydan fa-inna kullu qabrin ḍamma hādhihi'l-ḥudūd` (And also, that every grave that contains these `hudūd`). Every grave is connected to these `hudūd mu'aẓẓamah`. `Fa-taḥtahu sirrun 'aẓīm min al-khamā'iri'l-kāminah` (For beneath it is a great secret from the latent leavens). Beneath the grave lies a great secret from the hidden leavens. `Fī tilka'l-biqā'i'l-mukarramah` (In those honored spots) — their noble places. `Fa-li-dhālika wajabat ziyāratuhā` (Therefore, their visitation became obligatory). Because of the hidden leavens present there, because the `ṣuwar` are casting their `ishrāq` upon the grave, because things have progressed while `āthār` remain — for all these reasons, visitation becomes obligatory. These places are exceedingly pure, and they benefit the believer and the visitor alike.

A story of this kind occurred in the time of Wali al-Muqaddas. A man named Amanullah Saheb, who served as a `Mullā Adīb` in the village of Taliwala, contracted cancer in his final days. The cancer was in his jaw, and his condition was grave. He was seen by various doctors, yet the cancer continued to worsen. He was a man of firm faith with deep trust in Huzur. Taliwala lies very close to Khankhah Sharif — it is the nearest village. At Khankhah Sharif there are two of our elders: Sayyidi Yusuf bin Ali Khan and Sayyidi Sharif Muhammad Saheb Sindhi. Amanullah Saheb would go there and sit as if in `i'tikāf` (seclusion). He would take the soil of that grave and apply it to the afflicted place. This became his daily routine, and he maintained it for months. When he later returned to the doctors, a disease like cancer had vanished, and he was completely well. This too is a condition that obtains: these masters of the graves, who lived noble lives, were `bi'l-quwwah` (in potentiality) during their lives and have now become `bi'l-fi'l` (in actuality). By virtue of becoming `bi'l-fi'l`, they have the capacity to benefit their followers and those who hold faith in them. In this way, benefit is received.

Amanullah Saheb — the one whose son has recently built the hospital. Yes, the father of Salim, who built the hospital. That was Amanullah Saheb.

`Allatī lā ḥādatah` (Which has no limit). Those noble `ṣuwar` meet both the visitor and the one whose grave is visited. Their `ṣuwar` are ever oriented toward their `fuḍalā'`, and because of this the `ishrāq` continues uninterrupted — the `ishrāq` of the `ṣūrah` toward its `fuḍalā'`. Therefore, the person who comes to visit, dwelling within that circle, also receives benefit.

As has been mentioned, the soil of Imam Husayn's grave is regarded as `khāk-e-shifā'` (soil of healing). Indeed. And there is also a saying of the Master of the Universe: `wa idhā ḍāqati'ṣ-ṣudūr fa'sta'īnū bi-ahli'l-qubūr` (and when the chests become constricted, then seek help from the people of the graves). When your chests become constricted — when you find no way out, when you are in such distress that you cannot see how to remove your trouble — then `fa'sta'īnū bi-ahli'l-qubūr`, seek help from the people of the graves. It was not said: seek help from Allah directly. Allah is the one who ultimately helps. But these are the people of Allah, and because they are the people of Allah, Allah helps through them — and through them, you receive help.

As it is in the Quran, in the verses revealed concerning Sayyidna Isa alayhis salam: `man anṣārī ilā'llāh` (Who are my helpers for Allah?). `Qāla'l-ḥawāriyyūna naḥnu anṣāru'llāh` (The disciples said, "We are the helpers of Allah"). "For the sake of Allah, who will be my helper?" The disciples answered: `naḥnu anṣāru'llāh` — "We are the helpers of Allah." Does Allah need help? This is a Quranic verse: `naḥnu anṣāru'llāh`.

`Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Allāhumma ashrik fi'l-fātiḥah, hadiyyatan waṣilatan suniyyatan zakiyyatan naḍiyyah ilā rūḥ Sayyidna Ali bin Hibatullah, qaddasa'llāhu rūḥahu wa nawwara ḍarīḥah, wa ḍā'afa 'alaynā min barakātihi wa lahdhatihi wa tā'īdātihi, wa taqabbal minnā al-ifādah wa'l-istifādah fī a'ẓam mā yakūnu bi-ḥaqqi Sayyidna Muhammadin wa ālihi ajma'īn wa sallim al-Fātiḥah.`
`Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-ḥamdu li'llāhi Rabbi'l-'ālamīn, al-Raḥmāni'r-Raḥīm, Māliki yawmi'd-dīn, iyyāka na'budu wa iyyāka nasta'īn, ihdinā'ṣ-ṣirāṭa'l-mustaqīm, ṣirāṭa'lladhīna an'amta 'alayhim ghayri'l-maghḍūbi 'alayhim wa lā'ḍ-ḍāllīn.`
`Al-Fātiḥah.`
`Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-ḥamdu li'llāhi Rabbi'l-'ālamīn, al-Raḥmāni'r-Raḥīm, Māliki yawmi'd-dīn, iyyāka na'budu wa iyyāka nasta'īn, ihdinā'ṣ-ṣirāṭa'l-mustaqīm, ṣirāṭa'lladhīna an'amta 'alayhim ghayri'l-maghḍūbi 'alayhim wa lā'ḍ-ḍāllīn.`

<!-- lecture 11 -->

As-salamu alaykum. Wa alaykum as-salam.

From *Kunooz al-Hikmah*, the tenth question — *as-su'āl al-'āshir* (the tenth question) — is now before us. After that comes *al-hādī*. *Dhibh al-hadī*. *Dhibh al-hadī*. The *dhibh* (slaughter) of the *hadī*. *Dhibh al-hadī fī Minā*. *Ma al-ḥaqīqatuhu* — what is its reality?

One meaning of *hadiyyah* is a gift. And *al-hadī* is the name given to the sacrificial animal — what is offered in sacrifice. As for *Minā*, we call it both *Minā* and *Munā*, though *Munā* is the more evocative form: *munā* means the ultimate desire, the deepest aspiration. The question the questioner has posed concerns the slaughter of an animal in Minā performed by a pilgrim (`ḥājj`): what is its true reality? *Wa ammā al-mathal wa'l-mawṣūl* (And as for the metaphor and its referent) — what is its *mathal* (metaphor) and its *mawṣūl* (referent)? *Faqad 'arraftuhu min tafaḍḍulukum* (For I have come to know it through your favor). I have come to understand, through your generosity, the *mathal* and *mawṣūl* of this matter.

*Wa mā yakūnu min Jabal 'Arafat* (And what is from Mount 'Arafat). In the plain of 'Arafat there is Jabal 'Arafat, the highest point of that plain. *Faqad 'araftu al-mathal* (For I have understood the metaphor) — I have grasped its apparent meaning. *Wa tafaḍḍal bi'l-jawāb al-shāfī wa'l-manwāl al-kāfī* (So please favor me with the healing answer and the sufficient provision). Grant me a full and complete exposition.

*Al-jawāb 'alā dhālika as-su'āl* (The answer to that question). *Naqūlu bi-'awni'llāh* (We say with the help of Allah). When the Sarwar-e-Kainat (Master of the Universe) stood at Jabal 'Arafat — for the entire plain of 'Arafat is a *mawqūf* (place of standing) — he ascended the hill and offered *du'ās* (prayers). Muslims generally make their way to that hill as well.

*Al-jawāb 'alā dhālika as-su'āl, naqūlu bi-'awni'llāh al-kabīr al-muta'āl* (The answer to that question — we say with the help of Allah, the Great, the Exalted). In answering your question, I say: with the help of Allah, the Great, the Exalted, *wa minnat walī al-'aṣr wa'l-zamān* (and the favor of the Guardian of the Age and Time), and with the grace of the *Walī al-'Aṣr wa'l-Zamān*, *'alayhi aṣ-ṣalawāt al-raḥīm al-raḥmān* (upon him be the blessings of the Merciful, the Compassionate), *wa ta'yīd mawlāya al-a'lam wa sirruhu al-bāhir al-mu'aẓẓam* (and the support of my master, the most knowledgeable, and his brilliant, magnificent secret) — from the support of Mawlāya al-A'lam, namely Hibatullah Saheb, and from the effulgence of his magnificent secret — I give the answer. *'An dhibh al-hadī fī Minā* (Concerning the slaughter of the *hadī* in Minā) — what is *al-ḥaqīqah fī dhālika* (the reality in that)?

Its meaning is as follows: when the *Mudabbir al-Ḥakīm* (the Wise Planner) intends *min irqā'ihā wa is'ādihā 'alā an-nasl al-qawīm* (from its elevation and its ascension upon the upright lineage) — that the flesh of the sacrifice be elevated and ascended to the pure lineage — the slaughter is performed. As for its soul, that which is called the *nafs ḥissiyyah* (sensory soul), it is punished through the act of slaughter. By slaughtering the animal, its *ḥissiyyah* is expelled from it.

If an animal dies a natural death, its *ḥissiyyah* retreats inward into the body — and that is a bad form of death, for its *ḥissiyyah* has turned inward. But when we perform the slaughter while invoking the name of Allah, the *ḥissiyyah* separates from the animal, which is why we may eat and make use of its flesh. Therefore, the flesh is elevated and ascended, while the *rūḥ* — that is, the *ḥissiyyah* — is punished and expelled.

Regarding the slaughter: if something other than *Allāhu Akbar* is said — is that permissible? As for the Jews who perform slaughter, they do not say *Allāhu Akbar* but invoke in their own tongue. Their slaughter is nonetheless permissible (`jā'iz`). Both the slaughter of a Jew and the slaughter of a Christian are *ḥalāl*. A Muslim will say *Allāhu Akbar*; but in the case of others, whatever they say in their own language for that purpose is accepted.

As for what happens in Saudi Arabia: there was a butcher who performed slaughter there. He explained that a tape recording of *Allāhu Akbar* is played in the slaughterhouse while the machine operates. That is a practice about which one may have reservations. Their contention, however, is practical: how many times can one repeat the invocation over a mechanical process?

Consider how immense the blessings that flow from saying *Allāhu Akbar*. There is a hadith of Rasulullah, Sarwar-e-Kainat: *aṣ-ṣalātu qurbānu kulli taqiyyin* (Prayer is the sacrifice of every pious person). Prayer is the sacrifice of every God-fearing person. The word *qurbān* (sacrifice) is used here deliberately. When we perform a slaughter we say *Allāhu Akbar*, making the animal *ḥalāl* so that we may eat its flesh. In prayer, having performed *wuḍū'* (ablution) and come to the masjid in a state of worship, we say *Allāhu Akbar* over ourselves. Our own *nafs ḥissiyyah* (sensory soul) is thereby being sacrificed — struck and expelled continuously. In a two-*rak'at* prayer alone, *Allāhu Akbar* is uttered eleven times. If one utterance of *Allāhu Akbar* renders an animal *ḥalāl*, what a magnificent difference it must make when a believer in a state of *īmān* (faith) pronounces *Allāhu Akbar* over himself. For the *nafs nāṭiqah* (rational soul) of a believer is so veiled in its inner workings that it does not disclose to the *nafs ḥissiyyah* — the soul closest to it — the secrets, the blessings, and the divine effulgence it receives from *'Alī 'Illiyyīn*. It does not inform the *nafs nāmiyyah* (vegetative soul) or the *nafs ḥissiyyah* in any way. Such is the nature of the *nafs nāṭiqah*.

And observe what the Messenger says: the most excellent *waẓīfah* (litany) consists of four phrases — the finest invocation one can perform. He said: *Subḥānallāh, wa'l-ḥamdu lillāh, wa lā ilāha illallāh, wallāhu Akbar* (Glory be to Allah, and all praise is for Allah, and there is no god but Allah, and Allah is the greatest). *Allāhu Akbar* is among them. *Wa lā ḥawla wa lā quwwata illā billāhi'l-'aliyyi'l-'aẓīm* (And there is no might and no power except with Allah, the Most High, the Most Great) — this fifth phrase is also included. It is also related in a narration that the Prophet counselled a person to recite these four phrases before sleeping, for doing so brings the reward of one *Ḥajj*. Such are the blessings contained within them.

As we noted in discussing the *tasbīḥ* (rosary): *Allāhu Akbar* corresponds to the *nafs nāmiyyah*, *al-ḥamdu lillāh* corresponds to the *nafs ḥissiyyah*, and *Subḥānallāh* corresponds to the *nafs nāṭiqah*. The final phrase — *Lā ilāha illallāh* — its form pertains to the *nayyirah* (luminous form). At the time of death, the three souls will unite, and they will be transformed into the noble form and the form of the *nayyirah*.


You said that the *hissiya* leaves its *jism* (body) — it escapes. Escapes means it… ascends? No, it doesn't ascend. The *natiqa* will absorb the *hissiya*, the *namiya* it will absorb. The *hissiya* spreading into the body is a very bad death, a bad state. But the *hissiya* being absorbed into the *natiqa* is a very noble thing. The *hissiya* becomes part of the *natiqa*.

At the time of death? But during life too, it keeps pulling it, does it not? Yes, absolutely. Throughout life this process keeps going on. The *natiqa* keeps absorbing the *hissiya*. The more obedient the *hissiya* is, the more completely it is absorbed.

This is why it is said that when the *awliya' Allah* (friends of God) pray, their state changes. The person he was before is not the person he is after. There are examples of this as well. It is said that the Messenger who, on the night of *Mi'raj*, went to meet Allah — who obtained those excellences, that nearness to God — when he returned, was it the same person who had departed? It is very far from reason that the one whom the Lord of the worlds had summoned for a meeting would be sent back in the same state.

Consider also Sayyidna Ibrahim bin Muhammad bin Fahd. Sayyidna 'Ali bin Sulayman said of him: "Had it not been decided in pre-eternity that I would precede Ibrahim, I could never have preceded him." What a momentous statement. Now, Sayyidna Ibrahim bin Muhammad bin Fahd remained in Yemen while you remained in Hindustan, because there is a principle of the *da'wah* (mission) that a *Da'i* (missionary) cannot leave the island of Yemen unless his successor is present there. So Sayyidna Ibrahim bin Muhammad bin Fahd remained in Yemen. It happened that at that time the Zaidi Imam who held power there imprisoned him, and he remained in jail for fourteen years. During those fourteen years, Sayyidna 'Ali bin Sulayman performed approximately eleven *nusus* (appointments) upon him. Someone from among the *hudud* (ranks) of the *da'wah* asked Sayyidna 'Ali bin Sulayman: "Master, you have already performed *nass* (appointment) on Sayyidna Ibrahim — why was there a need to perform it again?" He replied: "The Ibrahim upon whom I had performed *nass* has changed. Ibrahim is no longer Ibrahim." See how great a statement that is. That Ibrahim is no longer the same Ibrahim; he has changed. I performed *nass* on a different Ibrahim. That is what he said.

There must be a great age difference between Sayyidna 'Ali and Sayyidna Ibrahim. I am not certain, but Ibrahim was the son of Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fahd, though their mother was the same — Khadija bint Nuh. Perhaps he was born after the passing of Sayyidna Sulayman, so the age difference may not have been that great.

*Wal-qatlu fi jami'i halatiha wa la taqtassu laha al-'inayat al-ilahiyya bis-su'ud* — the divine providence did not take *qisas* (retribution) from the *su'ud* (ascension) of the *zabihat* (slaughtered animal) *li kawnihim min azdad al-hudud* (because it was from the opposites of the ranks). Who is meant? Its *hissiya*. The meat of the slaughtered animal did ascend and entered into the food of the believers.
