But its *hissiya*, being from the opposites of the ranks, was not elevated through retribution. *Wa yufariquhu bi wasitat al-tasmiyat al-mu'azzamah 'inda arbab al-huda* — it separates from it by means of the magnificent naming of God in the view of the masters of guidance. The *hissiya* separates by means of the *tasmiyah*, that is, by saying *Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim* — such a *Bismillah* as is magnificent in the view of the masters of guidance.

So where does it go? It goes to desolate deserts. The *hissiya* goes to *Ras al-Zanab*, which is its abode. The *hissiyat* that gather there — whether they are *azdad* (opposites) or those of the slaughtered — because they are evil, they cause harm to the believers. The *azdad* of the *awliya' Allah* in particular are given a respite of forty days during which they roam between the heavens and the earth, harming the believers. After forty days they are drawn into *Ras al-Zanab*, which is their permanent abode.

Their powers are also greatly increased during this time. Yes. Because the opponent of the *da'wah* has also performed good deeds in his life. The reward for those good deeds will not come to him in the hereafter; rather, in this very world he is given free rein — do as you please. That is the reward he receives.

But as for the *zabihat* — the meat is, at the level of *yawm an-na*, ascending in human form. Yet you are eating it, I am eating it, fifty people are eating it, all separately. So that which belongs to the unit — say, the goat of a particular individual — how does nature keep its account when it has been divided among fifty people? Only God knows. But it has entered the state of ascending, and even though it is divided among many, it remains in that state.

Look at how great the importance of *Bismillah* is. The Prophet says in one of his prayers: *Bismillahilladhi la yadurru ma'a ismihi shay'un fil-ardi wa la fis-sama'i wa huwa as-sami'ul 'alim* — In the name of Allah, with whose name nothing in the earth or the heavens can cause harm, and He is the All-Hearing, the All-Knowing. The *Bismillah* carries such power and strength that nothing can harm the one who says it, neither in the earth nor in the sky. This prayer is well established and known even among the general people. Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fahd has also mentioned it in his collection of prayers. There is mention of reciting it three times in the morning and three times at night, and it is a promise from Allah conveyed through the tongue of the Messenger that no harm will come to the one who does so.

The matter of the *zabihat* — that which we make *halal* — is now understood. But the animals that are not *halal*, their matter then goes into the *darkat* (lower levels)? Yes, absolutely it goes into the *darkat*. How absolutely subtle a matter this is in the world. The greatness of making something *halal* is a great virtue — one utterance of *Allahu Akbar* makes a creature *halal*.

Those of us who eat meat that comes from outside ought to exercise great caution regarding how it has been prepared.

The meat of the *zabihat* of Eid al-Adha should not be given to a non-Muslim. We generally do not give it. As for those who are paid wages — no, it is not given. The meat of the religious offering is different. As for people who take their Eid al-Adha *zabihat* home and distribute it themselves, what they do is their own responsibility. But the meat of the *zabihat* of Eid — and similarly the meat of the *'aqiqah* — should not be given to a non-Muslim. And the mother whose child's *'aqiqah* has been performed should not eat that meat either.

She should not eat it? No, she should not. The child is still a nursing infant. If the mother eats that meat, its nutrition will enter her body, form her strength, and form her milk. The child will drink that milk, and for him that milk is not *halal*. This matter is generally not known to people. So the mother should not eat it — not even the soup made from it.

Look at how many dimensions there are within what is *halal*. If one brings two animals, and one remains alive while the second shivers from the cold and dies, the dead one is simply a carcass to be discarded. The living one is slaughtered with *Allahu Akbar*. An animal that dies its own death dies a bad death — except for the pigeons of the Baitullah. If the pigeons of the Baitullah die their own death, that death is *halal*; they are not to be slaughtered, nor are they to be eaten by slaughtering them, but their natural death is *halal*. This question was put to the *du'at* (missionaries): since there are pigeons at the Baitullah and also pigeons in Madinah al-Munawwarah, are the pigeons of Madinah like those of the Baitullah? The *Da'i* Sayyidna Isma'il bin Hibatullah replied: "No, the pigeons of Madinah are not like the pigeons of Baitullah, because the station of the pigeons of Baitullah is very high."

Consider: the *riyat* (subtle essence) of a buried believer rises within three days, ascends to Jupiter, then to the moon, and at the level of *yawm an-na* descends as completely pure and transparent matter to form the body of the Imam. The *fuzala* (remains), however, go into the *buruj* (constellations) to be refined. Many things — hard bones, teeth, the skull — take a very long time to be cleansed, while the soft tissue ascends more readily. After being refined, these remaining things emerge in three forms.

The first: they become *jawahir* (jewels). For those who are the *fuzala* of the *hudud al-da'wah* who performed great service in their lives, even their remains are honoured — as *Fallahu khayrun hafizan wa huwa arhamur rahimin* (For Allah is the best of guardians, and He is the most merciful of the merciful) — and they become jewels set in the crowns of kings or preserved in their treasuries.

The second: they become pigeons — those very pigeons whose natural death is *halal* and who need not be slaughtered. Remarkably, they do not perform *bawl-o-baraz* (urination and defecation). The animal that produces the most *bawl-o-baraz* is ordinarily the pigeon — it is a nuisance in any house it inhabits. Yet the pigeons of the Baitullah do not make a mess, nor do they fly over the Baitullah itself. The pigeons of Madinah also do not make a mess. This nobility is theirs because they are formed from the *fuzala* of the *hudud al-da'wah*.

The third: they become the salt of which it is said, *al-milhu qabla kulli ta'amin wa ba'dahu yadfa'u sab'ina da'an ahwanuha al-judham wal-junun* — salt before and after every meal wards off seventy diseases, the least of which are leprosy and insanity. This is the nature of that salt. And where does that salt come from? Not the ordinary salt we use for cooking and other purposes, but the salt that nature refines and brings to our table so that we taste it at the beginning and end of a meal. It carries such blessing that it wards off diseases. That salt is formed from the bones of the *hudud al-da'wah*.

As for wild animals that die their own death — they are not slaughtered; they simply die. Does that mean they will always remain in a fallen state? Yes, in a fallen form. But in *yawm an-na*, their resurrection must also occur. The disbelievers and polytheists? For them, because *shirk* (polytheism) is a great wrong, progress will not happen until they recite the *kalimah* of *La ilaha illallah*.

Is the *da'wah* presented to them at the time of every death? Yes, absolutely. Islam is presented. At every death, and when they change bodies (*qalib*), the *da'wah* is presented to them as well. This continues until they fall so far as to enter the body of tar (*dammar*). At that point the *da'wah* is no longer presented to them. There is a prayer in the *munajat*: *Wa libas al-qatiran la tulinni* — do not make me wear the garment of tar — meaning, may I never fall so far as to reach that state.

And in today's times, we are wearing it — nylon and whatever else is now in use. Nylon is made from petroleum, and the final residue of petroleum is tar. Yes, that is correct.


…a thing made of thread and a thing made of air, of which there is neither emptiness nor fullness. In the air, no thing is empty; rather, all things are filled. It is our eyes that lack the capacity to see this. This is why it is said that one should not become naked, for the things present in the air will have an effect upon a person. Therefore, one should not become naked, for what is in the air exerts its influence.

*Wa lidhalika mathaltu 'ala qatl al-mukhalifina 'inda qiyam qayam yawm ad-din* — And for this reason, when the *Qayam* rises on the Day of the Hereafter, these opponents will be killed. Which opponents? The *azdad* — the opposites. This *hissiya* (sensory soul) — what was it? It was from the opposites of the ranks. Those opposites of the ranks will be given a human garment and will come forward near the time of *Qiyamah* (Resurrection). They will appear dressed in human form because this *Qayam* who is coming occupies a very high station — the station of the seventh *kashf* (unveiling) and the seventh *sidr* (Lote-tree). For such a *Qayam*, what kind of assembly will there be? From ancient times, those who were the opposites, who had died and been destroyed, will be presented before the *Qayam* in physical form. Yazid, Mu'awiyah, Marwan bin Hakam, Nimrud, Fir'awn — all of them will be brought in human form to the plain of Mina. Nature will drag them, just as a flock is driven, and they will be brought to the plain of Mina. The *Qayam 'ala dhikrihi al-salam* will command the believers to kill them. They will do so, and the plain of Mina will be entirely covered with their corpses, until such a stench arises that the believers will declare they cannot remain in that condition. They will complain to the Imam, who will command the wind. That wind will blow and carry the corpses toward *safrah*. There are four doors to that *safrah*, one of which is here in Hadramaut as well. The mention of all four doors is recorded in a book by Sayyidna 'Ali bin Sulayman. The corpses will be taken inside, and their duration there is one *qawr-e-a'zam* (great epoch) — *illa ma rahima rabbi* — except for those upon whom my Lord has shown mercy. Those who had performed some good deeds will, on account of those deeds, be released before the full *qawr-e-a'zam* has elapsed. And how will that release occur? Through volcanoes — they will emerge as lava. Just as 'Umar bin 'Abd al-'Aziz: he was a caliph from the line of Mu'awiyah, from the Banu Umayyah.

What is the example here? 'Umar bin 'Abd al-'Aziz was a caliph of the Muslims from the Umayyad line, yet he did two things of consequence. The *Bagh-e-Fadak*, which had been usurped by 'Umar ibn al-Khattab and thereafter remained in the possession of successive governments, he returned to the Ahl al-Bayt. That was one thing. The second was this: from seventy thousand pulpits, the cursing and reviling of Sayyidna 'Ali and his progeny had been carried out, and even keeping the name 'Ali had been forbidden. He put a stop to that practice, declaring it an injustice upon the Ahl al-Bayt. Because of these two deeds, his duration in that state was shortened, and he was released before the full *qawr-e-a'zam* through a volcano.

The gate named 'Abd al-'Aziz — a gate in that courtyard bears that name. Though the 'Abd al-'Aziz of that name is theirs, from the Saudis.

The lava that comes out — that is a good form for them, is it not? They are emerging. And that lava then becomes soil, and from that soil vegetation grows, and from the vegetation food comes, and it ascends and refines.

And *Jabal 'Arafat* (Mount 'Arafat)…

Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. O Allah, convey this Fatihah as a gift, a means, a noble and pleasing sacrifice, to the soul of Sayyidna 'Ali bin Hibatullah — may Allah sanctify his soul, illuminate his grave, and shower upon us from his blessings, his effulgence, and his support — by the right of the master of the messengers, Sayyidna Muhammad. Accept it from us, O Allah, for the purpose of benefiting and gaining benefit in the best way possible, by the right of the master of the messengers, Sayyidna Muhammad al-Mustafa, and the great *Qa'im*, and his pure and holy Ahl al-Bayt, may the blessings and peace of Allah be upon them all. Al-Fatihah. Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi rabb al-'alamin. Ar-rahman al-rahim. Maliki yawm al-din. Iyyaka na'budu wa iyyaka nasta'in. Ihdina al-sirat al-mustaqim. Sirat alladhina an'amta 'alayhim ghayri al-maghdubi 'alayhim wa la al-dallin. Amin.

By the intention of Sayyidna, our master, the master of the Husayni *da'wah* from the Sindhi lineage — the noble of nobles, the exalted and magnificent, the wise and learned, the guiding and rightly-guided, the holy — Sayyidna Husayn bin Sayyidna Muhammad, Muqaddas, and his father, Sayyidna Muhammad bin Hibatullah, Muqaddas, may Allah sanctify their souls. May Allah the Exalted protect him, guard him, and aid him against the plots of the polytheists — by the right of the master of the messengers, Sayyidna Muhammad al-Mustafa, and his pure and holy Ahl al-Bayt, may the blessings and peace of Allah be upon them all. Al-Fatihah. Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi rabb al-'alamin. Ar-rahman al-rahim. Maliki yawm al-din. Iyyaka na'budu wa iyyaka nasta'in. Ihdina al-sirat al-mustaqim. Sirat alladhina an'amta 'alayhim ghayri al-maghdubi 'alayhim wa la al-dallin. Amin.

As-salamu alaykum.

<!-- lecture 12 -->

As-salamu 'alaykum wa rahmatullahi wa barakatuhu. These are two editions. I have page 57, and what you all have is page 26. I will read. *Wa amma Jabal 'Arafat*.

Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Allahumma salli 'ala Muhammad wa aali Muhammad. *Wa amma Jabal 'Arafat al-sharif* — And as for the noble Mount 'Arafat — *fa-tahtahu sirrun latifun* — beneath it is a subtle secret — *wa fi Yawm 'Arafah, tashruqu fi dhalika al-mahall, min al-suwar, ma la yakhturu 'ala qalbi basharin* — and on the Day of 'Arafah, there shines in that place, from the forms, what has never occurred to the heart of any human.

As for the matter of Jabal 'Arafat, which is noble — Jabal 'Arafat, that is, the *maqam* (station) of 'Arafat, the *maydan* (plain) of 'Arafat — it is obligatory for the *haji* (pilgrim) to set foot there. Among the six days of Hajj, 'Arafat becomes the *ruh* (spirit) of those days. If a person goes for Hajj and does not have enough time to perform the 'Umrah of *Tamattu'*, but reaches 'Arafat directly on the ninth day of the month, his Hajj is considered complete. Even if he did not reach Mina, by reaching 'Arafat his Hajj is considered done. Such is the *'azmah* (greatness) of 'Arafat. The three *maqamat* (stations) — Mina, 'Arafat, and Muzdalifah — are each associated with one of three great souls. 'Arafat is associated with Mawla-i Ka'inat, Mawla Rasulullah, salawatullah 'alayh. The *maqam* of Mina is associated with Janab-i 'Ali, 'alayhi al-salam. And Muzdalifah is associated with Janab-i Bibi Fatimah. In the *Dawr al-Satr* (Period of Concealment), these three are personalities without equal — the *lubab* (essence), the *lubb al-lubab* (very essence), of the *Dawr al-Satr*.

Regarding 'Arafat — one must arrive before *ghurub al-shams* (sunset). One must set foot there before sunset, because then the next day begins; the tenth date begins. On the ninth date, one must reach 'Arafat somehow, and before sunset one's feet must touch the ground. Jabal 'Arafat has its own *sharafah* (nobility) when one reaches it, for there the *khuddam* appeared. Jabal 'Arafat is the most noble place.

*Wa li-hadha, fa-tahtahu sirrun latifun* — beneath it is a *sirrun latifun*, a subtle secret. And on Yawm 'Arafah, *wahi* (revelation) was continuously descending upon Rasulullah. When he was reaching the plain of 'Arafat, what must have been his state? So much revelation was descending, so many times, that his she-camel would sit down from its weight. It is related that from the weight of the revelation, the she-camel would repeatedly lower herself, and people would try to raise her, but Rasulullah would say, "Do not lift her; she sits down from the weight of the revelation." Consider how pure and luminous Rasulullah's blessed heart must have been — that an animal could not bear what descended upon it. The Quran and its *anwar* (lights) descended upon that heart.

On Yawm 'Arafah, *ishraq* (illumination) occurs in that place from *suwar* (forms) such that "*ma la yakhturu 'ala qalbi basharin*" — what has never occurred to the heart of any human. Such *suwar-i nayyirah* (luminous forms) manifest and shine upon it. And the most intense *ishraq* that occurred was connected to Ghadeer-i Khumm — that is, because of the imminent arrival at Ghadeer. Rasulullah was heading toward Ghadeer afterward. One reason was the exalted station itself and its association with Rasulullah. Another was that the noble Kalam was nearly complete — only the final *surah* relating to the succession remained. Whatever *wahi* was descending, whatever *anwar* or *ahkam* (commands) were coming from Allah's side, it was on account of this that the she-camel was in that condition.

Immediately after Hajj, Rasulullah set out for Medina. On the thirteenth of the month, he arrived in Makkah and there informed Janab-i 'A'ishah that Allah had commanded him to appoint 'Ali as his *wasi* (successor) and *khalifah* (caliph), and asked her to keep this matter secret. But she went and told her father Abu Bakr. Abu Bakr then told 'Umar, and their scheming began. When Rasulullah reached the approach to Ghadeer-i Khumm, the company was passing through an extremely dark valley at night. Certain persons — *munafiqun* (hypocrites) — drew their daggers with the intention of attacking Rasulullah. But by Allah's power, lightning flashed so brightly, so forcefully, and for so long that Rasulullah saw all of their faces clearly. Among those who witnessed this was Hudhayfah al-Yamani. Rasulullah said, "Hudhayfah, did you see what I saw?" He said, "Yes, O Rasulullah, I also saw." Rasulullah then said, "I am instructing you to keep this a secret and not tell anyone." Hudhayfah al-Yamani kept that trust for as long as Rasulullah lived, telling no one. Afterward, 'Umar ibn al-Khattab would persistently ask Hudhayfah al-Yamani, "Was I among those who attacked the Prophet?" Hudhayfah would reply, "Rasulullah made me swear not to say this to anyone." But 'Umar would say, "By Allah, I was among them." These are great spectacles of history. As for the route — Rasulullah came to Makkah, and from Makkah he went directly to Ghadeer-i Khumm; he did not return to Medina first.

*Wa li-hadha tunfara fihi al-dhunub* — And for this reason, sins are dispersed therein. Due to the shining of these *suwar*, what occurs there? Sins are forgiven, faults are pardoned, and *mawa'iz* and *balaya* (trials and tribulations) are removed and finished. *Wa fi al-mawaqifi quma'irun tahirah wa asrarun zahirah* — And in the stopping places are pure hidden treasures and manifest secrets. In the *mawaqif* — meaning Mina, Muzdalifah, and 'Arafat — at these stations of standing, there are *khama'ir* (hidden essences) that are very pure, and secrets that are manifest; things like *tayy al-ard* (the folding of the earth) are among them. *Wa li-hadha hurrima 'ala al-anam qatl al-ashjar* — And for this reason, it has been forbidden for mankind to cut down the trees and shrubs there. *Fi tilka al-jiha al-lati nahat fiha al-asrar* — in those regions in which the secrets are contained. *Fa-hiya mujawiratun li-tilka al-khama'ir al-kaminah* — for the trees are neighbours to those hidden *khama'ir*, mingled with the *dhubat al-batinah* (hidden essences). *Wa hadhihi qatratun min bihar 'ilm Mawla-i al-'Alam* — And this is a drop from the oceans of the knowledge of the Master of the World. *Wa sadaqatun min jumlati sadaqatihi al-mursalah 'ala 'abdihi al-mawjud al-mujid lahu min al-'adam* — And a charity from his many charities, sent upon his servant whom He brought into existence from nothingness. I was nothing at all, and from nothingness He made me, and made me capable of giving answers and clarifying realities. This is said about his *walid* (father).

*Jazahu Allahu 'an imami wa sayyidi afdala ma jaza al-du'at* — May Allah reward him on behalf of my Imam and my master with the best of what the *du'at* (supplicants and propagators) are rewarded. *Wa razaqna minhu maqbul al-shafa'at* — And grant us sustenance from his accepted intercessions. *Wa sunna 'an al-bayni al-ladhi ulqiya 'alayka bi-haqqi mawlaya* — And may we be protected from that veil which has been cast upon you, by the right of my Mawla, which is established upon you. *Wa man adha'a shay'an min hadhihi al-asrar fa-qad tabawwa'a maq'adahu min al-nar* — And whoever broadcasts anything of these secrets has certainly prepared his seat in the Fire. *Wa bari'un min al-'Aziz al-Ghaffar* — And he is disavowed by the Mighty, the Forgiving. Disavowed means he will be disassociated — Allah will be free of him. Just as in Surah al-Bara'ah: *bara'atun min Allahi* — displeasure is declared, that Allah and His Messenger's displeasure rests upon the polytheists. That is the meaning of it: to become independent of, to be free from.


The eleventh question, from Sayyidna Amin al-Mu'taman Sulayman ibn Hasan. The title of Amin al-Mu'taman was given to him by Sayyidna Da'ud ibn 'Ajabshah. Da'ud ibn 'Ajabshah had Sayyidna Sulayman ibn Hasan write two *nussus* (texts of appointment). The first *nass* is very long, written by the hand of Da'ud ibn Qutb, and at its conclusion he wrote that "*ana turabu na'layhi*" — I, Da'ud ibn Qutb, am the dust of the feet of Sayyidna Sulayman ibn Hasan. The second *nass* was dictated to Sayyidi Raj three days before his passing, in the year 970 Hijri. In this first *nass* from Sayyidna Da'ud ibn 'Ajabshah, he employed twenty-eight distinct titles. Sayyidna Muhammad ibn Fa'd states that these twenty-eight titles point to the *darajat* (ranks) of Sayyidna Sulayman — that he was the possessor of twenty-eight exalted titles, corresponding to the twenty-eight *huruf* (letters) of the *Kalimah al-Shahadah*. After this *nass*, whoever recites "La ilaha illa Allah, Muhammadun Rasulullah, 'Aliyyun Waliyullah" is required to bear an additional *shahadah* — a testimony for Sulayman ibn Hasan — otherwise he does not remain within the circle of Imamah. This was established by Sayyidna Muhammad ibn Fa'd al-'Alawi. When Sayyidna Da'ud passed away, his two sons Ibrahim and Ahmad were present with him, along with his wife.

Sayyidna Muhammad ibn Fa'd has offered magnificent praise for Sayyidna Sulayman. In one passage he states that Sayyidna Sulayman ibn Hasan stood, in relation to the Imam of his time, at the *maqam-i nasut* (station of humanity), while in relation to his *abna'-i jins* (fellow believers) — the circle of believers under him — he stood at the *maqam-i lahut* (station of divinity). This is a most profound declaration.

*Wa-iqamatuhu li-waladihi fi ardhi bi'rayn* — and his establishing of his son in the land of the two wells. Sayyidna Sulayman ibn Hasan established his son Ja'far at the place of two wells while he was traveling. The precise details of this incident are not fully known, as some accounts have been abbreviated in transmission.

Regarding Sayyidna Muhammad ibn Isma'il, *qaddasa Allahu ruhahu* — the one who appointed Sayyidna Hibatullah as Da'i and served as his support — *fa-qad sami'tu annahu salla jama'atan fi tarfi bilad al-habashah*: it is reported that he led a congregation in prayer in the lands of Habashah. *Fa-la-'amri la-qad faqa man mada* — by Allah's oath, he surpassed all who had come before him in this regard, for the *du'at* had never previously led prayer outside their designated three islands. The questioner then says: *fa-haqqiq li-'abdika al-faqir fi hadhihi al-mas'alah, bi-haqqi fulan, bi-haqqi fulan* — so investigate this matter for your humble servant, by the right of such-and-such, by the right of such-and-such. *Wa-lakin akhafu an tatla'a 'ala da'fi 'aqli* — but I fear that you will perceive the weakness of my intellect. *Wa-qad 'udtu bi-dhambi wa-dhilli wa-dhalili fa-ya harrun* — I come burdened with my sins, my abasement, and my lowliness, so O my protector.

Regarding the three islands: the Da'i of Iran would lead prayer within Iran, but the Da'i of Yemen was appointed over three specific islands and would not lead prayer outside them. Habashah and other regions each had their own Da'is. The island of Sham, for instance, is a distinct island, and no Da'i of Yemen prays there. The Da'i of Yemen is superior in rank to the *du'at* of all other islands, yet even so he confines himself to the three islands — Hind, Sindh, and Yemen. When Sayyidna 'Ali ibn Husayn al-Makrami traveled to London, he did not lead prayer there, as it lay outside his island. Similarly, no Da'i may lead prayer either outside his island or within another's island. The *mansub* (appointee) stationed in Najran holds a *mansub* superior to all other *manasib* (appointments), even if appointed at a later time, because his nobility and station are elevated — he is, *bi-l-quwwah* (by potential), the successor of the Da'i, possessing the eligibility to become the Da'i's khalifah regardless of how many other *manasib* there may be.

*Wa-qad ahattat bi dhambi wa-yuhallu bi qarbi* — my sin has encompassed me, yet it is resolved by my closeness to you. Pardon my sins and resolve the debt that weighs upon me. *Wa-lakin aqulu ya Allahu ya man azhara al-jamila wa-satara al-qabiha* — but I say: O Allah, O You who make the beautiful manifest and conceal the ugly. This is in fact the supplication of Husayn ibn 'Ali. *Fa-mnun 'alayya bi-ihsanika wa-fadlika* — so favor me with Your grace and Your bounty. *Fa-kayfa akhafu wa-ihsanuka awsa'u wa-fadluka 'indi ajma'u* — how then should I fear, when Your favor is most vast and Your grace is most complete with me, and blessings have been continuously bestowed upon me? *Wa-la ziltu fi hifzi al-da'i wa-husni ri'ayatihi* — and I have never ceased to be in the protection and good care of the Da'i, meaning in Allah's protection. By the right of Sayyidna Muhammad and his pure and noble family.

The answer to that question follows, with the help of Allah the Great and Exalted, by the bounty of the Wali of the Age — may the blessings of the Merciful be upon him — and with the support of Mawla, the Most Luminous Secret and the Great Ocean, and with the support of Mawla Hibatullah and his shining proof.

When *ifadah* and *istifadah* (imparting and receiving benefit) take place — when the one who imparts knowledge and the one who receives it sit together — the *suwar* (souls) of believers who have passed away, who carried within them a longing for knowledge, find that longing still alive, though they can no longer act upon it. They have no body, and thus cannot pray, fast, perform Hajj, engage in Jihad, or carry out any deed that pertains to bodily existence. Yet the quest for knowledge persists even after death. When such *majalis* (gatherings) are held — gatherings in which the words of the Awliya' Allah are spoken and discussed, in which *ifadah* and *istifadah* take place — those souls gather there. And when a questioner puts forth a question, in reality it is not the questioner alone who asks; through his tongue, those souls ask their question. It is as though the *hadd-i 'ali* (high-ranking representative) causes the question to be asked through him.

In this connection, an incident comes to mind. Dhul-Ma'ali, in the old Ziyarah Muqaddasah, had been giving *asbaq* (lessons) in his house — in the mosque after the Fajr prayer — due to pain in his leg. Then an incident arose: a spirit possessed the mother of Hadrat Ziyarah Sahib Muqaddas, casting a shadow over her. When Hadrat Ziyarah Sahib Muqaddas questioned the spirit through his knowledge, it emerged that she was the soul of a Bohra woman. He rebuked her, but she said she had come with no ill intention — she wished to enter the noble community. He then administered the *'ahd al-sharif* (noble covenant) to her, and she departed. But after some time she returned. He rebuked her sharply, reminding her of her promise to leave and not return. She replied that she had not come of her own accord but had been sent — sent by his Mawla and hers, Fardali Sahib Muqaddas, who had already passed away. She had been sent with three messages. The first was that the *asbaq* being conducted at the Ziyah Manzil should be moved to the mosque, because the *suwarat* (souls) who attend have a longing to hear them and gather there for that purpose. The second was that Ja'far 'Ali Sahib — the forefather of Maqsood bhai and Safdar bhai now in Hyderabad — was building a house and was sitting beneath a wall for shade while supervising the workers; he should be warned that the wall was about to fall and he must not sit there. A third matter was also conveyed, though the precise details of it have not been retained in memory.

One sees from this how such things come about, and how the spiritual dimension enters into worldly affairs. Similarly, it is recounted that Sayyidna Sulayman ibn Hasan was once delivering a sermon in a *majlis* with the *ikhwan* present, when Sayyidna 'Ali ibn Sulayman stood up in the midst of it and said, "Mawla, you have graced my *majlis* with your presence, and it is a great honor that you have come. Please pray for me." Then he sat down. The gathering was astonished. This happened on several occasions, and when people asked what was occurring, he said that Sayyidna Sulayman ibn Hasan had come — that he had perceived his presence there.

When the Da'udis filed a case in the time of Sayyidna 'Ali, Sayyidna 'Ali and Hasan Khan ibn 'Abd al-Malik were required to appear in court. The night before their appearance, Sayyidna Ja'far would come to Sayyidna 'Ali Sahib in a dream and say: "Tomorrow the judge will ask you this question — give this answer." The next day they would appear, the judge would ask the very question foretold, and they would give the prescribed answer. On another occasion, when they were greatly troubled by a government matter, a person appeared before both Sayyidna 'Ali ibn Sulayman and Sayyidna Hasan Khan ibn 'Abd al-Malik and recited a verse of the Quran to them: "*La takhafa, inna ana ma'akuma*" — do not fear, for I am with you both. With this they were consoled and their hearts steadied.

Another incident: Sayyidna Husayn ibn Ahmad had been placed under a form of house arrest in Ta'if. From there he would come to Baitullah, and during his stays in Makkah he would perform seven *tawaf* every day without fail. On one occasion, while performing *tawaf*, he raised both hands and cried out, "*Wa shauqah, wa shauqah*" — O my longing, O my longing. The companions and *hudud* who were with him had never witnessed such a state in him, and afterward they asked him about it. He said that he had been seeing three *suwar* — the souls of Sayyidi Fardali Sahib Muqaddas, Sayyidi Ziyali Sahib Muqaddas, and Sayyidna Ghulam Husayn Sahib Muqaddas — performing *tawaf* before him. They were circling with great speed, but he, encumbered by his body, could not match their pace. The sight of them intensified his longing, his *shauq*, because he could not perform *tawaf* as they did.

On yet another occasion, a person in Najran recounted that he was sitting beside Sayyidna Husayn ibn Isma'il, both of them reciting — for Sayyidna Husayn ibn Isma'il was known to recite the Quran abundantly and with great speed. As they were reciting, a figure appeared and extended his hand toward Sayyidna Husayn ibn Isma'il. The moment Sayyidna Husayn ibn Isma'il saw him, he kissed the figure's hand and immediately rose to his feet — and at that moment the figure vanished. It is possible that this was Imam 'Ali, *'alayhi al-salam*, himself.


So, with your support, Mawla-i 'Alam, and with his magnificent secret, from the affair of Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan — Sulayman-i Humam, Sulayman al-Imam. The story of Sulayman-i Humam is that he, in the *jazirah* (island), is Sulayman, and one of his forms is established in Hadramawt, whose name is Humam. This is something that has not happened with anyone else. Because the Imam of your time is the 35th Imam, and he is the seventh (*sabi'*) of his cycle (*haftah*). And not only this, but he is included among those four Imams — Sayyidna Ahmad bin Muhammad — whose greatness and station are very high. You have combined two cycles (*haftahs*); you are the possessor of 14 Imams. So, due to these circumstances, your station is very high. And when the Imam's station is high, the Da'i's station also becomes high.

*Wa-iqamatuhu li-waladihi Sayyidna Ja'far* (And his establishing of his son, Sayyidna Ja'far) — and you established your son in Bi'rayn, among the people. *Wa-dhalika kama fa'ala asluhu al-'azim fi al-dawr al-qadim* (And that is as his great forefather did in the ancient period). So this matter happened just as in the ancient period, when Sulayman bin Hasan and Ja'far bin Sulayman appeared — you did it then, and so now too you have done the same. What that incident is, I do not know. *Allahu yahdi man yasha'u ila siratin mustaqim* (Allah guides whom He wills to a straight path). *Wa-aydhan lamma ittasalat maddatu imamatihi ilayhi* (Also, when the substance of his Imamah connected to him) — when the Imam's confirmations connected with you, *dhakara qurbanahu wa-mathubatahu* (he mentioned his sacrifice and his offering), that you should present your sacrifice.

*Wa-amma sallatu Sayyidna Badruddin* (And as for the prayer of Sayyidna Badruddin) — *'ala qudsuhu fi lina kama dhakarta fi ghayri hadhihi al-jazirah al-yamaniyyah* (may his soul be sanctified, in a land as you mentioned, in other than this Yemeni island). The prayer led by Sayyidna Badruddin — that is, Sayyidna Muhammad bin Isma'il — his leading of the prayer, as you have mentioned, in other than the island of Yemen: that island was not Yemen's. That is, the prayer he led in Habashah was — *fa-dhalika li-'izzati rutbatihi al-bari'ah* (so that was due to his sublime and great rank). So that matter came to pass because your rank is very sublime and great among the people. *Wa-li-ihtijabi imamihi 'anhu bi-suratihi al-mu'azzamah* (And because his Imam became veiled from him with his magnificent form) — the Imam became veiled from you with his magnificent form in the island. *Wal-idhnu lahu mu'adhdhinan lahu bi-l-salah fi ghayri jazirah al-mukkaramah* (And the permission for him to lead the prayer in other than the honored island) — and the Imam gave you permission to lead the prayer outside the honored island, meaning outside the island of Yemen. *Wa-lahu sha'nun 'azimun* (And his is a great station). *Wa-khatbun jasimun* (And a weighty matter). *Wa-ma hajara min ardhihi wa-baladihi illa li-'adhami sha'nihi* (And he did not migrate from his land and his country except for the greatness of his station). You did not migrate from your land and your country but on account of your great and magnificent state. You were in Sa'fan, in Yemen, but you migrated to Najran and went to Habuna — and you did so because of that greatness and magnificence. *Wa-quwwatihi al-ruhaniyyah wa-li-ihya'i marasim al-da'wah* (And for the strength of his spiritual power and to revive the rituals of the Da'wah) — and due to your spiritual powers, and in order to revive the rituals and secrets of the Da'wah.

Did he have a personal meeting with the Imam? No — the Imam's luminous form became veiled from him. I do not know how that happens. There is a treatise by Kamaluddin, Sayyidna Husayn bin Ahmad himself — *Al-Hijab wal-Muhtajib* (The Veil and the Veiled). It is a small treatise, but it is very difficult; it is not easy to grasp what he intends to say. These states are beyond comprehension — how a veiled one is veiled by a veil, because the veil is actually...

He is going for a test. I am stopping the lesson here. It is alright, Father. I am finishing now. He has arrived. Let us recite the Fatihah. Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. O Allah...

Maulana, may I?

Attendees, I am stopping the lesson here. A doctor has arrived, for whose sake I must get up. I will recite the Fatihah, and we will continue the lesson tomorrow, Insha'Allah. We will continue tomorrow. Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. We present the Fatihah as a gift and a connection — pure and pleasing — to the soul of Sayyidna 'Ali ibn Hibatullah; may Allah sanctify his soul and illuminate his resting place, and may his blessings, lights, and supports be multiplied for us, and may He accept from us this imparting and receiving of benefit in the best possible way. By the right of Sayyidna Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, peace and blessings be upon them. Al-Fatihah. Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi Rabb al-'alamin, al-Rahman al-Rahim, Maliki Yawm al-Din. Iyyaka na'budu wa iyyaka nasta'in. Ihdina al-sirat al-mustaqim. Sirat al-ladhina an'amta 'alayhim, ghayr al-maghdubi 'alayhim wa-la al-dallin. Amin.

*Wa-bi-niyyati Sayyidi Mawlaya al-Mansub al-Mutlaq fi al-Jazirah al-Hindiyyah al-Sharifah ila al-Faqih al-Nabil, al-'Alim al-Jalil al-Hajj al-Sharafi Usamauddin Ghulam Husayn ibn Sayyid al-Mansub al-Muqaddas Nasrullah ibn Hibatullah ibn al-Mansub al-Muqaddas Fathullah. Tawwala wa-abqa'ahu Allahu Ta'ala, yahfazuhu wa-yahmihi wa-yubqihi bi-tayyibi 'ayshihi min sharri jami' al-amrad wal-asqam, min sharri al-awja' wal-a'lam, min sharri al-ghilal wal-hijam, min sharri al-nufus al-khabisat wal-fusufat, min al-taqdidat wal-tathrisat, wa-bi'ati al-munazarat, wa-yudda wa-yazid fi nasrihi wa-yahfazuhu fi ma'uni 'amrihi min 'aybi al-nawafis wal-nuqsan, wa-yahmihi fi al-hifazat al-rabbaniyyah al-shahaniyyah, wa-tawala Allahu fi tuli hayatihi, wa-af'ali wa-kalamihi wa-darajatihi, wa-yashkurana iyyakum fi sa'ati da'awatihi, wa-yadhkuruna iyyakum fi anfa'i hadaratihi, bi-jahi Sayyid al-Mursalin, Sayyidina Mawlana al-Mubayyin wa-Ahl Baytihi al-Tayyibin al-Tahirin, salawatullahi wa-salamuhu 'alayhim ajma'in. Al-Fatihah.*

Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi Rabb al-'alamin, al-Rahman al-Rahim, Maliki Yawm al-Din. Iyyaka na'budu wa iyyaka nasta'in. Ihdina al-sirat al-mustaqim. Sirat al-ladhina an'amta 'alayhim, ghayr al-maghdubi 'alayhim wa-la al-dallin. Amin.

Thank you. Fi amanillah.

<!-- lecture 13 -->


<!-- page 62 -->

...the matter of praying in the *ghayr jazirah* was under discussion — in Habashah, so its mention was coming up. This is page 62 in my book. Which book do you have? Twenty-eight. Okay. Is there a *da'wah* in Habashah? Yes, there is a *da'wah* in Habashah. There is a *da'wah*. But they do not give allegiance to our *Da'i*. No. It was up to the time of Sayyidna Husayn bin Isma'il — up to Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan. Ali bin Ali related that Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan had sent him to Habashah to establish a *qiyam* there. Yes. So after returning from there, perhaps in Shahwallah, he had established the *qiyam*. A strange thing I had learned — I had not mentioned it to you before. There was a Sajjad, who had said that Syedi sahib had said that your father, in the last phase of his life, would be the *Da'i* of Habashah. The Prophet had not given permission for that. In the *ghayr jazirah*. And he was given permission for prayer in the *ghayr jazirah* of Makkah al-Mukarramah. *Wa lahu sha'nun 'adhim wa maqamun jasim* — and he has a great station and a lofty rank. Your station is very great and your rank very high. *Wa ma hajar min ardihi wa baldatihi illa li-'adhm shanihi* — and he did not migrate from his land and his city except for the greatness of his station. You did not perform *hijrah* from the land where you were born, from that land and from your city, except that it was for your greatness and station. The people of Satan, when you presented a *nadhranah* — so that *hijrah* which was performed was because of your great station. Yes. And after that, after many years, Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan went to those places where Sayyidna Mahmud bin Isma'il had performed *hijrah*. He then stayed there for several months. He also built a *masjid* there — Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan — and he also built a *Dar al-Hijrah* there. And then later, in our time, Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan built a magnificent *Dar al-Hijrah* there, and it was a very splendid *masjid*. Yes.

So then when we went there, I said that we were sitting in the *masjid*. I said, "Malik, if you permit, I will recite about Sayyidna Sulayman sahib." He said, "Yes." I recited the *qasidah*. It was a very long *majlis* — Sayyidna sahib kept listening with concentration.

*Wa li-ihya'i marasim al-da'wah* — and for the revival of the ceremonies of the Da'wah. You — the *marasim al-da'wah*, meaning those written documents and the *nusus* that were there — you reissued them, or brought them to notice. The things you did in the Da'wah, you did all of these things. *Wa fi'iluhu bi-al-wa'il* — and his action by the earlier ones — until they were lying in the abyss. But you brought them into action; you made people aware that these have been written in the past, these documents have been made, these writings the *du'at* have ordained — apart from the books. So you manifested that.

*Wa li-istikhraj al-sawa' al-kamil min bilad al-Yaman* — and for extracting the complete secrets from the lands of Yemen. And the things that were hidden in the lands of Yemen — *bilad al-Yaman* — you brought them to light. *Min al-sadis* — from the sixth — *shurur al-sharifah* — the noble secrets. And you brought those *shurur al-sharifah* to light.

*Wa law tulina al-kalam* — and if we were to prolong the speech — and if we speak on this, it will become very long. The ranks of the *du'at* are *rutab al-'aliyah* — high ranks. And the ranks of the *du'at*, and besides that the ranks that are there, the high ranks — they are different. *Fi 'uluww al-darajat* — in the loftiness of degrees. They are different. Meaning it is not one type of rank, because of their lofty degrees.

*Li-anna kull al-martabah fi dhatiha sab' rutab* — because every rank in itself has seven ranks. *Yu-khafi min al-asrar al-thaniyah* — hidden from the second secrets. And every rank is divided into seven ranks, meaning the high secrets there are, according to that. Meaning, if there is a *da'i*, then one *da'i* is divided into seven ranks, and among them is one who is in the highest rank among the seven ranks. Sometimes it happens that a *da'i* is in the highest rank among the seven ranks.

*Inda dhawi al-adhan al-saqiyah* — with those of piercing intellect — among the *dhawi al-adhan al-saqiyah*. *Wa minhum man yakunu fi awsatihi* — and among them is one who is in its middle. Some *du'at* are in the middle of these ranks. *Tilka al-rutab al-madhkurah* — those mentioned ranks. Yes — whose mention has been made. And among them is one who is in the lowest rank. And some *du'at* are in its lowest rank. Yes.

— And who decides which rank you are in?
— That is the Imam of the time. The Imam of the time decides which *da'i* is in which rank. And then its declaration happens later. The succeeding *du'at* say that he was in that rank, and his rank was this. Its mention is in our books. Some *du'at* have also expressed their ranks — that this one was in such-and-such rank, or that one was in such-and-such rank.

Then it is said that among them there are also such *du'at* who — because of their past *sawabit*, their *hasanat*, the good deeds that were theirs — *ila adna rutab al-hujjiyah* — come to the lowest rank of the *rutbah* of *hujjiyah*. Meaning, they leave the rank of *da'i* and come to the lowest rank of *hujjat*. *Ma fawqaha min rutab al-'aliyah* — what is above it from the high ranks. Or they go to a rank even higher than that. Meaning, the rank of *hujjat* also has several ranks, so they go into that as well. Yes.

A *hujjat* is of two types. One is the rank above the *du'at* — that is the rank of *hujjat*. And one *hujjat* is also what the son of the Imam is called. The son of the Imam is also called a *hujjat*.

— Today, are our *da'i*s... this...
— Yes, yes. All the *du'at* who came after Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan, until the *Qiyamah*, they were all established in the rank of *hujjat*. Meaning, their ranks were elevated and they were established in the rank of *hujjat*. This is their *shan*. Meaning, the *qa'imiyyat* — the *du'at* of the era of the *qa'im* — because there are three eras, right? The *dawr al-nabawi*, the *dawr al-tayyibi*, and the *dawr al-qa'imi*. The *dawr al-nabawi* lasted until Mawlana Ali al-Tayyib. And then after Mawlana Ali al-Tayyib, the *dawr al-tayyibi* happened. Until Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan, then the *dawr al-qa'imi* began. When 1000 years were completed, the *dawr al-qa'imi* began.

*Wa minhum man yakunu mustawda'an* — and among them is one who is a temporary trustee. Now look, this is a special point. And among those *du'at*, there is also this — that some are of the rank of *mustawda'* (temporary). And — *wa fi al-mustawda'... wa minhum man yakunu mustaqarran fi al-istida'* — and some are *mustaqarr* (permanent) in the *istida'*. *Istida'* is the rank of *du'at*; it is a *mustawda'* rank. Yes. And the rank of the *a'immah* is *mustaqarr*. So in *istida'*, some are *mustawda'in*, and some *du'at* become *mustaqarrin*. Yes.

— Can you pinpoint?
— Like it was said that Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan — despite being so learned, and despite the greatness he had, that an example was set in your presences, the *shan* that Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan had — despite that, he was in the rank of *mustawda'in*. And Sayyidna Ja'far bin Sulayman was *mustaqarr*. And other *du'at* came after him. Yes. Well, not all *du'at* have been mentioned, but some *du'at* became *mustaqarr*. But among them, the first was Sayyidna Ja'far bin Sulayman, who was in the rank of *mustaqarr* — meaning in the *istida'iyyat*, he became *mustaqarr*. Yes. They are all *mustawda'in*, but in the *istida'iyyat* he became *mustaqarr*. So Sayyidna Ja'far's rank became even higher. He reached the station of *hujjiyah*. Yes.

— Like the Nabi's was.
— Yes. The Nabi's station is something else. But...
— Mawlana, the definition of *mustaqarr* is...
— That is...
— The succession of son after father — that is what we...
— No, not like that. It is not like that. That is only in the case of the Imams. The lineage of son from father is among the Imams. But this is that *mustawda'* and *mustaqarr* means — its explanation has not come fully, but their rank moves from *istida'* towards *istiqrar* (permanence). And it is possible that they become masters of special *majami'*. Allah knows best.
— From *mustawda'* to *mustaqarr* is higher — that is what we understand from this passage.
— Yes, yes. It could be that they become masters of *majami'*, special ones — that special support is obtained by them from the Imam.

Just as Sayyidna Ja'far also received. When Sayyidna Ja'far went to Janad — there is a place in Yemen called Janad — so when he went to the *masjid* there, he was on a journey. With him was Sayyidna Ibrahim, his brother, meaning the son of Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fawz. And with him was Sayyidna Husayn bin Ahmad, his sister's son. And there was a Sindhi brother with him on the journey. So right at the time of *Maghrib*, when the time for *Maghrib* was very near, Sayyidna Ja'far said, "Look, at this moment the *tajalli* of the Imam is upon me. And at this moment, whatever *du'a* you make, it will be accepted." So Sayyidna Ibrahim bin Muhammad bin Fawz made this *du'a*, that his son should be a *Da'i* and the lineage of *du'at* should continue in his progeny until the *Qiyamah*. He made this *du'a*. And Sayyidna Husayn bin Ahmad said that he should obtain such knowledge as neither the first ones have received nor the last ones have received. So then he wrote such a magnificent book — *Badi'at al-Bayan*, that book, and the *Hashiyah* on the *Anwar*, that book — and other books, in which the *du'at* are astonished and perplexed. The *du'at mustaqarrin* are astonished and perplexed. And when they mention his name, they call him Sayyidna Husam — while he was not *mustaqarr*; he remained *ghayr munfarid*. But they mention his name with great respect and call him Sayyidna Husam. And then the Sindhi brother who was there — his handwriting was very poor — so he said that his handwriting should become very good. So, just as the capacity was, so were the supplications, and his handwriting also became very good.

This narration we were reading — in *hijrat*, in the time of Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan. Between *Maghrib* and *'Isha*, the book of *Sirah* of the *du'at* used to be read. So incidents of the *du'at*'s *sirah* would come up. So one time, Sayyidna sahib — this incident passed. So Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan looked at Ghulam and said, "Ghulam, you people are always worried about your handwriting." At that time, Syedi 'Abdullah had arrived from Hindustan — Hadrat 'Abdullah sahib had arrived. So Sayyidna sahib said, "Mawlana, he was not a Hindi, he was a Sindhi." Sayyidna sahib passed away.

— Syedna Husayn bin Ahmad Dhul-Jaddayn you are talking about?
— Yes. Dhul-Jaddayn.
— Yes, yes.
— No, no. The one who is in Baroda — Sayyidna — he is not Dhul-Jaddayn. He is Ali bin Husayn... Ali bin Husayn bin Ahmad bin Husayn bin Dhul-Jaddayn — Husayn bin Ahmad bin Dhul-Jaddayn. Meaning, Sayyidna Husayn bin Ahmad whose story is being told with Sayyidna Ja'far — he is his grandson. He was sent in the time of Sayyidna Hibatullah, Ali bin Husayn. So this is actually a misunderstanding that the descendant of Dhul-Jaddayn is here — but the one here is not his son, it is his grandson. He passed away at 45 years of age.
— In Baroda.
— Yes. And he was a great scholar, that person who is buried here. He was a very great scholar. Yes.
— That is why Sayyidna Sulayman sahib always...
— Yes.
— ...referred to Sayyidna Ja'far as "my mawla."
— Yes. *Al-Mawla*. "Mawla Ja'far, Mawla Ja'far." What a great station it must be, that the father — the *abyat* that Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan wrote, in which he described the *shan* of Sayyidna Ja'far bin Sulayman — there the word he used is "Mawla Ja'far, Mawla Ja'far." You can imagine. Then Sayyidna Ja'far, *qaddas Allahu ruhahu*, after his passing, transcribed it. His handwriting was very strange and unusual, completely unique. So he transcribed it, and he removed the word "Mawla." Yes. When Sayyidna Sulayman sahib passed away, Sayyidna Ja'far was 11 years old. Despite that, at such a young age — if he had been much older and he called him "Mawla," the matter would be understandable. But at such a young age, it was said: "my Mawla Ja'far."

But Sayyidna Husayn — Ali bin Husayn — the one just mentioned, he is the one whose *rawdah* is...
— Yes, Hadrat Shakir sahib is buried there.
— Yes, he is the one.
— ...is called the *rawdah* of Dhul-Jaddayn.
— Yes. And in it, Hadrat Shakir sahib Muqaddas is also there, and 'Azizah Bu is also there. On one side is Hadrat Shakir sahib Muqaddas, and on the other side is 'Azizah Bu. Yes.
— Yesterday was his day, wasn't it?
— Yes? Yesterday was his day. Yes, yesterday was his day. Yes.

And when Sayyidna Ali bin Husayn al-Makrami came to Hindustan, Hadrat Shakir sahib Muqaddas was alive. So he requested in Baroda, "Mawlana, my age has advanced, and *kullu nafsin dha'iqat al-mawt* — every soul will taste death — so I want to select the place for my burial — where I will be buried." So Syedi sahib looked at all four sides and then said, "No, your place should be here." Meaning, where he was present — on one side, there are four corners — so in one corner he is buried, in one corner is 'Azizah Bu. 'Azizah Bu was already there. He said, "This is your place." Yes.

— Hadrat Shakir sahib had chosen another spot?
— Yes, he had thought of being near Dhul-Jaddayn. Yes. He said, "This is the place."

Mawlana, about Sayyidna Sulayman sahib — what you mentioned about Sayyidna Ja'far — at the birth, what was that — was that not an indication? That your *Da'i* has been born. Meaning, greater than you — because at that time Sayyidna Sulayman sahib had not yet become the *Da'i*. Yes, yes. So was that not an indication? Yes. At that time it was the era of Sayyidna Da'ud bin 'Ajab Shah, right? Who passed away in 999 Hijri. Yes. So, before that is the matter — that when Sayyidna Sulayman sahib... when Khadijah bint Nuh was pregnant and Sayyidna Ja'far bin Sulayman was in the womb — Sayyidna Sulayman sahib said to Janab Khadijah bint Nuh, "Your son is very blessed." So she said, "Mawla, who knows whether it will be a boy or a girl?" So he said, "It will be a boy, and he will be the *Da'i* of his time." And he also said, "You will serve his *da'wah* greatly." The believer sees with the light of Allah. That this happened — and the second thing that happened was that for Khadijah bint Nuh, what did he say? He used the term *rijal al-da'wah* — that she is wearing an earthly dress, but in reality she is from the *rijal al-da'wah*. She is one of the *rijal al-da'wah*.

And Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fawz, *'ala al-usul*, mentioned to Sayyidna Ja'far — when the *intiqal* had happened — he mentioned that, "I think that for the prosperity and well-being of the *da'wah*, I should marry the wife of Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan." Sayyidna Ja'far related this to his mother. So she said, "I will ponder this matter." So then after some time, she agreed. And she was such a blessed wife — you can imagine — that from Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan, Sayyidna Yusuf bin Sulayman was born. From Sayyidna Ja'far bin Sulayman, Sayyidna 'Abd al-Tayyib was born, and Sayyidna Ali was born. And all four became *du'at*. Sayyidna Yusuf and Sayyidna 'Abd al-Tayyib were *du'at ghayr munfarid*. And Sayyidna Ja'far and Sayyidna Ali sahib were *munfarid du'at*. And Sayyidna 'Abd al-Tayyib wrote many books — his handwriting was very fast and very clear. He wrote several books. Yes.

And then, when he married Mawlana Khadijah bint Nuh, Sayyidna Ibrahim sahib was born — and other children were also born. Yes. And Sayyidna Ibrahim sahib — when in Yemen the Zaidi king had imprisoned him — for 14 years he remained in prison. And for those 14 years, Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan used to say, "Ghulam, the love of you Hindustanis for the *du'at* is astonishing and strange. Our grandfather, when he was in prison, a Hindustani brother was there who would perform *Hajj* for him." In those days, performing *Hajj* was a very difficult task — with great difficulties *Hajj* was performed. "For our grandfather, a Hindustani brother would perform *Hajj* every year." So what a great matter of distinction this is.

— The lineage of *du'at* continued through Sayyidna Ibrahim.
— Yes.
— None of his brothers...
— No, none of his brothers. Sayyidna Ibrahim sahib had appointed his son Isma'il. But he passed away in his lifetime. Then after that, he appointed Sayyidna Muhammad bin Isma'il — his grandson. On his grandson he appointed. Yes.

*Kama kana sayakun* — as it was, so it will be. *Wa ma 'ulima sayu'lam* — and what was known will be known. And just as it has been known, it will certainly be known. *Wa ma taht al-shams shay'un bi-jadid* — and there is nothing new under the sun. And under the sun there is no new thing.

I am taking the covenant of Allah, which is very firm. *Wa mithaquhu al-shadid* — and His covenant is strong. And His covenant is very strong. *Alayka ayyuha al-akh al-sa'id* — upon you, O fortunate brother — upon you, O my fortunate brother, in protecting the secrets of *al-'Aliy al-Majid*. And this noble covenant is that you will protect the secrets of *al-'Aliy al-Majid*. Yes.

*Wa man nadhara ila hadha al-kitab bi-ghayr idhn al-da'i al-majid* — and whoever looks at this book without the permission of the glorious Da'i. And whoever looks at this book without the permission of the *Da'i al-Majid* — *aw mimman lahu 'adw al-'ilm* — or from one who holds the reins of knowledge — or without his permission, in whose hand is the rein of knowledge, meaning who makes decisions in the *da'wah* — without his permission. *Wa al-nadhr al-sadid* — and with sound insight — and who has a special insight, meaning he is high in rank. Without his permission, if you look, then *fa-qad bari'a min al-Mubdi' al-Mu'id* — then he is free from the Originator, the Restorer. So he will be free from *Mubdi' al-Mu'id*.

*Wa qawluhu... man adha'a sirrana adhaqahu Allah bard al-hadid* — whoever spreads our secret, Allah will make him taste the coldness of iron. "Whoever broadcasts our secret, Allah will make him taste the punishment of *bard al-hadid* — cold iron." Meaning, the punishment of cold iron. Cold iron's punishment is what? That he will be in Qutb Shumali or Qutb Janubi. Meaning — the three punishments, right? One is *'adhab al-adna*, *'adhab al-awsat*, and *'adhab al-akbar*. So he will go into the *'adhab al-awsat*. Yes.

— Meaning, without permission, if he reads and...
— Yes, without permission, if he reads and...

And now we are concluding this book — Alhamdulillah — we are concluding this book. *Al-'Aliy al-Wahhab* — the High, the Bestower — with His praise, and *al-salat wa al-salam 'ala sayyidina Muhammad* — blessings and peace be upon our master Muhammad — *al-muntaqil min al-aslab al-tahirah ila al-arham al-mutahharah* — the one who transferred from the pure loins to the purified wombs. And may Allah send blessings and peace upon our master Muhammad, who kept transferring from the pure loins of the pure forefathers to the pure and holy wombs. Meaning, none of his ancestors committed *shirk* — meaning they were all pure and clean, because they were the *a'immah* of their time. So how could they commit *shirk*? All of them were righteous and extremely — the essence of the universe, those people were. The *a'immah* of the time.

*Wa 'ala akhihi wa wasiyyihi* — and upon his brother and his legatee. And the two sons of Janab Ibrahim — one was Isma'il and the other was Ishaq. So you are from the progeny of Janab Isma'il, not from the progeny of Janab Ishaq. And from the progeny of Janab Isma'il came the *a'immah* — *mustaqarr*. Yes. And from the progeny of Janab Ishaq came the *anbiya'*. You see, Janab Isma'il's station is very high — with Janab Ibrahim, in the building of the Ka'bah, in placing the Hajar al-Aswad, he was also present. And in raising the walls, he was also with Janab Ibrahim. And the genesis of Zamzam also happened through his feet. Everything is there — but his rank is less than his son's. His son, Qaydar — Qaydar's rank is less than his. Because Qaydar is the seventh Imam. Yes.

*Wa sallallahu 'alayhi wa 'ala akhihi wa wasiyyihi* — and may Allah send blessings upon him and upon his brother and his legatee. *Al-mu'in al-a'immah al-ghurr* — the helper of the brilliant Imams — *bi-wasitat al-pak* — through the medium of the Pure one — *fi al-sirr al-mustasirr* — in the hidden secret. *Wa 'ala al-a'immah al-akhyar, majami' al-anwar* — and upon the righteous Imams, the collections of lights. And the *a'immah al-akhyar*, who are the *majami' al-anwar*. *Wa 'ala Imam al-zaman, al-natiq, al-mubdi'... fi hal al-wiladah bi-'alaniyah* — and upon the Imam of the time, the speaker, the originator, who in the state of birth spoke openly. The *Mubdi'* — in the state of *wiladah* — spoke with him openly. This they are saying. *Wa al-radi' fi hadd al-tufuliyyah ila awan al-zuhur bi-nuraniyyah* — and the infant at the stage of childhood until the time of manifestation with luminosity. And he progressed from the stage of childhood towards manifestation, until his manifestation happened with *nuraniyyah*. *Wa sallam 'alayhim* — and peace be upon them.

— Whom is he talking about?
— He is talking about the Imam of the time — that the Imam of the time, whose rank is such that *Mubdi' Ta'ala* spoke with him in the state of *wiladah* openly. And he progressed from the state of childhood towards manifestation, until your — his — manifestation happened. Meaning, he grew up. With *nuraniyyah*. *Wa sallam 'alayhim* — and peace be upon them. And may blessings be upon them all — and upon their forefathers, and upon their pure progeny, and upon their *du'at*, who are *al-mayamin*, noble and honorable. *Al-na'ibina manabahum ila qiyam yawm al-din* — their representatives until the establishment of the Day of Judgment.

— The mention of *Mubdi' Ta'ala* — would that not be something elevated? Meaning, 'Aql al-Awwal or 'Aql al-Thani, if mentioned, it would make sense, but *Mubdi' Ta'ala* is...
— Yes. Now, by *Mubdi' Ta'ala*, it is possible that the term *Mubdi'* has been used but it refers to the ranks below it. Because *Mubdi' Ta'ala*... it so happened that...
— Nowhere else has the mention of *Mubdi' Ta'ala* come.
— Yes. I mean, by *Mubdi' Ta'ala*, that is not the intent — the one who brought existence from non-existence. That is not the intent. Because what he did — he brought existence from non-existence and it finished. After that, he has no other action. He has no other action. Except that it was asked — will he do this action again? Bringing existence from non-existence? So it was said that he will never do such a thing — he will not do it again. So he said, "What? Does he not have the power?" He said, "No, despite having the power, he will not do this." Yes. And about him — since when he is, what he is, what he is doing, and why he did it — the *du'at* have no answer for that. Except this: that he did it, and it was his *tafaddul* and *mihrabani* that he did such a thing. That is all the answer there is.

Now this is that 'Aql al-Awwal — the *ma'rifah* that he had of *Mubdi' Ta'ala* — from that, 'Aql al-Awwal became a *hijab*, a *bab*, and a continuous and flowing knowledge was established upon it, which can never stop. For this reason it is said that *la ta'khudhuhu sinatun wa la nawm* — neither slumber nor sleep overtakes him. That he neither feels sleepiness nor is overcome by slumber, 'Aql al-Awwal. Meaning, in sleepiness a person becomes partially heedless. And in sleep, he becomes completely heedless. Neither is he partially heedless, 'Aql al-Awwal, nor is he completely heedless. Yes. And *al-Hayy al-Qayyum* — the Living, the Self-Sustaining — he has become. Meaning, alive and life-giving. This. Until it was said that *al-Hayy al-Qayyum*, which is one of Allah's names and is among the high ones — and in this *al-Hayy al-Qayyum*, the *Ism al-A'dham* is present. Some such words are there, that it is said — like *Dhu al-Jalal wa al-Ikram*, *Ya Dha al-Jalal wa al-Ikram* — in this the *Ism al-A'dham* is present. Or *al-Hayy al-Qayyum*, which is the *tasbih* for Sunday. So *al-Hayy al-Qayyum* points towards 'Aql al-Awwal — that he is alive and life-giving.

And then what did he do? The substance and support that 'Aql al-Awwal received from *Mubdi' Ta'ala* — he gave to 'Aql al-Thani, meaning al-Munba'ith al-Awwal, only two and a half percent of it. He gave two and a half percent of it — like it is in *zakat*. So whatever things were gathered with him, he gave a two and a half percent portion of it. Because if he had given the whole thing, his existence would have been consumed. And in the same way, al-Munba'ith al-Awwal gave to the ranks below him two and a half percent of his. So as it went on, and that which is the Qur'an al-


<!-- lecture 14 -->

*As-salamu alaykum wa rahmatullahi wa barakatuh*. *Wa alaykum as-salam*. *As-salamu alaykum wa rahmatullah*. *Wa alaykum as-salam*. *As-salamu alaykum*. *Wa alaykum as-salam*.

*A'udhu billahi minash shaitanir rajim* — I seek refuge in Allah from the accursed Satan. *Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim* — In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful. *Wa bihi nasta'in, sallallahu ala rasulih, Sayyidina Muhammadin wa ala aali Muhammadin wa barik wa sallim* — And in Him we seek help; may Allah bless His Messenger, our master Muhammad, and the family of Muhammad, and grant them blessings and peace.

The first question concerns *Mawlana Mehdi*, *sallallahu alayhi*. There are, in fact, two figures bearing the name *Mehdi*. One is our eleventh Imam, who is *Mehdi*, and the other is our fiftieth Imam, who is also a *Mehdi*.

This fiftieth one is, in a sense, of the same order as *Rasulullah* — he does not fall within the circle of *Imamat*. Thus *Mehdi Akhir al-Zaman*, the last *Mehdi*, likewise stands outside the circle of *Imamat*. He is not an Imam, nor is he the son of an Imam — yet his station (*shaan*) is immense. He is also called *Mim-e-Akhirah*. *Mim-e-Ula* is *Rasulullah*, *'ala dhikrihis salaam*, and *Mim-e-Akhirah* is *Mawlana Mehdi*, the fiftieth Imam. The significance of this parallel is that just as *Rasulullah* broke the chain (*silk*) of *Imamat* — the twenty-ninth person breaks that chain — so too did this *Mawlana Mehdi* break the chain of *Imamat*, because after the forty-ninth Imam the *Qa'im* was due to come, yet he came in between. Today's Imam is *Abul Qa'im*, and after *Abul Qa'im* the *Qa'im* should have followed; but *Mawlana Mehdi* came in between, owing to his great precedents (*sawabiqat*) and his exalted station.

Yet the forefathers (*jadd*) of *Janab-e-Mehdi* were Imams, just as the forefathers of *Rasulullah* were — though his immediate father was not.

A question was then raised, drawing on the Qur'anic verse: *Wa qala Ta'ala — ta'ruj al-mala'ikatu wa al-ruh ilayhi fi yawmin kana miqdaruhu khamsina alfa sanah* — "And Allah the Exalted says: The angels and the Spirit ascend to Him in a day whose measure is fifty thousand years." *Fa ma'na al-yawm 'alayhi sallallahu alayhi wa al-dawr al-'adhim, dawr al-kashf* — the meaning of "the day" for him, peace be upon him, is the great cycle, the cycle of unveiling (*kashf*). From *Janab-e-Mehdi*, the cycle of *kashf* began; his cycle is called the cycle of *kashf*.

It is recalled that while studying with *Sayyidna Husayn bin Isma'il*, the concept arose that *Mehdi* and *Qa'im* are distinct. He said: "*Al-Mehdi huwal Qa'im, wal Qa'im huwal Mehdi*" — the *Mehdi* is the *Qa'im*, and the *Qa'im* is the *Mehdi* — and cautioned against treating them as separate figures.

*Fa al-ma'na 'alayhi sallallahu alayhi wa al-dawr al-'adhim, dawr al-kashf — wa lahu mutimmoona min waladihi — wa huwa ya'khudh zakaa* — the meaning for him, peace be upon him, is the great cycle, the cycle of unveiling; and for him are completers (*mutimmin*) from his progeny; and he will receive the *khilafah* of *zakaa*, meaning the sun. The word *zakaa* appears in a *munajat* recited in *Sadaqallah*, composed by *Khassa 'Amir bin Sa'b*, where it is written: *Antum al-zakaa wa al-nasu* — "You are the sun, and the people are..." — meaning *Janab-e-Ali* holds the position of the sun, while others hold the position of stars.

As for the term *mutimmin*: just as there were *mutimmin* at the close of the cycle of 'Isa — those who came at the end of a *dawr*, or at the beginning of a *dawr*, are called *mutimmin* — similarly, in *Mawlana Mehdi*'s progeny there is a *mutim*.

*Wa kam takunu khidmatuhu? Wa ya'khudh khilafat al-'ashir* — And what will his service be? He will receive the *khilafah* of the *'Ashir*. *Tafaddal 'alayna ya mawda' al-sirri wa al-sara'ir* — Bestow upon us, O repository of secrets and hidden matters. *Jazakallahu afdal al-jaza'* — May Allah reward you with the best of rewards. *Bi-haqqi Sayyidina Muhammadin wa aalihi al-tahirin* — By the right of our master Muhammad and his pure family.

*Al-jawab: Bi-'awnillahi subhanahu wa mannihi wa yusrihi wa ta'yidi mawlana al-Malik, mahbat ash'at al-mala'ik* — The answer: With the help of Allah, Glorified is He, and His grace and ease, and the support of our master, the King — namely *Sayyidna Hibatullah* — the place where the rays of the angels descend. *Anna mawlana al-Mehdi al-'adhim, alladhi huwa qa'im dawr al-sitr* — Know that our master, the great *Mehdi*, is in fact the *Qa'im* of the cycle of concealment (*dawr al-sitr*).

*Wa lahu sha'nun 'adhim* — And his station is very great. *Wa fadlun jasim* — And his virtue is immense. The author describes him not as a confluence (*jami'*) but as the *Qa'im* of the *dawr al-sitr*. *Wa huwa majma' thaman suwar — suwar 'idham samiyah* — And he is the confluence of eight surahs, surahs of great and lofty rank. This is on account of the verse: *Wa yahmilu 'arsha rabbika fawqahum yawma'idhin thamaniyah* — "And on that Day, eight will carry the Throne of your Lord above them" — meaning eight angels will bear the Divine Throne on the Day of Resurrection. *Wa sab'u suwarin fi dawr al-kashf al-madi li-hadha al-shakhs* — And seven surahs belong to the past cycle of unveiling for this person — that is, a *surah* that came every seven thousand years in the past *dawr al-kashf*. Regarding this, *Sayyidna 'Ali bin Hanzala* — may Allah sanctify his soul — is cited: *Wa hiya yas'adu fi asna'ihi min suwar — Sab'un min al-qism alladhi — 'azimah fi thalathat alaf fi dawr al-fatrah* — And it ascends in its midst from the surahs; seven from the portion which... great, in three thousand years in the period of interruption (*dawr al-fatrah*). These are three surahs, each representing one thousand years, yielding three in total.

*Wa hadha dawr al-sitr al-mutaqallibina fihi ahl al-sharra'* — And this is the cycle of concealment, in which evil people have prevailed. *Wa yawmuhu huwa dawr al-asna'* — And his day is the lofty cycle. *Wa al-imamah fihi mustamirrah* — And the *Imamat* within it is continuous, just as it was throughout the *dawr al-sitr*. *Min dhurriyyatihi, alladhina hum ma'ani al-asma' al-husna* — From his progeny, who are the meanings of the Most Beautiful Names.

*Wa dhakarta annahu ya'khudh zakaa, fa-laysa dhalika kadhalik. Bal alladhi ya'khudhuha ba'da intiha' al-rub'atati huwa al-rukn al-asham* — And you mentioned that he will receive the *khilafah* of the sun, but that is not the case. Rather, the one who will receive it after the end of the four (*rub'atat*) is the lofty pillar (*rukn al-asham*) — meaning the *rukn al-asad* — who will receive the *khilafah* of the sun.

*Fa-'rif dhalika wa ta'aqqal ma hunalika. Wa 'alayka bi-al-tasawwur al-mustaqim* — So understand this matter and comprehend what is there, and hold fast to straight understanding. *Li-takuna min sukkan jannat al-na'im* — So that you may be among the dwellers of the Garden of Bliss.

*Wa amma al-Qa'im, 'alayhi salamu, wa tahiyyatuhu wa ikramuhu, fa-innahu 'inda firaqi min hadhihi al-dar, yakhluf al-'ashir* — And as for the *Qa'im* — may peace, greetings, and honor be upon him — upon my departure from this abode, at the time of passing away, he will succeed the *'Ashir*.

A question arises: for how long will the *Qa'im* remain in the world? Only three days. The *qiyam* of the *Qa'im* will last just three days.

Are the *Qa'im* and *Mehdi Akhir al-Zaman* then two distinct personalities? They are indeed two distinct personalities. However, the *Qa'im* of the *dawr al-sitr* has been called *Mehdi*, and the station of *Mawlana Qa'im* is certainly greater than that of *Mehdi*. That *Qa'im* — peace be upon his mention — will receive the *khilafah* of the *'Ashir 'Aziz al-Jabbar*: the *'Ashir* here refers to the second *juz'i da'i* of this *kawr*. When he progresses beyond one *kawr*, he will become the *juz'i da'i* of the next *kawr* — that will be the *Qa'im*, and that too in a *juz'i* (partial) capacity, not in a *kulli* (total) capacity, for many others stand in the line ahead.

This is illuminated by the opening of Surah Ya-Sin: *Yasin. Wal-Qur'an al-hakim, innaka la-mina al-mursalin, 'ala siratin mustaqim* — "Ya-Sin. By the wise Qur'an, indeed you are from among the messengers, on a straight path." Allah grants such greatness as to say, "You are from among the messengers" — and then adds, "You are on the straight path." At first this seems incongruous, like introducing someone as a PhD professor with degrees from ten universities and then adding that he has passed his matriculation examination. Yet the meaning becomes clear: *'ala siratin mustaqim* refers to that line — the line of *juz's* — and among those standing in it, the Prophet, peace be upon him, is one. Before him in that line stand the *Thalathat Kubara'* (the Three Great Ones), and after them it is he.

There is a further verse in the Qur'an in which Allah says, *Inna rabbi 'ala siratin mustaqim* — "Indeed, my Lord is on a straight path" — which is also reported as a hadith of the Prophet: "My Lord is on the straight path," referring to Abu Talib, the one who raised him, affirming that he too is on that path. From all of this, *sirat mustaqim* signifies this very path of *juz's* — it is the straight path. Yet for the *A'immah al-Tahirin* (Pure Imams) and for *Rasulullah*, the *sirat mustaqim* carries a different and higher meaning.

When we recite *Ihdina al-sirat al-mustaqim* — "Guide us to the straight path" — this is a magnificent prayer. As *Haddrat al-Muqaddas* once explained, it does not merely mean "place us on the straight path" but rather "make us walk upon it" — cause us to move forward along it. The chain extending from a *mu'min* to the Imam passes through both the lower ranks (*hudud daniyah*) and the higher ranks (*hudud 'aliyah*). To walk that path is to move swiftly toward the *Bab al-Abwab*, which is the destination of every *mu'min*'s spiritual form (*surah*). When that station of *Bab al-Abwab* is reached and its magnificent assembly — *fasl al-khitab*, the decisive speech — is attained, and when the Imam receives that assembly and conveys it to the *Farzand-e-Imam* (son of the Imam), it becomes, as it were, a fixed deposit: wherever the *Farzand-e-Imam* goes, it accompanies them.

There are, therefore, two kinds of spiritual progress (*taraqqiyat*): one in which a person strives and advances through their own effort, and another in which one advances by sitting in the lap of another — resting in that nearness. Both are genuine forms of progress. The pace of advancement depends on each person's capacity, their *taqwa* (piety), and their *taharat* (purity). Furthermore, the *sadaqat* (charities) and good deeds performed after a *mu'min*'s passing — when children recite on his behalf and perform righteous acts — their benefits reach him as well, causing him to progress more swiftly from whatever station he has attained.


<!-- page numbers are preserved from INPUT; context-overlap is dropped -->

A human's *surah* — it is divided among the Imams. No, all the units gather in one place. Yes, they gather in one place. Because the one who — the previous Imam — yes. And he doesn't come into the world, right? A *surah sharif* that is progressing keeps on progressing. Whatever comes into the world — as a person passes away and after a thousand years returns — he comes back in the same way, but minus the vegetative soul (*namiyah*), minus the sensory soul (*hissiyah*), minus the rational soul (*natiqah*). And sometimes it even rises above his *moththalan ikhtira'i* (archetypal form). But its progress is very slow. It keeps progressing. And in one *kawr*, its manifestations (*zuhurat*) happen 360 times. Yes, 360 times. Of the *walad* — the integration of the *walad*. Yes. Therefore, *Sayyidna Ali bin Sulayman* said that in the human body there are 360 *nufus* (souls/parts). This means that one of his *nafs* progresses in one *kawr* — in one *dawr*. When his integration through grace happens, then one part out of 360 progresses.

*Bhaijaan*, so — yes? The *du'as* (prayers) of *Mawlana Ja'far al-Sadiq*, which are to be recited daily — yes — in them too there is such a phrase: *Innaka 'ala siratin mustaqim* (Indeed, you are on a straight path). In the 27th *tariqah*. Its meaning is addressed to Allah, right? That "O Allah, you are on the straight path." Yes, yes. That's the same answer. Yes, yes. "Allah" there does not mean *Mubdi' Ta'ala* (the Originator, the Exalted), but the *juz'i da'i* (partial missionary) of that *kawr*. The indication is towards him — that you too are on the *sirat mustaqim*, meaning you are in the line to take the *khilafah* of the *'Ashir*. Yes, yes. That's what it means.

Because everyone has to progress. Yes. *Allati ma yuhitu biha illa man safat suratuhu min al-rayb* (Which cannot be encompassed except by one whose form has been purified from doubt). These things can only be understood by — and one can only encompass them — one whose *surah* has been completely purified from doubts and suspicions. Yes. *Wa tawalla amra 'alam al-tabi'ah* (And he took charge of the affair of the world of nature). And they become masters of the affairs of the natural world. *Wa yadharu fi afwaniha surur al-badi'ah… Fa-hafiz hadha al-jawab al-mukhtasar wa sunhu fa-innahu min al-sirr al-akbar* (And in its horizons appear wondrous joys… So preserve this concise answer and guard it, for it is from the greatest of secrets). You should know this concise answer and guard it, for it is from the loftiest of secrets.

Let us stop here for today, as the need has been met.

*Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim*. *Qul huwallahu ahad…* *Fatihah*. A gift, a connection, a praise — pure and growing — to the soul of *Sayyidna Ali bin Hibatullah*, and for us from his blessings, his support, and his aid… *Sayyid…* May Allah accept it from us, and recite it in the best way possible… *salam… Fatihah*. *Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim*. *Al-hamdu lillahi rabbil 'alamin…*

And also for *Sayyidna Mawlana* — the departed, *Janab-e… Sayyidna… al-Hajj… Shaykh… Janab… Mawlana Ghulam Husayn… Haddrat al-Muqaddas* — and *Mawlana…* and for all — so that Allah may elevate their ranks, forgive their sins, gather us in their company and in the company of *Ahl al-Bayt*, and grant the fulfillment of our needs, and bless our children and their progress, and the *khilafah*, and the Imam of the time, and our health… and for *Sayyidna Mawlana…* and for all… *Fatihah*. *Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim*. *Al-hamdu lillahi rabbil 'alamin…*

<!-- lecture 15 -->

*…wa salli Allahu 'ala rasulihi sayyidina Muhammadin wa 'ala aali Muhammadin wa barik wa sallim.*

This — before showing the camera — this is *al-su'al al-thani* (the second question). Yes. *Al-su'al al-thani 'an qawlihi ta'ala: Yawnma natwi al-sama'a katayyi al-sijilli lil-kitabi. Wa hal dhalika lahu haddun ma'roof am huwa ma yakunu dhalika illa min ba'di an tafattatat al-jibalu wal-hijara…* (The second question concerns His, the Exalted's, saying: "The Day when We will roll up the heaven like a scroll rolled up for books." Is there a known limit for that, or will it not happen until after the mountains and stones have crumbled…) Yes. So the second question — that is, they had asked two questions, right? Who had asked them? Ibrahim had.

Ibrahim bin — yes, this book was, so to speak, finished, but then, for this reason, one person had asked two questions and they were appended at the end of the book.

His name was Ibrahim. Ibrahim bin 'Ali. Ibrahim bin 'Ali asked two questions, so he wrote those questions at the end of the book.

We read one question yesterday. This is the second and final question: concerning the saying — that on that day, *natwi al-sama'* (We will roll up the heaven). Allah Ta'ala says — it is an *ayat* of the Qur'an Majeed — "We will roll up the heaven as a scroll is rolled up for the book" — the pages of the book — in that way We will roll up the heaven. *Wa hal dhalika haddun ma'roof…* It is *lil-kutub* (for the books). There is a scribal error. It should be *lil-kitab*. I have checked — let us see — it is *kutub*. The *ayat* is *kutub*. Yes, *lil-kutub* — just as books are rolled up. *Wa hal dhalika lahu haddun ma'roof?* (Is there a known limit for that?)
